# Changelog

Generated by o-inventory from the snapshots in ~/hub/inventory: one entry per snapshot, newest first, only the concepts that changed. Every line but Commits is derived from the diff; `fixed:` lines quote `fix:` commit subjects.

## Unreleased

- Added: application-defined list row classes (`ListItem.Classes`, `NounBuilder.Classes`, `ClassDecl`) and `list.next-class` / `list.previous-class` motions. Declarations generate overridable `] KEY` / `[ KEY` binding rows with counts, help and hints. Motions visit shown rows, clamp without spilling, and report plain status when no match remains. Empty keys are palette-only; undeclared lists are unchanged. Stories `lists/class-motions`, `lists/class-help`.

- Added: optional tree branch lines with `Guides()` (`guides` in story specs and catalog tree lists), the dim `tree.guide` tone, and `tree.guides=app|off` (default `app`, trees opt in). Folding, zoom and text/prop filters retain correct branches. Catalog lists can opt into trees (`tree`, row `parent`), preserving ordinary list defaults. Stories `tree/guides`, `tree/guides-filtered`, `tree/guides-folded`, `tree/guides-off`.

- Fixed: prop-filter focus jumps reveal hidden rows; tree filtering preserves deep selection, marks and row instances, and `/` alone keeps its previous fold behavior. Prop-filter context can be folded with h/l/enter and clearing restores original folds. Saved-filter cycling skips invalid definitions with a message; malformed `list.filters` JSON is refused on write and reported on load. Zero matches remain visible with the strip off. Row props/search text, row nouns and parsed saved settings are cached. Added `list.filter-save`, `filter-saved-delete`, `filters` and `filter-saved-pick` to manage user definitions. The footer shows `z /`, with `z !` hidden.

- Added: framework prop filters on lists and trees (`PropFilter`: `=`, `!=`, `contains`, `in`, ANDed with `/` text). Trees keep matching descendants' ancestors, including collapsed paths, and restore collapse state on clear. `z /` picks a prop and observed value, `z !` removes one condition, `z C` clears prop conditions. `NounBuilder.Filters` and catalog `nouns[].filters` declare named defaults; the `list.filters` setting adds user definitions scoped by noun or list. `list.filter-saved` applies a name; `z ]` / `z [` cycle saved filters. `list.filter-strip` and `list.filter-cycle` default on and opt out of the respective UI. Standard noun lists accept `--filter NAME` beside unchanged `--where`, before `--limit`. Tests and stories cover lists, tree ancestors, cycling, clearing, CLI and catalog mounting. Matomeru's mine/agents/all adoption is a separate consumer lane.

- Fixed: the inspector toggle uses `alt+?`, an unused single key, so it no longer turns an editor's `g` into an app-level sequence and steals `g y` or the count of `g g`. The `ochazuke.state.toggle` verb is unchanged; the default stays off. Story `inputs/inspector-prefixes`.
- Added: live state inspector (`Runtime.Inspect`, `Inspection.Compact`/`Parts`, `ochazuke.state inspect` with CLI JSON), an `alt+?` toggle and `inspector.enabled` (default off). Includes modes, focus/row path, bounded register values and yank head, last message/tone, request/result (including job completion), selection, marks, count/prefix, modals, jobs and reported writes. Apps add fields with `AddInspectField` and report writes with `RecordWrittenFile`; config/binding exports report their paths. Story `lists/inspector-yank`.

- Added: events and hooks (lane event-hooks, plan `mtm-tasks-source-of-truth` lane C). `Runtime.Emit(event, fields)` and an event registry (`DeclareEvent`, `Events`, `EventSpec{Name, Description, Fields, Source}`); the framework emits `surface.opened` and `surface.left` when a modal, form or page opens or closes (`kind`, `id`, `noun`; on leave `changes`, `changed` (the nouns changed while it was open, counted where a change verb ran or `Runtime.Changed` was called; the framework's own `ochazuke.*` nouns and a hook's own changes are not counted) and `opened_at`) and `app.quitting` (what changed since the last `surface.left`; the Model waits up to `QuitHookWait` for its hooks' jobs). Rows `{on, where, do, args, from, off}` in `hooks.json` run verbs on events in order, an application verb as a job off the loop; a failing hook is a notice. The app owns its hooks config: each application reads its own `<config dir>/hooks.json`, merged after its own defaults (`AddDefaultHooks`) and its extensions' catalog defaults (`CatalogDoc.Hooks`/`Events`, `cli.MountCatalogHooks`); there is no framework or global hooks file, and extensions have none. Verbs `hooks list|check|export|reload|path` (`export` defaults to `<config dir>/reference/hooks.reference.json`) and `events list|emit`; `config reload` reads hooks.json. Setting `hooks.enabled` (default on) turns every hook off. README "Events and hooks" has the comparison with Vim's autocommands. Stories `events/*`.
- Changed: `bindings export` no longer requires a path (owner: "path should not be required...should default to .config/matomeru/references/"). With none it writes `<config dir>/reference/bindings.reference.json`, the file `config export` writes (same `Config.Paths().Reference` directory, kept singular `reference`), creates the directory and returns the path it wrote; with no config directory it asks for a path. `--path` works as before and never writes over the live `bindings.json` without `--force`; an explicit path's directory is created too.
- Fixed: `h` on a tree row with nothing to do (a closed or childless top-level node outside a zoom) no longer swallows the key: `ochazuke.outline.collapse` is unavailable there (guard `outline-collapses`), and the tree's list takes the table's `h` rows (R5.11), so on a tree with columns `h` scrolls the columns back and then moves the focus to the previous stop, as the flat table did (found by matomeru's Work task tree). Setting `tree.spill` (ochazuke › Lists): `left` (default), `both` (`l` on a leaf also falls through to the next stop) or `off` (a tree's list never takes the table's `h`/`l` rows). `l` on a leaf still stays in the tree by default (R11.1). Stories `tree/h-spills-left`, `tree/h-spill-off`, `tree/l-spill-both`; test `TestTreeCollapseUnavailableWithNothingToDo`. Consumers: matomeru (Work tree, Panes trees, outline modal), o-json (structure tree) pick it up on the next tag.
- Added: `ChooserRow.Tones []ToneSpan`: a chooser row's runs drawn in theme tones, as a list row's are (`Cell` indexes `Cells`, or 0 for the `Label`); the highlighted row keeps its reverse bar. For matomeru's task picker path (`parent › child`, the prefix dimmed). `Runtime.toneStyles` is shared by lists and choosers. Test `TestChooserRowTones`.
- Changed: the `?` help draws a binding's arguments one per line instead of as JSON: the name on the left in tone `help.arg.key` (bold in every built-in theme), the value on the right in tone `help.arg.value` (plain), the values aligned in one column; a nested object is flattened into dotted names (`request.verb list.top`), a list of plain values is one line (`keys g, g`), a list holding objects is flattened by index (`steps.0.key`), a string has no quotes (the empty string reads `""`). The here and pick views (arguments column) and the table view's detail (under each row) go through one formatter (`help_args.go`). A long value is cut on its line; rows with several arguments are as tall as their argument count. Added: `Column.Lines` (`"lines"` in a catalog's columns): a cell's newlines start lines of the row and each line is cut at the width; a `Wrap` cell's newlines are line breaks too. Changed: a tone span inside a cell wrapped onto several lines keeps its place on the lines it falls on (before, only whole-cell tones survived a wrap). A themes.json `tones` row restyles either argument tone. Story `help/args-one-per-line`; `help/arguments` and the help goldens updated; tests `help_args_test.go`.
- Added: highlight layers compose, and a table's non-data parts have their own tones (o-jj owner request: "anytime two selections overlap/intersect we need a diff bg color for the intersecting cells"; "row numbers and column headers also need a distinct style"). `ToneSpan.Layer` (`LayerTone`) names a span's highlight; where two or more layers cover a cell (the selected row is `LayerSelected`, a part is a layer) the cell draws in its part's intersect tone out of the reverse bar (`list.cell.intersect`, `list.header.intersect`, `list.rownum.intersect`; `IntersectSuffix`). The header row draws in `list.header` (a band), a `Column.Part` column (`PartRowNumber`) in `list.<part>` (`list.rownum`); `List.SetHeaderTones`/`HeaderTones` tone header cells. Defaults in every built-in theme. Settings `list.intersect` and `list.part-tones` (both default on; off draws as before). Stories `tables/intersect-row-column`, `tables/intersect-off` (the o-story list spec gains `headerTones`); tests `layers_test.go`, golden `testdata/layers.golden`. Needs monogatari's `cellText` change (lane `cell-intersect`) for a story's `cell` expect on a crossing cell.
- Added: normal-mode `ctrl+w h/l` move to the previous/next focus stop, and `ctrl+a o` cycles to the next; all use the existing wrapping focus verbs. Inside a terminal, leader `o` cycles, and leader `a` sends a literal leader (pressing the leader twice still does). The key set is recorded in UX rule R3.15 and can be overridden or unbound in `bindings.json`. Stories `panes/ctrl-w-h-prev-stop`, `ctrl-w-l-next-stop`, `ctrl-a-o-next-pane`, `terminal/leader-o-next-pane`, `leader-a-sends-prefix`.
- Added: one pane alone in a split (o-jj's `ctrl+w o`). `ochazuke.pane.only {pane, page}` draws the focused panel of the page's split over the whole body (`pane` the instance whose pane to keep, default the focused stop; the focus moves into it); `ochazuke.pane.restore {page}` draws both again, as does the focus leaving the kept pane. `Layout.Only` (set by `Runtime.PaneLayout`; `SplitFocusBlock` draws its focused side alone), `Runtime.ShowPaneOnly`, `RestorePanes`, `PaneOnly`, guard `pane-only`. Rows (`bindings/panes.json`, hidden): `ctrl+w o` restores when one pane is shown, else keeps the focused one (guard `pane-split`); `ctrl+w =` restores before it resets the divider. Opt out by unbinding the rows. UX-RULES R3.16 (and the R3.15 units table). Stories `panes/only`, `panes/only-restore-keys`; tests `pane_only_test.go`.
- Added: insert-like modes. A mode that extends insert (`ModeExtends("insert")`, `"extends": "insert"`, any depth) is insert for completion (`completion-available` and the trigger check), for an input's undo grouping (its typing is one undo change, as an insert session is), the read-only guard, the paste rule, the bar cursor, and the cursor step-back when left for a mode that extends normal (o-jj's JQ line gets path completion with its own badge). `Runtime.ModeIs(mode, base)` follows the extends chain. Story `completion/insert-like-mode`; tests `insert_like_test.go`. UX-RULES R2.6.
- Added: per-mode badge hues are data (o-json Design D, lane `ochazuke-mode-tones`). The badge hue of a mode is the tone row `mode.<name>` (built-in rows `mode`, `mode.normal`, `mode.insert`, `mode.visual`, `mode.visual-line`, `mode.visual-block`, `mode.pending`, `mode.command`, `mode.terminal`, `mode.list_filter`, `mode.macro_recording`, the colours of v0.59's Go table), and the rich and subtle levels derive `chrome.rich.mode.<name>` / `chrome.subtle.mode.<name>` from it unless a theme gives that row; `off` derives nothing and draws as before. An application declares its own with `Appearance.SetModeTone(mode, hue)` or `Appearance.SetTone(name, style)` (`Appearance.AppTones`, a `*ToneRows`; `ToneRows.Rows()`), laid over the built-in rows and under every `themes.json` entry (a user scheme's own tones win too). A `themes.json` theme's `mode.<name>` row recolours a built-in or an application mode at both levels. Changed: `DefaultTones()` holds the hue rows and no longer the 22 `chrome.{rich,subtle}.mode.*` rows (they are derived; a theme that sets one still wins). Stories `chrome/mode-tone-app`, `chrome/mode-tone-theme` (the o-story demo gains page `modeTones`); test `TestChromeModeTonesDeclared`.
- Added: an instance declares its base mode (o-json Design D, owner question 26). `Instance.Pin`, `Runtime.PinInstance(id, mode)` and the verb `ochazuke.mode.pin {instance, mode}` (`instance` default the focused stop; an empty mode drops the pin). Focus landing on the instance, or entering a stop beneath it with no nearer pin, enters the mode (`focusMoved` reads a pinned input's mode, the instance's `Pin`, its noun's `Pin`, then the nearest ancestor's; an open modal is a boundary). A re-pin of the focused pane applies in the same update while the runtime stands in the mode it declared before; a lifted mode (insert, an app's EDIT) is kept, and under a modal the mode it restores follows. `mode.set {mode: declared}` (`ModeDeclared`, `Runtime.DeclaredMode(id)`) sets the focus's declared mode, normal where none, so `esc` rows never name a pane's mode; `declared` is reserved (`DefineMode` refuses it). The editor's insert `esc` row (`bindings/editor.json`) now runs `mode.set {mode: declared}`: the same as `normal` for every input that pins nothing. UX-RULES R2.7, R3.2. Stories `modes/instance-pin-focus`, `modes/instance-pin-live`, `modes/esc-declared` (the o-story demo gains page `modes` and a list's and an input's `pin`); tests `mode_pin_test.go`.
- Added: `tab` and `shift+tab` cycle the `?` help's views (here, pick, table; wrapping), the family rule that `tab` cycles the views of anything with views and, in a search line, the match mode (UX-RULES R1.10). `ochazuke.bindings-help.view` takes `step` (integer, default 1; -1 the previous view); `ochazuke.bindings-help/normal` gains hidden rows `tab` (`step` 1) and `shift+tab` (`step` -1). `S` still steps forward. Before, `tab` in the help fell through to `focus.next`. Unbind the rows in `bindings.json` to get that back. Story `help/tab-cycles-views`.
- Docs: the README's path chooser line said `tab` cycles the sources; since v0.59 it is `ctrl+o` (`alt+o` back).
- Fixed: `?` inside the help opened over another modal (the docs viewer: `:help`, `?`, `?`) shows the help again in its place, instead of closing it with `Error: a modal is open above this level`. `OpenModal` with `ReplaceOpen` now opens the replacement from the focus the replaced modal opened over: the opener inside it (a help row) went with it, so the top-level check saw an opener beneath the viewer. Story `help/reopen-over-docs`; test `TestOpenModalReplaceOpenOverModal`.
- Added: `q` closes the help (`ochazuke.bindings-help/normal` `q` runs `ochazuke.modal.pop`, hidden), as it closes the docs viewer and the text view; before, `q` in the help reached `app/normal` and started a macro recording beneath it, so the help stayed open. `esc` still closes it; unbind the row to get the old `q`. Story `help/leave-with-q`.
- Added: old parameter names on an action alias (Keep actions, for a flag). `ActionAlias.ParamAliases` (`[]ParamAlias{Alias, Param, Since, RemoveAfter, Note}`; `"paramAliases"` on an actions.json alias row, copied by `cli.MountNouns`) name old spellings of the action's parameters the alias still takes: `pane.star` declares `{Alias: "starred", Param: "value"}` so `bin pane star w1 --starred=false` runs as `--value=false`. Read from CLI flags (typed as the parameter), palette `--OLD` words, request and binding-row args; renamed before the alias's presets (so a preset the parameter excludes gives way); the parameter's own name wins over its old one. A dated one posts a one-time notice (`ALIAS: OLD is an old name of NEW (renamed in ...; removed after ...)`, a CLI `notice:` line); `bindings check` notes a row using one; help never lists it and the palette never completes it. `Runtime.UseParamAliases(q)` renames a request's old names and returns the notices (the CLI shell calls it). Story `actions/param-alias-old-name`.
- Fixed: `?` or `:bindings help` while the help is open shows the help again in its place, for the focus and mode it opened over (`bindings.help` opens with `ReplaceOpen`, R4.8), instead of `Error: modal "bindings-help" is already open`; the palette no longer presents an answer over a modal the command opened again in its place (it compares the modal instance, not its id). Story `help/reopen-while-open` (o-json UX round 2, R2-F1).
- Fixed: `settings.edit` run again while the Settings page is shown is a no-op and answers nothing, so the palette opens no results modal over the page (R2-F4). Story `palette/settings-edit-no-results` now runs it twice.
- Fixed: the path chooser's typed source always has a row for a typed path: one that cannot be used (its folder missing, a folder in a file pick, a file in a folder pick) reads `PATH · WHY` and enter on it fails naming why (`/nonexistent-dir does not exist`), the chooser kept open, instead of `type a path` and `Error: nothing matches` (R7.8, R2-F7). New `ChooserRow.Refuse`: a row that cannot be picked fails with that reason and leaves the chooser open. Story `chooser/typed-missing-dir-says-why`.
- Fixed: `esc` in MACRO PENDING returns to the mode `q` was pressed in, so `V j q esc` is back in VISUAL-LINE with the selection (`app/macro_pending` `esc` runs `mode.leave`, R2.5; R2-F10). Story `modes/macro-pending-esc-returns-to-visual`. The same for REGISTER PENDING (`"`) and REPLAY PENDING (`@`): their `esc` runs `mode.leave` too, so `V j " esc` and `V j @ esc` keep VISUAL-LINE and the selection. Stories `modes/register-pending-esc-returns-to-visual`, `modes/replay-pending-esc-returns-to-visual`.
- Added: setting `yank.report` (ochazuke › Keys, default on): a yank in an editor or the read-only text view says on the status line what it copied (`copied 1 line`, `copied 2 lines`, `copied 5 characters`, `copied 3 lines (block)`), as a table's `y` does; off yanks silently (R2-F16). Stories `palette/text-result-yank-says`, `palette/text-result-yank-report-off`; golden `settings/layout-sections-100` redrawn (Keys 14 to 15 settings).
- Added (colour pass C1, for v0.59.0): setting `chrome.color` (appearance; `off`, `subtle`, `rich`, **default `rich`**). `rich` colours every border (an unfocused panel teal, a focused one in the mode's colour, bold, a toned pane by its state), fills the mode badge with the mode's colour and draws hint keys in the accent, bold; `subtle` tints foregrounds only and colours only the alert states; **`off` draws the chrome byte for byte as v0.57.0** (every o-story frame compared). Every colour is a tone row `chrome.<level>.<role>` in `DefaultTones()` that a `themes.json` theme's `tones` override; `off` has no rows. Roles: `border`, `border.focused`, `border.<tone>`, `border.modal`, `border.completion`, `border.split`, `panel.title`, `panel.title.focused`, `title.mark`, `title`, `mode.<name>` → `mode.pending` → `mode.<extends>` → `mode` (the badge), `hint.key`, `hint.text`, `hint.sep`. The tone role `mode` (the current mode's badge colour) is new; an application's accent stands for `accent`, `border` and `mode` in chrome rows.
- Added: `Layout.WithPanelTone(tone)` gives a panel's border a tone, as `Card.Tone` does a card's: the row `border.<tone>` (built in at rich: `state.working` blue, `state.needs-input` amber bold, `state.errored` the error colour bold, `state.done` green; at subtle needs-input and errored). Precedence: the state row, `Appearance.Accent`, `border.focused`, `border`, the theme's border; focus keeps its heavy lines. A card's `Tone` takes the `border.<tone>` row when the level has one.
- Added: `Styles.Chrome(role, fallback)`, `Styles.WithChrome(level, roles)` (applied by `Model.Styles`), `Styles.HasChrome`, `Styles.ChromeLevel`, `Runtime.ChromeLevel()`, `ChromeColorSetting` and the level constants; `HintCell{Key, Text}`, `HintCells(r, focus)`, `JoinHints(cells)` (`BindingHints` is unchanged text), `Footer.Hints`. The footer's and every modal's hint line colour their keys.
- Changed: the modal and completion borders read `border.modal`/`border.completion`, the path picker's split `border.split`; the markdown package's note box reads the tone `markdown.note` (built in, today's `#875f00`/`#d7af5f`) and its table rules `markdown.rule` (no built-in row), so both are reachable from `themes.json` (`markdown.NoteTone`, `markdown.RuleTone`).
- mono and `NO_COLOR` draw a chrome row's attributes alone: under `rich` the badge, a focused border and a needs-input border are bold; `off` is today's mono exactly. Opt out with `:set chrome.color=off`.
- Stories `chrome/colour-panel-states`, `colour-modal-border`, `colour-badge-modes`, `colour-hints`, `colour-accent-precedence`; the o-story demo gains page layout `panels` and a list's `panelTone`, and its `split` layout draws in the Model's styles. `settings/layout-sections`, `tab-step` and golden `layout-sections-100` count the new Appearance row. Tests `chrome_test.go`, `chrome_contrast_test.go` (WCAG 4.5:1 text, 3:1 borders, both sides, true colour and 256 colours, no ink on its ground's slot at 16; helpers copied from tuios `internal/overlay/contrast.go`).

- Added: match modes in every search. The `/` list filter (lists, tables, trees), choosers and the path picker match their query as `substring` (every word, any order), `fuzzy` (characters in order) or `regex` (Go RE2; an invalid pattern matches nothing and the search line says why, `regex: missing closing )`, never a modal); `search.case` applies to all three (a regex folds through `(?i)`; smart case skips escaped letters). New settings (ochazuke › Search): `search.match` (the filter's starting mode, default `substring`), `chooser.match` (choosers and the picker, default `fuzzy`), `search.show-match` (the mode's tag at the right of the search line, default on). New verbs `ochazuke.search.cycle-match {step}` and `ochazuke.search.set-match {mode}`, for the open search only (no setting written): `tab`/`shift+tab` in the filter line and in choosers, `ctrl+t` in the path picker. `Matcher`, `NewMatcher`, `Runtime.Matcher`, `MatchModes`, `NextMatch`, `MatchTag`, `List.MatchMode`, `Chooser.MatchMode`, `Picker.MatchMode`. One fuzzy scorer (`fuzzyScore`, the picker's) now serves choosers, the picker and the fuzzy filter. Stories `lists/filter-match-cycle`, `lists/filter-regex-invalid-says-why`, `lists/filter-match-setting`, `chooser/match-cycle-tab`; goldens with a filter or chooser search line gain the tag and the `tab search.cycle-match` hint.
- Changed (keys): in a chooser `tab`/`shift+tab` now cycle the match mode; `ochazuke.chooser.next-source` moved from `tab` to `ctrl+o` and `ochazuke.chooser.previous-source` from `shift+tab` to `alt+o` (`bindings/chooser.json`; the verbs are unchanged). Rebind them in `bindings.json` to taste.
- Added: noun aliases for any noun, so a host mounting an extension's nouns as `<ext>.<noun>` (`cli.Namespace`) gives each a bare name. `Runtime.AddNounAlias(NounAlias{Alias, Noun, Since, RemoveAfter, Note})` (a deprecated alias with `Since`/`RemoveAfter`: runs with one notice per runtime naming the noun, `NoteNounAlias`/`NounAliasNotice`, the CLI's `notice:` line; `bindings check` notes a row naming it; never completed, drawn, listed in CLI help or in the catalog's `aliases`), `Runtime.Unalias(alias)` (rename a short name live), `NounAliasesOf(noun)`, `DeprecatedNounAlias(name)`; `NounAlias` gains `Since`, `RemoveAfter`, `Note`. `Alias` is `AddNounAlias` without them. UX-RULES R7.11, R12.3.
- Changed: a noun is drawn (hints, `?` help, palette, jobs ticker, `DisplayNoun`/`AliasOf`) by its shortest live alias, any noun, not only the framework's short names. New setting `nouns.display` (ochazuke › Keys: `shortest` default, `framework` the behaviour before, `canonical`).
- Changed (palette): a noun's other names (its canonical spelling and every alias) are taken typed whole and never completed, and an exact name beats a prefix (`doc rows` runs `json.doc` aliased `doc`, not a prefix of `docs`; before, only the canonical spelling and framework short names were read). Namespace words: leading words joining with dots into a noun are that noun (`json doc rows`, as the CLI reads them), and after `json ` the namespace's nouns complete (`doc · json.doc`; a typed prefix offers `json · namespace`), setting `palette.namespace-words` (default on). Tails: a word no noun or alias claims reads as the namespaced nouns ending in it, one taken (`skills` for `ai.skills`, which before named nothing), several offered by their full names and none picked (`"doc" is ambiguous: json.doc, md.doc`), setting `palette.noun-tails` (default on). R7.11.
- Changed (palette): `:page open ` offers the pages' titles (`JSON  ext/json`) and takes a title, quoted when it holds a space; the id or a prefix of it still names the page. `Choice.Word` and `PaletteInstance.Word` (a `word` key in a source's `{id, label, word}` rows); `PagesPaletteSource` sets it to the title. Setting `palette.choice-words` (`word` default, `id` as before). Taking a completion now replaces the word being typed from its start, so a quoted word with a space is replaced whole. R7.12.
- Stories `palette/noun-alias-exact`, `noun-namespace-words`, `noun-tail-ambiguous`, `noun-alias-deprecated`, `page-open-titles` (demo pages take `nouns: [{noun, verbs, aliases}]`); `palette/set-shadow-pa-tab` expects `path.hidden` (the new palette settings push `path.start` below its window); golden `settings/layout-sections-100` (section counts). Tests `palette_aliases_test.go`, `cli` `TestCLINounAliasesMatchThePalette`.
- Fixed: a second text result while the text view is open (`:demo program`, then `:demo mapping`) shows the new text in the same view, from the top, over the focus the first opened over (`OpenTextView` passes `ReplaceOpen`; R4.8). Before, the palette dropped any answer while a modal was open beneath it, so nothing happened and nothing said why. The palette now presents an answer while the modal and the page it opened over are unchanged; the results view re-targets the same way. A verb that opens a page of its own (`settings.edit`) no longer gets its list answer in a results modal over the page. Stories `palette/text-result-reinvoke-retargets`, `palette/settings-edit-no-results`.
- Fixed: a read-only input offers no `c` or `d` (R9.8): the editor's normal `c`/`d` rows carry `"when": "input-writable"`, so they leave the hint line and never enter OPERATOR PENDING there; `y` still does. Story `palette/text-result-readonly-no-operators`.
- Changed: a focused read-only input owns the editor's keys. The editor table answers right after the input's own level (before the panel, the page and `app`), so its motions and selections win over the page's rows in every mode (visual-line's inherited normal rows included), and an editor key that does not run there (an edit) is consumed rather than reaching a page row on the same key; the hint line follows (the editor's rows are still listed after the page's). Keys the editor does not bind still reach the page. An app no longer needs `ochazuke.key.ignore` rows for `p`, `u`, `ctrl+r`, nor its own `w`/`e`/`v` rows, on a read-only input. Writable inputs are unchanged (page rows still override editor rows). Story `inputs/readonly-keys-stay-in-the-input` (story inputs take `readOnly` and `multiline`).
- Added: `Input.SetText(text)` refreshes an input from the program, read-only or not: the text becomes the undo root, the cursor and a visual anchor are clamped into it as normal mode leaves them, an open select range and insert session end; the same text again keeps the history.
- Fixed: a key that completes a bound sequence whose rows do not answer here (the action unavailable or the row's guard false) is consumed with the sequence instead of being replayed alone by `sequence-unknown` `pass`: `g c` (choose-column) on a plain list no longer runs `c`; an app's `g v` where values does not apply no longer runs `v`. `z o` on an open timeline card is consumed, not unhandled. Story `modes/sequence-unavailable-consumed`.
- Added: setting `chooser.stable-height` (ochazuke › Choosers, default on): an open chooser keeps the tallest height it has drawn, padding with blank rows as typing narrows the matches, so the search line never moves (R4.9); off sizes it to the matches as before. Stories `chooser/stable-height`, `chooser/stable-height-off`.
- Fixed: the chooser's and the palette's `printable`/`paste` insert rows are hidden, so their hint lines no longer end in `<printable> input.insert`.
- Fixed: a store with a required prop other than the label registers: standard `create`'s positional words are the label, the focus fills, the other required props, then the `pairs` rest (it failed "required parameter TYPE comes after the rest parameter pairs").
- Fixed: the CLI applies an action alias's presets after the caller's flags (`Operation.WithPresets`), so a flag that excludes a preset drops it: `bin task star t1 --value=false` writes false instead of failing "set takes a value or toggle, not both"; bare words still skip a parameter a preset gives.
- Fixed: `list.apply` over several rows asks the store's own prompt when the store builds one (`Store.ConfirmFor`, given every key), not "Run task.delete on 2 rows?".
- Fixed: `list.apply`'s confirm fills the prompt's fields (`{{label}}`, `{{id}}`, ...) from the rows' own request, not list.apply's arguments: a store's prompt over several rows read "Delete task true?" (v0.56.0, mtm's `V j d`), and a single row's did too. One row names its label from the store; several name the first and count the rest ("Delete task Water the plants and 1 more?"), as a direct many-subject request does. `openConfirmAbout` renders one request and stores another. Story `demo/task-delete-visual-confirm`; test `TestListApplyStorePromptAddsToTheRowsCount`.
- Fixed: the mode badge holds still at the right end of the title row. The focus display (`focus-display`) was drawn after the badge (`NORMAL · Inbox › outline › list`), so the badge slid left and right as the path grew and shrank; now the row reads title … focus … key echo … badge (`Inbox › outline › list  NORMAL`), the focus growing leftward into the room the title leaves, cut first at a narrow width, then the title, never the badge (`Layout.titleRow`). The ` · ` between the badge and the focus is gone. New setting `mode-badge.width` (`ModeBadgeWidthSetting`, Appearance; `widest` default, `fit`): `widest` pads every base mode's label inside the badge to the widest registered base mode's name (`Runtime.ModeBadgeWidth`, passed as the new `Header.BadgeWidth`), so a mode change moves neither the badge nor the focus beside it; `fit` is the badge in its own cells (only its right edge holds). A pending mode's keys or a recording may still widen the badge, leftward. UX-RULES R2.10. Stories `chrome/badge-corner-focus`, `badge-corner-echo`, `badge-corner-mode`, `badge-corner-narrow`; tests `TestBadge*`; every golden's title row redrawn (77 goldens, the header row only, plus the Appearance section's count in `settings/layout-sections-100`).
- Added: the key echo, a screencast mode for recordings and demos: setting `keys.echo` (`off`, the default; `footer`; `badge`) shows the last keys the dispatcher handled, grouped as typed (`5j`, `g g`, `j ×3`, `"hello"`, `:page open docs`), with `keys.echo-width` (reserved cells, so the line never reflows), `keys.echo-count`, `keys.echo-linger`, `keys.echo-typing` (`text` or `count`), `keys.echo-commands` and `keys.echo-terminal` (off: a pane's keys never show). Verb `key.echo {placement}`. New `Operation.Typing` (set on `input.insert`, `terminal.send`, `terminal.paste`), `Input.Secret` and `RecordField.Secret` (from a `Secret` prop: nothing typed into it is echoed; the field still draws its text). `Model.JobLingerTick` is the Model's one linger timer: the earlier of the jobs ticker's and the echo's deadlines.
- Added: smart case in every search. Setting `search.case` (ochazuke › Search, a new section): `smart` (the default) ignores case unless the query holds an upper-case letter (`unicode.IsUpper`, so `É` counts), then matches it exactly, as Vim's `ignorecase` with `smartcase`; `ignore` is the behaviour before (always ignore case); `sensitive` always matches it. It covers the `/` list filter (tables and trees too), choosers (matching, ranking, word spelling), the path chooser's browse and typed completion (only the name being completed decides) and the path picker's fuzzy search, whose ranking for a lower-case query is unchanged. API: `CaseFor(mode, query)`, `Runtime.CaseFor(query)`, `Runtime.SearchCase()`, `CaseMatch` (`Fold`, `String`, `Rune`, `Contains`, `HasPrefix`), `SearchCaseSetting` and its values; `examples/csv`'s column filter uses it. Changed by default: a query with a capital (`README`, `/IDLE`) now narrows to rows spelled that way; set `search.case` to `ignore` for the old matching. UX-RULES R5.17. Stories `lists/filter-smart-case`, `lists/filter-ignore-case`, `forms/path-smart-case-browse`, `forms/path-smart-case-typed`, `forms/path-ignore-case`.
- The Settings page has owner tabs and a section list (settings-layout lane B, `settings_page_layout.go`): a tab per owner (the application, the owners it declares such as an `extensions` tab, then `ochazuke`; no tab row with one owner), the shown tab's sections in a list on the left (`__settings/sections`, rows of the new `ochazuke.settings-section` noun: title and count), the shown section's settings over the detail on the right. Rows show the full setting name (the word `:set` takes); a setting's `Group` is a heading row above its group. `◦` marks a tab and a section holding a session value (`settings.mark-session` turns it off with the bold values). While the section list has the focus the detail is the section's line.
- New settings (ochazuke › Settings): `settings.layout` (`tabs+sections` default; `tabs` is the page as it was, a tab per section and no section list; `sections` drops the tabs and lists every owner's sections under owner headings) and `settings.narrow-width` (72: below it the section list folds into a selector line `Section ‹ Keys ◦  2/11 ›` atop the settings, `h`/`l` step it, `j` goes down into the settings, `s` or `enter` picks a section from a chooser; 0 never folds).
- Keys moved (`bindings/settings-editor.json`): `]`/`[` now run the new `settings.tab {step|name}` (the next or previous owner tab, reopening it on the section it last showed; hidden `g t`/`g T` too); `tab`/`shift+tab` run `settings.section {step, across: true}`, walking every section in drawing order across the tabs, wrapping (owner decision 15; `across` is a new parameter, without it `settings.section` steps within the shown tab). `settings.section` is unchanged as a verb and a user can bind it again. New rows: `l`/`enter` on a section row `settings-section.open`; `h` on a setting row that does not step goes back to the section list (`focus.previous`); the narrow selector's `h`/`l`, `j`, `s`/`enter` (guard `settings-narrow`).
- New verbs on `ochazuke.settings-section {section}` and their twins on `ochazuke.settings-tab {owner}`, sharing the handlers: `open`, `reset` (every setting of it, one undo step, `scope`), `write` and `revert` (its session values), `copy` (yank its values that differ from the default as `set NAME=VALUE` lines); `settings-section.pick` (the narrow selector's chooser).
- New list option `SpillToSelection()`: a spill into the list (R3.4) lands on its selected row rather than its edge, for a list that chooses what is drawn beside it; the section list uses it, so `k` on a section's first setting goes back to that section.
- Stories: `settings/tab-step`, `sections-spill`, `narrow-selector`, `layout-sections`, `layout-sections-100` (golden); `page-tabs` now runs under `settings.layout: tabs`; goldens `page-80`, `page-120`, `filter`, `session-marker-80` redrawn.
- Settings owners and sections (settings-layout lane A): `Setting.Owner` (JSON `owner`; "" is the declaring application, `OwnerApp` = `app`; the embedded rows and a framework feature's declarations inside `Runtime.Builtin` are `OwnerFramework` = `ochazuke`; an application that declares a framework row's name to change its default keeps the framework's owner, section and group unless it gives its own) and `Setting.Group` (JSON `group`, a heading inside a section). `SettingOwner{ID, Title, Description, Order}`, `SettingSection{Owner, ID, Title, Description}` (owner "" titles the section for every owner), `Runtime.DeclareSettingOwner`, `Runtime.DeclareSettingSection`; the framework's own are the embedded `settings/sections.json` (owners `app` order 0, `ochazuke` order 100; General plus the ochazuke sections Appearance, Keys, Terminal, Help, Palette, Records, Lists, Choosers, Status bar, Pages, Markdown, Settings). Every row of `settings/ochazuke.json` and `settings/terminal.json` names its section; `theme` is in Appearance, `history-limit` and `history-fetches` in Pages. `Segment.Owner` (JSON `owner`) gives a segment's setting its owner.
- Changed: a setting's section without `Section` is the name before its **first** dot (it was the last): `tree.zoom.root`, `tree.zoom` and `tree.breadcrumb` are one section `tree`; a name without a dot is in section `general` (`SettingsGeneralSection`, titled General by `sections.json`), no longer `""`. `settings.section {name}` takes a section's ID or title.
- `settings list --json`, `settings get` and `config export`'s `settings.reference.json` carry each setting's resolved `owner`, `section` and `group`. New verbs `settings.owners` (the owners in page order: id, title, description, section and setting counts) and `settings.sections {owner}` (an owner's sections, every owner's without one: owner, id, title, description, the settings in page order, grouped). `Settings.Owners()`, `Settings.Sections(owner)`, `Settings.OwnerOf(name)`.
- The `ochazuke.setting` things: `owner` is the owner the setting files under (`app`, `ochazuke`, or one the application declares; no longer an enum of who declared it, which `setting show --verbose` and the reference still say), `section` the resolved section (`general` for none), and a new `group` prop.
- o-story's engine moved to its own library, monogatari (`github.com/unixsuperhero/monogatari`, lane L1 of the o-story-for-apps plan): the story format, runner, driver, recordings, trimming and the explorer, with an app seam (`App`, `Fixture`, `Env`, `Built`) so any app can build its own story tester. The demo pages are now the demo app, `cmd/o-story/demo` (`demo.App()`), registered by o-story like any app; a story with no `app` field is a demo story, so every story file and golden is unchanged (460 stories: 444 pass, 16 skipped gaps, as before; the record fields `thing` and `reopen` are the demo app's too).
- `cmd/o-story` and `examples` are nested modules (`cmd/o-story/go.mod`, `examples/go.mod`) that require ochazuke and monogatari; the root module requires neither. Locally they build through the untracked `go.work` (`go work use . ./cmd/o-story ./examples ../monogatari ../oden`); `make test` and `make explore-quick` run each module.
- The demo application moved from `examples/demo` to `internal/demo` (root commands `cmd/ochazuke`, `cmd/docgen`, `cmd/renamekeys` and the root tests import it, so it stays in the root module); its explore survey stays at `examples/demo`.
- Deprecated: the `story` and `explore` packages are frozen for one release (o-json still imports `story`) and then deleted; use `github.com/unixsuperhero/monogatari` and `github.com/unixsuperhero/monogatari/explore`.
- o-story's commands, catalog, index TUI, player and explore watch mode moved to monogatari's host (`monogatari/host`, lane L2); `cmd/o-story/main.go` is `host.Main` registering the demo app, and `stories_test.go` runs the stories through `host.Test`. Every command and verb keeps its name and output; user stories and recordings stay under `o-story`.
- o-story's catalog gains the `fixture` and `application` nouns (list, show, and the stories and fixtures they relate to), `story copy` (to the user's stories under a new name, with its golden), `story fixture`, `story recordings`, `recording delete`, and filters `story list --app/--fixture`, `fixture list --app`, `recording list --story`; story rows carry `app` and `fixture`.
- `make test` is the gate: gofmt, `go vet` and `go test` of the root and both nested modules. A bare `go test ./...` at the root runs the root module only and now fails `TestNestedModulesRunToo` saying to run `make test` (which sets `OCHAZUKE_NESTED_TESTS=1`), so the stories cannot go unrun silently.
- `examples/csv`: `App.Program()` is the tea.Model `main` runs; its explore survey drives it through `explore.Subject.Program`, the shape an app with a wrapper model follows.
- Added: `Runtime.AddVerbAvailability(key, guard)` adds an application's guard to any verb beside the verb's own (a framework verb such as `ochazuke.list.choose-column` keeps its `table-cells` guard): the verb is available only while every guard holds, so keys, hints, the palette, the help and `Execute` all drop it. `RemoveVerbAvailability` and `VerbAvailability` (every guard, own first) go with it; `Noun.Availability` holds the added guards. An app no longer needs `when` on each binding row of a library verb.
- Fixed: a confirm's `{{noun}}` is the request's noun, never the focused or targeted row's or list's (`mtm note delete --id X` asked "Delete list X?"); a request naming its thing by the key argument also gets that key for `{{id}}`/`{{target}}` and the thing's label for `{{label}}`.
- Fixed: in the palette a first word that is both a noun and a verb reads as the noun when the next word is that noun's verb, prop or instance (`:note rename` on mtm's Notes page ran the focused Markdown doc's own `note` verb with `rename` as its text); a word the noun does not know keeps the verb reading, and after the noun the verb > prop > key order is unchanged.
- Fixed: `List.Items()`, `Selected()` and `Marked()` return rows with their own `Cells` and `Tones`, and `SetItems` keeps a copy of what it is given: editing a returned row's cells in place (or reusing the slice given to `SetItems`) and calling `SetItems` re-measures a table instead of keeping stale cached widths. `Data` is still the application's value, shared.
- Added: `Store.ConfirmFor(c, verb, keys, prompt)` builds a standard verb's confirm for one request (after the store's `Confirm` and the archive prompt): a store can say what a delete costs (mtm's task delete: the subtasks and notes it leaves without a task) and keep the standard verb; the answer is a template (`{{label}}` still expands), `""` asks nothing.
- Fixed: undo of a standard `set` restores every writable prop the write changed, not only those the request named: the change record diffs the whole thing before and after (mtm's move writes the project and its store clears the parent; undo now puts both back). Read-only props the store derives are not recorded, since undo could not write them.
- Added: setting `modal-highlights` (`keep`, the default, or `plain`): `plain` draws the page beneath a modal without reverse video or underline, so a list's cursor bar no longer peeks out beside a wide modal (o-json's `detail.backdrop-cursor`/`unreverse` workaround); `keep` is today's dimmed highlights. The frame's text and size are the same either way. Stories `modals/backdrop-plain`, `modals/backdrop-keep`.
- Fixed: an input's hint line (the footer, a modal's, the chooser's) no longer lists the editor's insert keys (`ctrl+k input.kill`, `ctrl+w`, `ctrl+y input.paste`, `backspace input.delete`, `space`/`<printable> input.insert`, `tab completion.open`, `esc mode.set`) or the palette key (`ctrl+space palette.open`): those rows are `hidden` in `bindings/editor.json` and `bindings/app.json`, as motions are; `?` help and `bindings list` still list them. Setting `help.hints-hidden` (default off) puts every hidden row back on the hint lines. Goldens with input hint lines regenerated.
- Fixed: standard `create` takes `prop=value` words as `set` and `duplicate` do (plan 2.6, `NOUN create [p=v ...]`): `mtm note create text="buy milk" project=hub` writes both props instead of storing `text=buy milk` as the label. The label stays the first bare word (`task create "Buy milk" project=hub`); a word in a prop's own slot whose left side names a prop create writes is that prop; the rest go to the new `pairs` rest parameter (`Pairs`, last in `Positional`); a prop given twice is refused. The CLI keeps a `NAME=VALUE` word's spaces in a `Pairs` rest parameter (quoting the value).
- Fixed: an action alias's preset yields to a caller's argument that excludes it: `Parameter.Excludes` (set's `toggle` excludes `value`) and `Operation.WithPresets(args, presets)`, the one merge the runtime, the palette and the CLI use. An alias `task.star` = `task.set {prop: starred, toggle: true}` given `value: false` writes false instead of refusing value plus toggle, so mtm's `star`/`pane.star` can be aliases; a caller giving both is still refused.
- Added: text results. A verb declared `.TextResult()` (`Operation.Result` `ResultText`, `"text"`, published as the catalog's `result`) opens what it answers from the palette in the text view (`Runtime.OpenTextView(title, text)`, modal `text-view`): a read-only input, one line or multiline by the text, a non-string value as indented JSON, where motions, `v`/`V`/`ctrl+v` and `y` work and `q`/`esc` close back on the origin; it keeps its size while it scrolls (R4.9). Setting `palette.text-results`: `editor` (default), `all` (any string answer too), `plain` (as before). The CLI and the API return the value unchanged. Demo verbs `demo.program` (one line) and `demo.mapping` (JSON); stories `palette/text-result-*`.
- Added: read-only inputs (`Input.ReadOnly`, the `ReadOnly()` option of `AddInput`; UX-RULES R9.8): edits are silent no-ops (no text change, no insert mode, a delete or change yanks nothing), and the editing verbs carry the new guards `input-writable` and `input-operator-writable`, so their rows stand aside and leave the hints (`?` help's pick and table views mark them `?`, "when input-writable").
- Added: the system clipboard. The clipboard register `"+` (`"*` is the same register) copies its yank to the system clipboard, and setting `clipboard` (`yanks` default: every yank but not a delete; `unnamed`: yanks and deletes, Vim's `clipboard=unnamed`; `none`) says which other yanks do. The `registers` component schedules the copy: `Registers.Copy(text) tea.Cmd` when the app sets it, else OSC 52 (`tea.SetClipboard`).
- Fixed: `esc` in a modal input's `V` selection closed the modal: `visual-line` extends `normal`, so the `modal` noun's `esc` → `modal.pop` answered before the editor's. The `modal` noun now has its own empty `visual-line` table, so the editor leaves the selection first.

## Unreleased

### Tables: fit columns to their content (`z f`, `z F`, `table.fit-follows-cursor`)

- New verbs on the list noun: `list.fit-column {width}` (`z f`, verb guard `table-cells`): the current column of a cell-mode table as wide as its widest cell over every row (filtered ones included) or its title, at most the table's width; `N z f` sets N cells (as Vim's `N ctrl-w |`). `list.fit-columns` (`z F`, every table, verb guard `table-fit-changes`): every column at its content's width when they fit; else the widest narrowed a cell at a time (ties the rightmost, never below the column's least width) so as few columns as can be are cut, and the rest scroll. A count is ignored, as in `ctrl-w =`.
- Both are sticky: they write fixed widths into the table's remembered widths (`tables.json`), as `z >`/`z <` do, so cursor moves, scrolling and filtering never reflow the table. `z =` (`list.reset-columns`) undoes them.
- Changed: `z =` now answers on every table with a width given or pins chosen (verb guard `table-resets`, was `list-table` with the key guarded `table-cells`), so it undoes `z F` on a row-select table; `z F` answers only where it changes a width, so `z` still waits for nothing on a row-select table that fits (R5.9). A row-select table now remembers its widths (`z F`, `List.SetColumnWidth`) with its pins; `TableState.Version` (1) marks them, and widths without it (left by v0.12.0) stay ignored on a row-select table.
- New setting `table.fit-follows-cursor` (boolean, default off): a cell-mode table lays its current column out at its content's width as the cursor reaches it (Vim's `'winwidth'`), the column going back when the cursor leaves; nothing is written and a given width wins. Off by default because it shifts the columns as the cursor moves.
- Go: `FitFollowsCursorSetting`, `TableState.Version`. Stories `tables/fit-column`, `tables/fit-columns`, `tables/fit-follows-cursor`; tests `TestFit*`, `TestNarrowToFit`, `TestRowSelectTableIgnoresUnversionedWidths`. README "Compared with Vim" note beside the table keys.

### Fixed: the Record page's editor (record editor fixes from the demo tape)

- Fixed: `e` could not edit again after a write from outside the editor (`:task status done`, `x x`): "record editor ID "task-form" must be nonempty and unused". `RecordEditor.Close` (the delivery contract's) left the closed editor's instance registered and the focus in its fields; the demo called it to close the editor. `AddRecordEditor` and `OpenRecordEditor` now free an id held by a closed editor, and the new `RecordEditor.Leave` closes an editor as `form.leave` does (its instance removed, the focus where `FocusOnClose` says, `OnLeave` run). The demo leaves through it.
- Fixed: a palette write threw you out of the editor. New `RecordSpec.Watch` (a noun): the open editor watches that noun's store and loads the record again when it changes from outside (a set, another client, `Runtime.Changed`); `RecordEditor.Reload` does the same on the application's call. A field holding no unsaved typing takes the new value, shown and written, and its undo steps are dropped; a field with unsaved typing keeps the typing, written over the new value at the next pause as the later change; a field the editor is still writing (queued, in flight, or inside `Save`) keeps its value. Focus, field and mode are kept. New setting `forms.outside-write` (`FormsOutsideWriteSetting`): `reload` (default) or `close` (the editor leaves, writing only what was typed, as the demo did before). The demo's editor watches `task`.
- Fixed: a request of the change kind run inside another (a palette line's `task status done`, under `palette.run`) now refreshes the watches once the top-level request has run; only a top-level change request did before, so a watch missed palette writes.
- Fixed: the footer kept `q form.leave · u form.undo · ctrl+r form.redo` after the editor closed (the focus stayed in the closed editor's fields); a closed editor is removed now, so the hints are the page's.
- Fixed: a socket file left by a killed application made the next start fail with `bind: address already in use`. `Listen` now connects to an existing socket first: refused (nothing listens) removes it and listens again; a live one is never removed ("another application is listening on this socket; quit it or choose another socket path"), nor is a file that is not a socket. The 0700 refusal names the directory and what to do (`chmod 700 DIR`, or a socket path in a private directory you own).
- Fixed: a choice field's chooser opened as a wide centered modal over the lines under the field. New `Payload.Anchor` (an instance id): a modal opened for something on the page is drawn beside it, sized to its rows (at least 24 cells), clear of the text of every row it spans; with no room beside, below it, then above it, else centered. The form chooser anchors on its field. New setting `modal.anchor` (`ModalAnchorSetting`, boolean, default **true**; off centers it as before). Compared with Vim: Vim's popup menu opens under the cursor and above it when there is no room (`:help ins-completion-menu`); a form chooser goes beside the field first because a form's other fields, and an application's lines, sit below it.
- Demo: the Overview's Library entry has its description line.
- o-story: a page record's `thing` edits one of the page's store things (loaded from it, written into it, the store's noun watched), and `reopen` binds a key that edits it again once closed. Stories `forms/outside-write-reloads`, `forms/outside-write-close`, `forms/chooser-beside-field`, `forms/chooser-centered-off`. Tests `TestRecordReloadKeepsTypingAndFocus`, `TestRecordReloadKeepsTheWriteInFlight`, `TestRecordClosedEditorIDIsFree`, `TestAnchoredPlace`, `TestListenReplacesOnlyADeadSocket`, `TestListenKeepsAFileThatIsNotASocket`, `TestListenRefusalSaysWhatToDo`; demo `TestDemoEditorReloadsAfterOutsideWrite`, `TestDemoEditorReopensAfterOutsideWrite`, `TestDemoOverviewDescribesEveryPage`.
- Consumers: the Settings page has two more rows. An app that closed an editor with `Close` should call `Leave`; one whose store an editor writes can set `Watch`. Every modal draws as before unless it sets `Payload.Anchor`.

### Standard verbs grammar: `NOUN PROP [VALUE]`, the optional key word, value completion, `:set` reads one-value nouns, `palette.standard-verbs` (standard verbs phase 1, lane S3)

- One grammar in the palette and the CLI (plan 2.6, `standard_grammar.go`). After a noun a word is a verb, else a prop, else a key or an instance (owner decision 18; `Runtime.ReadNounWord`, `NounWord`): an exact verb, an exact prop, a unique verb prefix, a unique prop prefix. `NOUN KEY` alone still runs a palette noun's default verb.
- New: `NOUN PROP [VALUE]`. `:task status` reads the focused task's status (the results view, one `prop  value` row), `:task status done` writes it, `:task starred --toggle` flips a boolean; `bin task status done --id t3` from the CLI (a command for that run only, so `--help` lists the verbs alone). It is a prop-shaped request (`PropShorthand`), so history keeps it as typed. A value for a read-only prop is refused by name.
- The key word after a verb is optional: a first word that is the next parameter's (a prop name, or `prop=value` after `set`) leaves the subject to the focus, a target or `--KEY` (`Operation.SubjectOmitted`, read from the parameters' `Enum` and `Pairs`, in the palette and the CLI's positional words). So `task set status done` acts on the focus and S2a's `task set t1 status done` still names t1; a key equal to a prop name is `--id status`. `get` takes its prop as the word after the key (`task get t3 status`, `task get status`).
- The palette reads `--NAME VALUE` (and `--NAME=VALUE`; a boolean `--NAME` alone is true) for any parameter of the line's verb, as the CLI does: `:task list --where status=todo --limit 5`. A `--NAME` still waiting for its value is the line's missing word. A word that names no parameter is read as before.
- Completion: the props after a noun (beside the verbs, `props` group), the props before the things after `set`, a prop's values after `set PROP` and `NOUN PROP`, and for `where` `prop=` (spliced, so the cursor stays after `=`) then that prop's values. New `Parameter.ValueOf` (the parameter holds a value of the prop another argument names: its choices and the palette's check are the prop's; set's `value`) and `Parameter.Pairs` (NAME=VALUE words of the noun's props; `where`, set's `prop`), both in the catalog.
- `:set NOUN` reads only one-value nouns (owner decision 17, R1): a noun whose `set` is the application's own (not `Standard`) or whose store is a singleton. A collection's or an instances store's standard `set` is reached as `NOUN set ...`, so `:set pa` offers the settings and the `palette` noun, never a collection named `parcel`; `bin set parcel ...` is a settings line. Read from each noun's kind and the `Standard` mark (`ReadBareAlias`, the palette's list after `set`). A registry test checks that every declared setting reads as a setting under `:set NAME`.
- New framework setting `palette.standard-verbs` (`StandardVerbsSetting`, a list, default `[last, tag, record]`; `Runtime.StandardVerbsView`): the one opt-out for every standard-verb change to the palette (owner decision 25). Ordering `last` (a noun's named verbs before its standard ones), `mixed` (one alphabetical list) or `hidden` (standard verbs left out of the verb list; typing the name, the CLI and bindings still reach them); `tag` marks them `· std`; `record` shows a standard `get` as `prop  value` rows in the results view. A part left out is today's palette, so `[]` turns all three off. The `?` help lists bound keys only and is unchanged.
- Changed for consumers: `:NOUN get` on a store noun now opens the results view (turn `record` off for the old behaviour); the verb list after a store noun lists named verbs first with `std` tags (`mixed` without `tag` for the old list). Nothing changes for a noun without a store.
- o-story: a page's `store` may name its `noun` (default the demo noun). Stories `palette/set-shadow-pa-tab.json`, `palette/key-prop-grammar.json`, `palette/prop-value-completion.json`, `palette/get-record-rows.json`, `palette/std-tag-off.json`; `demo/task-get-focus.json` expects the record rows. UX-RULES R7.9, R7.10.
- Not in this lane: completing a second `where` pair after a comma, value completion of set's several-prop words after the first, and `?` help ordering (it lists keys, not verbs).
- Tests: `TestNounWordPrecedence`, `TestPropShorthandInThePalette`, `TestOptionalKeyWord`, `TestPropValueCompletion`, `TestSetReadsOnlyOneValueNouns`, `TestStandardVerbsSetting`; demo `TestTaskGrammarFromTheCLIAndThePalette`.

### Fixed: a delete that will only archive says so (`delete.archive-confirm`)

- Fixed: with `delete.archive` on, the first `delete` of a live thing asked "Delete task X?" though it only archives it. It now asks `DefaultArchiveConfirm`, `Archive {{noun}} {{label}}? (delete it again to delete it for good)`, from the same template mechanism as `DefaultDeleteConfirm`; the real delete of an archived thing still asks "Delete task X?". A selection holding any thing that will be deleted asks the delete prompt. A store that names its own delete prompt (`Store.Confirm["delete"]`) keeps it for the real delete; `""` asks nothing either time. The archive notice reads "delete it again to delete it for good".
- New setting `delete.archive-confirm` (`DeleteArchiveConfirmSetting`, boolean, default **true**): off, a delete that will only archive runs without asking (`restore` undoes it); the real delete always asks.
- New: `Operation.ConfirmFor` (not in JSON), the prompt template one request asks in place of `Confirm` (`""` asks nothing), read by `ExecuteAt` and by `list.apply` on its rows; a standard verb row sets it with `ConfirmFor`. The standard `delete` uses it (`deleteConfirm`), driven by the archive role and the two settings, no noun name.
- o-story: a page with a `store` gets the standard `demo.delete` over its things in place of the row remover. Stories `palette/delete-archive-confirm`, `palette/delete-archive-confirm-off`; test `TestDeleteArchiveConfirm`.
- Consumers: the Settings page has one more row; apps with `delete.archive` on see the new wording.

### `keytest` is exported: `github.com/unixsuperhero/ochazuke/keytest`

- `internal/keytest` moved to the public package `keytest` (((same API: `Model`, `Msg`, `Press`, `Type`, `Paste`))), so other modules can drive models with binding key names. monogatari, the story library in its own repo, imports it. Importers inside ochazuke now import `github.com/unixsuperhero/ochazuke/keytest`; no behaviour changes.

### The mode badge's held keys: `mode-badge.key-case`

- New framework setting `mode-badge.key-case` (`ModeBadgeKeyCaseSetting`, a row of `settings/ochazuke.json` after `focus-display`; `upper` default, `as-typed`): the case of the keys a pending mode holds in the mode badge. `upper` keeps today's badge (`NORMAL G` for both `g` and `G`, `LEADER PENDING CTRL+A`); `as-typed` shows them as typed (`NORMAL g`, `NORMAL G`, `LEADER PENDING ctrl+a`). The mode's name is upper case either way. `Runtime.ModeBadgeKeyCase` reads it every frame. UX-RULES R2.10 names it.
- Consumers (mtm, nabe, o-md, the o-* tools) see a new row on their Settings page and nothing else until a user sets it.
- Story `modes/badge-keys-as-typed.json`; test `TestModeBadgeKeyCase`.

### Standard writes: `create`, `set` (alias `patch`), `delete` and prop-shaped requests (standard verbs phase 1, lane S2a)

- New: `Store.Create`, `Store.Patch`, `Store.Delete`, `Store.Confirm` (a standard verb's prompt by verb; `""` asks nothing) and `Store.When` (a standard verb's availability by verb, set as `SetAvailability` does). A noun with a store gets `NOUN.create` (one argument per prop it may write, `Default` filling an omitted one, the label prop its first word: `task create "Buy milk"`), `NOUN.set` (when the store has `Patch` and a prop is writable) and `NOUN.delete`, rows of the standard verb table like `get` and `list`. `set` and `delete` take many subjects (`Many`): over a visual selection they write each row once, one undo group (`Ctx.Each`). All three are history changes.
- `set` is one verb (owner decision 1): `set KEY PROP VALUE`, `set KEY PROP --toggle` (a boolean), `set KEY p=v p=v ...` (quoted values unquoted) or `{changes: {p: v}}`; the forms do not mix. `NOUN.patch` is its alias (source `standard`), registered with it; an application verb named `patch` takes the name silently. A value is typed by its prop (`3`, `true`, `400ms`, a JSON array or object); read-only, create-only and key props are refused. New parameter type `any` (`AnyParameterType`): CLI and palette words arrive as text, API values as sent.
- `delete` asks first: `DefaultDeleteConfirm` (`Delete {{noun}} {{label}}?`) unless the store names its prompt. `{{label}}` in a standard verb's prompt is the thing's label prop, else its `Fallback`, read from the store for a key argument (`task delete t3` from the CLI) as for the focused row; a visual selection's prompt names the first row's label prop.
- New: prop-shaped requests (from the props draft): `Request.Prop`, `Value`, `Toggle` (`IsProp`, `Writes`, `Empty`). `{noun, prop}` reads, `{noun, prop, value}` writes, `{noun, prop, toggle: true}` flips a boolean, for the thing the request acts for (target, focus, or `args` holding only the key). They run as the noun's standard `get` or `set`, so availability, confirms and subjects are the verbs'; history keeps the request as it was sent (a write a change, a read a fetch). The API's JSON request (the socket's one line) accepts them.
- New: prop rows in bindings. `SetProp(noun, prop, value)`, `ToggleProp(noun, prop)`, and in the grouped bindings file `{"key": "x", "request": {"noun": "task", "prop": "status", "value": "done"}}` in place of `action` (the rows format carries the request as it is). A prop row is validated against the prop (known, writable for a write, a boolean for a toggle, a value of its type); its `from` may fill only the value; it acts on the row it matched; its hint and help read `NOUN.PROP` and "Set PROP to VALUE". `bindings check` passes on good prop rows and reports bad ones.
- New: `PropSpec.When` (published as `availability`): a prop's own guard, apart from the verb guards (`Runtime.PropGuard`). `Available` reads it for standard verbs: a request naming a prop hidden where it acts is unavailable, `set` naming none is unavailable when no writable prop is offered there, and `get` leaves hidden props out.
- History (owner decision 12): one simple-value test (`simpleWrite`: every written prop an enum, a boolean, a number or a duration, never text, multiline, a reference, a list, an object or a secret) decides both `.` and the history file. A simple `set` or prop write repeats on the new focus with its value and persists whole; any other value write stays in the ring, never repeats and writes no line. So `.` after a text edit repeats the simple write before it. `create` never repeats. A repeat that drops its target also drops the recorded subjects.
- New: `PropField(p)`, a prop as a `RecordField`.
- Demo: the task store creates, sets and deletes (`task create "Buy milk"`, `task set t2 status done`, `task set t2 title=Ship estimate=5`, `task patch t2 --changes '{...}'`, `task delete t3`, which asks); `x` on the Record page is a prop row setting the status to done.
- o-story: a page store writes too (`set KEY p=v`, `create KEY p=v`, `delete KEY` in the writes); story `demo/task-set-visual`.
- Not in this lane: before/after records, `Store.Undo` and `history.undo` (S2b); the optional key word, `NOUN PROP [VALUE]` and completion of prop values (S3); `rename`, `move`, `duplicate` (S4). Without S3 a word after `set` fills the key first, so the CLI and the palette name the key (`task set t1 status done`).
- Tests: `TestStandardWriteVerbs`, `TestSetOneAndSeveralProps`, `TestCreateAndDelete`, `TestPropRequests`, `TestPropRowsInBindings`, `TestPropGuardsAndStoreWhen`, `TestConfirmUsesTheFallbackLabel`, `TestVisualSetIsOneUndoGroup`, `TestSimpleValueTest`, `TestDotRepeatsSimpleWrites`, `TestBindingsCheckOnPropRows`, `TestPropRequestDecodes`, `TestPropField`; demo `TestTaskWritesFromTheCLI`, `TestTaskWritesFromThePalette`.
- Consumers: nothing changes for a noun without a store, or a store without `Create`, `Patch` or `Delete`. A store that adds `Delete` gets a confirm by default.

### Undo of the standard writes: `history.undo` (standard verbs phase 1, lane S2b)

- New: `HistoryEntry.Changes` (`ThingChange`: noun, key, `before`, `after`). Every standard change entry (`create`, `set`, `delete`, prop rows) on a store that declares `Undo` records, per thing, the changed props' values before and after, read with the store's `Get` around the write; on any other store it records a mark (noun and key, no values, never written to the file), so undo stops there; a create has no `before`, a delete no `after`. A visual selection's write is one entry, its changes one group. In memory always; the history file keeps them only when every value is simple (owner decision 12's test), so a simple create's or delete's line can stay while its text values are left out.
- New: `Store.Undo` (history.undo may revert this store's changes) and `Store.AcceptsKey` (its `Create` keeps a key the values give).
- New: `ochazuke.history.undo` (owner decision 21), **no default key** (owner question 26 is open): the palette, the CLI and a `bindings.json` row reach it. It reverts the newest change not yet undone: `set` writes `before` back, `create` is undone by `delete`, `delete` by `create` under the old key (needs `AcceptsKey`). Each run goes one change further back; the inverse is not itself undone. Unavailable (guard `history-undoable`) when the newest change is on a store without `Undo`, or a delete on a store without `AcceptsKey`; it never skips to an older change. Nothing left: the info `nothing to undo`, and unavailable in an app where no store declares `Undo`. Changes restored from the history file are never undone.
- Demo: the task store declares `Undo` and `AcceptsKey`.
- Tests: `TestUndoRecordsBeforeAndAfter`, `TestUndoChangesPersistOnlyWhenSimple`, `TestUndoInverses`, `TestUndoVisualWriteIsOneGroup`, `TestUndoUnavailableWithoutStoreUndo`, `TestUndoSkipsRestoredEntries`, `TestUndoUnavailableWithoutAnyUndoStore`; demo `TestTaskUndo`. No story: no key in phase 1.
- Consumers: a store without `Undo` is not read any more than before; its entries carry a mark in memory only. A store with `Undo` is read with `Get` before and after each standard write. `history.undo` is in every app's catalog, and offered in the palette only where some store declares `Undo`.

### Derived standard verbs: `rename`, `move`, `duplicate`, `archive`, `restore`; settings `create.from-focus` and `delete.archive` (standard verbs phase 1, lane S4)

- New rows of the standard verb table, read from a prop's role or the store's handlers, each running the standard `set` or `create` (history, watch, availability and the store are theirs): `rename` (a writable `RoleLabel` prop and `Patch`; argument `name`, or `to` when the key prop is named `name`, the reserved-key rule), `move` (a writable `RoleOwner` reference and `Patch`; argument `to`, a thing of the reference's noun; `Many`), `duplicate` (`Get` and `Create`: every prop create writes, read from the thing, with `prop=value` overrides as `pairs` or `changes`), `archive` and `restore` (new role `RoleArchive`, a boolean prop: set it true or false; `Many`). `archive` and `restore` join the reserved standard verb names (no prop takes them), and `to` the standard parameter names (no key takes it). A verb the application registers under one of these names still wins.
- Repeat and history read the one simple-value test: `archive`/`restore` repeat with `.` and are persisted; `rename` (text) and `move` (a reference) never are.
- New: `PropSpec.FromFocus` on a reference prop: the standard `create` fills it, when omitted, from the focused thing of its noun (the instance on the focus path the request was made at; its key prop in `Data`, else its id) and says so: the palette slot reads `focus: Work` (it comes right after the label on `create`'s line), the args form holds it, and a notice names it (`project Work (from the focus)`). A given value wins; nothing focused leaves it empty. New setting `create.from-focus` (`CreateFromFocusSetting`, boolean, default **true**): off fills nothing, the reference stays empty, and create is never refused.
- New setting `delete.archive` (`DeleteArchiveSetting`, boolean, default **false**): on, the standard `delete` on a noun with a writable archive prop archives a live thing (answer `{archived: KEY}`, a notice says to delete again) and deletes an archived one for real; the confirm is asked both times (see the `delete.archive-confirm` fix above for its wording). Nouns without the role are unchanged; off, delete is the store's delete.
- New: `Parameter.Focus` is also declared by a create parameter of a `FromFocus` prop, and the palette slot of any `Focus` parameter names the thing it would be filled with. New `Parameter.Edits` (catalog `edits`): the prop of the subject the parameter writes; the args form opens holding its current value, selected as a rename is (`rename.select`), or, when it is empty, empty with the prop's `Fallback` value as a muted placeholder (new `RecordField.Placeholder`). `rename`'s argument declares it.
- New: an alias named like a prop of its noun clashes (either order), except the prop's own set (`task.status` as `task.set {prop: status}`, 2.5). `bindings check` and `actions check` report, as `info:` lines, a named verb that shadows a standard one (`StandardShadows`).
- Demo: the task has `project` (owner, `FromFocus`, a reference to the new read-only `project` noun: Home, Work) and `archived` (archive role), so it gets the five verbs with no code of its own. o-story: a list item may name its noun (`"noun": "project"`). Stories `palette/create-from-focus`, `palette/create-from-focus-off`, `palette/rename-chooser`, `palette/move-chooser`.
- Not in this lane: the other side of `move` (`project.*` verbs for `task.move`, phase 2), a default key for `move` or `rename`, `associate` as `move`'s alias (an mtm verb). Tests: `TestDerivedVerbsFollowTheRoles`, `TestDerivedVerbsWrite`, `TestRenameArgumentAvoidsTheKey`, `TestCreateFillsFromTheFocus`, `TestDeleteArchive`, `TestAliasPropClashException`, `TestChecksReportShadowedStandardVerbs`, demo `TestTaskDerivedVerbsFromTheCLI`, `TestTaskCreateFillsTheFocusedProject`, `TestTaskRenameFormStartsOnTheOldName`, `TestTaskDeleteArchives`.
- Consumers: a noun with a store gains the rows its roles and handlers allow (a label prop gives `rename`; `Get` with `Create` gives `duplicate`); an app verb of the same name wins, and `bindings check` says so. The Settings page has two more rows.

### Framework nouns on the standard verbs: `setting` and `page` (standard verbs phase 1, lane S7)

- `ochazuke.setting` is a collection: its things are the declared settings, keyed by `name` (a setting's name holds dots, so settings are things, not props), with the read-only props `type`, `default`, `value` (in effect, file form), `enum`, `description`, `section` (the Settings page's), `owner` (`ochazuke` or `app`) and `origin` (`default`, `file` or `session`). `setting get` and `setting list` (`--where owner=app`, `--props value`) and `setting watch` are standard; a watch sees every setting change, the Go API's too. On the Settings page the focused row is the subject of `setting get`. `setting set`, `show`, `toggle`, `cycle`, `reset` and the arithmetic verbs stay the named `:set` verbs (the named verb wins, owner decision 20), and `:set` reads as before. `settings list`/`get` (the `ochazuke.settings` noun, `SettingValue` records) are unchanged.
- `ochazuke.page` is an instances noun: its things are the registered pages in order, keyed by `id`, with `title` (label), `hidden` (writable: it writes `pages.hidden`) and `active`. `page get`, `page list`, `page set ID hidden true|false` (or `--toggle`) and `page watch` are standard. `page.hide` and `page.show` are now aliases of `page.set` with `{prop: hidden, value: true|false}` preset: the names, the id word, the messages, the refusals (the last page in the tabs; nothing to do) and the availability (hide while the named page is in the tabs, show while it is hidden; guard `page-set-changes`) are as before, but they answer the page record instead of the context, and the palette lists them on `set`'s row (`also hide, patch, show`). The guards `page-shown` and `page-hidden` stay defined for rows that name them.
- The `instances` store kind is real (it read like a collection in S1): `Store.Live` names the live instances that are the things (default every instance of the noun, in registration order); `Get` answers only a live thing's key (`unknown NOUN "id"` otherwise), the instance's own props (id as the key, its label as the label prop, its `Data` record's fields) under the store's `Get`, which may be omitted; `List` is built in; the subject from the focus is the nearest live thing on the focus path. `set` fits instances stores too. A row whose `Data` is a plain string is read as its thing's key (the Settings rows).
- `:set` is unchanged: lane S3's one-value-noun rule (`shadowsNoun`, owner decision 17) keeps `set page` a setting word now that `page` has a standard `set`.
- Not in this lane: the settings-layout `settings-section` noun (that plan has not landed); `setting patch` (the standard `patch` alias follows a standard `set`, and the setting store writes nothing); a `rename` for pages (titles are the application's).
- Tests: `TestSettingThingsAndProps`, `TestNamedSettingSetStillWins`, `TestSettingGetFromTheFocusedRow`, `TestSettingWatchFollowsChanges`, `TestPageThings`, `TestPageHideShowAreAliasesOfSet`, `TestInstancesStoreReadsLiveInstances`. Stories `settings/setting-list`, `pages/page-hide`.
- Consumers: every app gets `setting get|list|watch` and `page get|list|set|watch` with no change; a binding or `actions.json` row naming `page.hide`/`page.show` keeps working. A script reading `page hide`'s output gets the page record instead of the context.

### Mounted nouns get their props and standard verbs; `--help` lists standard verbs on one line; `standard-verbs.md` (standard verbs phase 1, lane S6)

- New: `cli.MountNouns(r, doc.AppNouns(), cli.HostMount{Local, Proxy, Param})` (`cli/host_mount.go`), the one mount a host (mtm, nabe) runs for an extension's catalog nouns: each noun under its host name with its columns and props (a reference prop and a choice source renamed to the host's noun, `cli.MountParameter`; a prop's `availability` guard is the extension's to run, so the host drops it), each verb as the host's proxy with the catalog's description, confirm, parameters, positional order, quiet, many, repeatable, rows, history kind and `standard` mark (`Operation.Standard`), then every verb's aliases (`patch`, preset aliases such as `done` = `set status done`, deprecated names) under the host names; an extension's bare alias becomes `NOUN.ALIAS`, never a global name in the host (the A1 follow-up "hosts re-register mounted aliases"). So a mounted noun gets `set`, `rename` and the other generated verbs with no host code per noun (working default 16), prop-shaped requests (`NOUN PROP VALUE`) run its forwarded `set`, and the host palette shows them as it shows its own standard verbs (`palette.standard-verbs`, lane S3).
- Mount hardening: a noun whose host name is taken, and a prop, verb or alias the host's runtime refuses (a clash, a bad name, an alias whose noun is not mounted), is skipped and reported in `MountedNouns.Warnings`; an unknown history kind or a positional naming no parameter mounts the verb without it, with a warning. Mounting never panics.
- `--help` (owner decision 24, R10): `BIN NOUN --help` lists the noun's own verbs and preset aliases as before and names its standard verbs on one line after the commands (`Standard verbs (BIN NOUN VERB --help for one): get, list, watch, create, set (patch), …`); the noun's summary in `BIN --help` names its own verbs first, then `· std` and the standard ones in table order. Every standard verb still runs and has its whole `BIN NOUN VERB --help`. Measured on the demo's `task`: 22 lines before standard verbs, 143 with each listed, 24 on one line. New framework setting `cli.standard-verbs` (`CLIStandardVerbsSetting`: `line` default, `list` gives each its own entry again).
- Generated reference: `refdoc.Model` gains `StandardVerbs` (the table's rows: kinds, what each needs, aliases, subject, many, repeat, history, confirm) and `Nouns` (each noun with props: its store's kind, props and verbs with their `standard`); new page `docs/reference/standard-verbs.md` and docs topic `standard-verbs` (`docs open standard-verbs`). The committed page lists the framework's store nouns (`ochazuke.page`, `ochazuke.setting`, lane S7) after the table; a verb's aliases follow it in one parenthesis.
- `config export` writes `props.reference.json` (`PropsReferenceFile`): the same standard verbs and nouns with props as JSON, one per line; the export README names it.
- `catalog inspect`: a host's mounted noun (props, no store kind) keeps its props table.
- Consumers: nothing changes until a host calls `MountNouns` (mtm and nabe still mount by hand; their switch is a follow-up). Every `BIN NOUN --help` of a noun with a store is shorter; `cli.standard-verbs list` restores the old listing.
- Tests: `TestMountNounsPropsAndStandardVerbs`, `TestMountNounsReregistersAliases`, `TestMountNounsSkipsBadEntriesWithAWarning`, `TestHelpListsStandardVerbsOnOneLine`, `TestMergeKeepsAMountedNounsProps`, `TestStandardNeedsCoverEveryRow`, `TestReferenceModelHasPropsKindAndStandard`, `TestConfigExportWritesThePropsReference`, `TestRenderStandardVerbs`; demo `TestMountedTaskHasItsStandardVerbsInTheHost`, `TestMountedStandardVerbsFollowTheHostsPaletteSetting` (its `hidden` half runs once `palette.standard-verbs` is declared).

### Rule R2.10: the current mode is always visible; Fixed: the leader's badge shows the leader key

- UX-RULES R2.10 (owner decision 2026-10-29): the mode badge is on the title row in every state (normal, insert, visual, a pending or leader mode, under a modal, while the palette is open, in an input), changes in the frame the mode does, shows the key sequence a pending mode holds and a recording's register, and names the mode for people. No setting hides it.
- Fixed: while `leader_pending` waits the badge read `LEADER PENDING`; it now reads `LEADER PENDING CTRL+A` (the leader setting's key).
