The badge adds a pending mode's held sequence (`Payload.Prefix`) whenever the mode's name does not already end with it, so a prefix mode still reads `NORMAL G` and any named pending mode entered with a prefix shows it.
- o-story: new expectation `badge` (the title row's badge, whole). Stories `modes/badge-every-state.json`, `modes/badge-leader-prefix.json`, `modes/badge-recording.json`.

### Fixed: the docs viewer opened again shows the new topic; its width holds while it scrolls

- `:help TOPIC`, `:h TOPIC` or `docs.open TOPIC` while the docs viewer is open now shows that topic in the same viewer, from the top, over the focus it opened over. Before, it failed with `modal "docs-viewer" is already open` and left the palette open. New `ModalOption` `ReplaceOpen()`: an `OpenModal` of a kind that is already the top modal pops it and opens the new one in its place; without it the second open is still refused (a confirm, prompt or chooser keeps its question), and a kind open beneath another modal is refused as before. Only the docs viewer passes it.
- The docs viewer's panel keeps one width while it scrolls. The panel is sized from the lines it draws, so a window of short lines narrowed it; the viewer now pads its top line to the page's widest wrapped line. New `Detail.Width()`: the widest line of the whole text the last `Window` measured.
- UX-RULES: R4.8 (re-opening an open modal changes its contents), R4.9 (a modal's size holds while it scrolls), and a failed standing experience check is at least `major` in Part C. Stories `docs/reopen-while-open.json`, `docs/width-steady-scroll.json`.

### Props, stores and the standard `get` and `list` (standard verbs phase 1, lane S1)

- New: `PropSpec` (`props.go`), a field of a noun's things: `Name` plus an embedded `Parameter` (type: the setting types or `object`; enum, noun for a reference, description), `ReadOnly`, `CreateOnly`, `Key`, `Role` (`label`, `owner`), `Fallback`, `Default`, `Secret`, `Multiline`. `NounBuilder.Props(...)`, `Runtime.Props(noun)`, `Runtime.PropOf(noun, name)`.
- New: `Store{Kind, Get, List, ListParams}` and `NounBuilder.Store(...)`; kinds `Collection`, `Singleton`, `Instances`; `Thing` (`map[string]any`) and `ThingOf(v)` (a map, a `Record`, or a struct through its json tags); `ListQuery{Where, Limit, Args}`.
- New: the standard verb table (`standard_verbs.go`, rows like `settingVerbs`): a noun with a store gets `NOUN.get` (every prop, `prop` for one value, `props` for some) and `NOUN.list` (rows with `id` and `label`, `where` as `prop=value` pairs, `props`, `limit`, the store's `ListParams`) when the store has the handler and the kind fits. Both are application verbs and history fetches; `list` declares rows of the noun, so it is the noun's row source and its things are a `Noun` parameter's choices. `StandardVerbs()` lists the table.
- New: `Operation.Standard` (the row name; the catalog's `standard`). The application wins: a verb it registers under a standard verb's name replaces the generated one, before or after the store (`Register` and `NounBuilder.Verb` replace an operation marked `Standard`), and the aliases naming the key stay. A duplicate hand-written operation still panics.
- New: `Parameter.Focus`, the declared flag on a standard verb's subject parameter. The subject parameter is named after the noun's key prop (never a generic `id` unless the key is `id`); a key argument wins, else the instance the request acts for after `ExecuteAt` applied the target and the focus path: the key prop in its `Data`, else its id. No new resolver.
- Clash rules: after a noun a word is a verb or a prop. A prop may not take a standard verb's name (`get list create set patch delete watch rename move duplicate`), an existing verb's, or a nested noun's; a verb registered later (in Go or `actions.json`) may not take a prop's. A `Key` prop is read-only and its name is reserved: no key named `prop`, `props`, `where` or `limit`, and no store list parameter named like the key. A prop named `id` must be the key and one named `label` must hold the label role (list rows carry both).
- Label fallback (owner decision 23): list rows' `label`, `{{label}}` in a confirm prompt and in an `actions.json` template, and the choosers that read a noun's list show the label prop, else the prop its `Fallback` names, read from a row's `Data`. Display only: `get` reads the prop itself.
- Catalog: `CatalogNoun.kind` and `props` (`PropSpec` as it is, a reference's `source` filled as a parameter's), `CatalogVerb.standard`. A generated verb is not builtin. `catalog inspect` draws a props table per noun, the store kind, and a `std` tag on generated verbs.
- Demo: the Record page's task is one of a three-task store (one with no title, shown by its summary); `task get` and `task list` work from the CLI and the palette with no handler of the demo's own, and `task get` on the Record page reads the task it edits.
- o-story: a page's `store` (props and things) gives the demo noun a store; stories `demo/task-list-label` and `demo/task-get-focus`.
- Not in this lane: `set`, `create`, `delete`, `patch`, undo, `watch`, prop-shaped requests, per-prop `When`, the `NOUN PROP` grammar and get in the results view (lanes S2 to S5). The `instances` kind reads its store like a collection for now.
- Tests: `TestStandardVerbsNeeds`, `TestPropClashRules`, `TestKeyNameReserved`, `TestSubjectOrder`, `TestAppVerbWins`, `TestListRows`, `TestLabelFallbackInConfirm`, demo `TestTaskStoreFromTheCLI`, `TestTaskStoreFromThePalette`, `TestTaskCatalogRoundTrip`.
- Consumers: nothing changes for a noun without props; an app adopts by declaring `Props` and a `Store` on a noun.

### The standard `watch` (standard verbs phase 1, lane S5)

- New: `NOUN.watch`, a standard verb of every store kind (owner decision 13: it reverses the parent plan's "no change tracking" for `watch` only). Its subject is optional and never from the focus: the key prop for one thing, or `where` (`prop=value` pairs) for the things that match, else every thing; `since` resumes from an earlier event's hash. A history fetch. It exists when the store has `Watch`, or `List`, or (a singleton) `Get`.
- New: `WatchEvent{Type, ID, Thing, Hash}`, one JSON line `{"type":"added|modified|deleted","id":…,"thing":{…},"hash":…}`. `thing` is the record `get` answers (no secrets); a deleted thing carries its last record when the watch saw it. `Hash` is the list hash after the change, the `since` to resume from.
- New: `Store.Watch func(Ctx, WatchQuery, emit) (stop, error)` (`WatchQuery{Key, Where, Since}`): a store that can push answers `watch` itself, from any goroutine; the runtime brings each batch to the loop through a command that waits for it (re-armed after each), so subscribers are called on the loop.
- Fallback (no `Watch`): the watch keeps each thing's record hash (`RecordHash`, the state delta protocol's) and lists again only when told: after a top-level request of the change kind, after a change verb's job completes, and on `Runtime.Changed(nouns...)` (an application whose store changed another way, such as the demo's record editor). No polling loop. Versions (sorted ids and record hashes under the list hash) are kept for `since`: in memory by default, `Runtime.SetWatchVersions(WatchVersions)` to replace; an unknown `since` is an argument error.
- New: `Runtime.Watch(noun, args, fn)`, the in-process subscription (`fn` on the loop, so a list can patch its rows from it; nil queues for `Take`); `Watch.Stop`, `Take`, `Hash`, `Stopped`, `Err`, `Noun`. Encoding a `*Watch` (the palette, a socket call) answers the events so far as an array and ends it.
- CLI: `BIN NOUN watch [KEY] [--where …] [--since H]` streams the events as JSON Lines until interrupted (Ctrl-C or SIGTERM end it cleanly, status 0) or the watch fails. While it streams the shell runs the scheduled commands and delivers their messages, as a TUI loop would. Its versions live in the shell's state cache (`StateCache`), so a later command's `--since` resumes. `CallMsg.Stream` asks `HandleCall` for the live watch.
- New: `ochazuke.RecordHash` and `ochazuke.ListHash`; `cli.RecordHash` and `cli.ListHash` call them (same hashes).
- `since` joins the reserved standard parameter names (no key prop or store list parameter takes it).
- Demo: `demo task watch` streams the three tasks, then each change; the Record page's editor tells the runtime after each write (`Runtime.Changed("task")`), so a watch sees editor writes.
- Tests: `TestWatchFallbackEmitsTheThreeEventTypes`, `TestWatchSince`, `TestWatchKeyAndWhere`, `TestWatchFollowsChangeRequestsAndJobs` (no polling: a watch lists once and schedules nothing until told), `TestWatchStoreHandler`, `TestWatchVerb`, `TestWatchRefreshFailureEndsIt`, `TestMemoryVersionsKeepTheNewest`, cli `TestShellStreamsAWatch`, demo `TestTaskWatchFromTheCLI`, `TestTaskWatchSeesTheEditorsWrites`.
- Consumers: nothing changes for a noun without a store. A noun with a `List` store gains `watch`; an app whose store changes outside its change verbs calls `Runtime.Changed`, or gives the store a `Watch`.

### `settings.review`: review session values one by one (lane F of the `:set` plan)

- New verb `settings.review`: a modal listing each setting whose session value differs from the saved one (setting, saved value and where, session value, origin), with the selected setting's description, default and what writing or reverting changes. Rows are the new `ochazuke.setting-change` noun; keys (data rows in `bindings/settings-editor.json`): `w` `setting.write`, `r` `setting.revert`, `s`/`space` skip (`list.move`, hint `skip`); on the modal (`ochazuke.settings-review`) `W` `settings.write`, `R` `settings.revert`, `q`/`esc` close. A written or reverted row drops out; the review closes when none is left. With nothing to review it posts an Info notice and opens nothing; from a CLI process (no session) it says so and points at `settings diff`.
- Changed: `settings.revert` asks first when it would drop session values that differ from the saved ones ("Revert 2 session values (scrolloff, theme) to the saved ones?"), from every path, as `settings.reset-all` does; with none it runs at once.
- No new alias: the verb is `settings review` already, and a bare `review` would shadow matomeru's `review` noun.


### Generated reference: `refdoc`, `docs/reference/`, the `docs` noun (generated-docs plan, lane 1)

- New package `refdoc` (stdlib only): `Stamp` (linker `-X refdoc.Commit/Version/Date/Dirty` first, then the build info: `vcs.revision`, `vcs.time`, `vcs.modified`, the main and ochazuke versions; `BuildStamp(tool)`, `FromGit(dir, tool, exclude...)` for files, `ShortCommit()` with `+dirty` or `unknown build`), `Model{Settings, Bindings}` with `Fingerprint()` (`sha256:` and 12 hex digits; the user's `Yours` fields left out), `Render(model, Options{Stamp, ShowConfig, Regenerate, Check})` returning `README.md`, `settings.md` and `bindings.md`, each opening with the header block (generated, do not edit; tool, commit, version, ochazuke version, commit date, catalog fingerprint), `Body`, `HeaderFingerprint`, `Check(model, opts, dir) []Diff` (missing, extra, changed; bodies compared below the header, the header only on its fingerprint), `Topics`/`TopicFile`.
- New: `Runtime.DefaultBindingsDoc()`, `BindingsDoc` without the user's `bindings.json` (the applied rows replayed backwards on a copy, as `DefaultBindings`); equal to `BindingsDoc()` with no user file. `Runtime.ReferenceModel()` fills a `refdoc.Model` from the declared settings (sorted by name, the section the Settings page files each under, who declares it) and `DefaultBindingsDoc`, with the user's values and rows as `yours:` data. `Settings.FrameworkSetting(name)`: a setting declared inside `Runtime.Builtin` (a framework package's `Register`) or for a framework feature (theme, history, `pages.hidden`, an embedded file's rows) counts as the framework's.
- New: `docs/reference/{README,settings,bindings}.md`, committed, the framework runtime's reference (`NewRuntime`, `NewModel`, `NewPicker`, `markdown.Register`; no demo nouns). `go run ./cmd/docgen -ref docs/reference` or `make reference` writes them, stamped from git (the output directory left out of the dirty check). `cmd/docgen` `TestReference` renders in memory and fails with the diff and `regenerate: make reference` when a default, a setting or a key row changed without regenerating (about 15 ms). A Go test only, never a pre-commit hook (owner decision 13).
- New: the `ochazuke.docs` noun (`docs`) with `docs.open {topic}` (positional; `index`, `settings`, `bindings`): a modal viewer (`ochazuke.docs-viewer`, a `Detail` scrolled by `j`/`k`, `J`/`K`, arrows, `ctrl+d`/`ctrl+u`, `g g`/`G`; `q` and `esc` close; `bindings/docs.json`) over the page rendered from the live runtime, headed `TOOL COMMIT[+dirty] · VERSION · catalog FINGERPRINT` (`Runtime.DocsPage`, `DocsTool`). No default key: the palette reaches it. Bare aliases `help` (shadows the verb `help` by what follows: `help bindings` is still `bindings help`, `help settings` is `docs open settings`) and `h` (owner decision 3), alias rows checked by the alias rules. Both are the palette's: new field `ActionAlias.Palette` (`palette` on an `actions.json` alias row) keeps a bare alias out of the CLI, so `BIN help` stays the shell's help (the viewer needs the TUI anyway).
- New setting `docs.show-config` (section docs, default on): the viewer marks the user's `settings.json` values and `bindings.json` rows with `yours:` lines; off, a page's text is the committed file's body byte for byte (`TestDocsViewerMatchesTheCommittedReference`).
- Compared with Vim: `:help {subject}` and `:h` are `help`/`h`; `:help` alone opens the index, as Vim's `help.txt`; the help buffer is read-only, as here. Vim's help opens a split, here a modal (no user splits); topics are three pages for now, not Vim's tags (later lanes add nouns, verbs and `topics.txt`).
- Story `docs/open-settings`. Tests `TestDefaultBindingsDocWithoutAUserFileIsBindingsDoc`, `TestDefaultBindingsDocUndoesTheUserFile`, `TestReferenceModelMarksTheUserConfig`, `TestDocsOpenShowsATopic`, `./refdoc`, `TestReference`.
- Consumers: every app gets the `docs` noun, `help`/`h` and the setting by bumping ochazuke; an app or user config with its own bare alias `help` or `h` clashes and the later registration is refused. Committing an app's own reference and test is lane 6.

### Vim keys: the `ctrl+w` pane prefix, `ctrl+e`/`ctrl+y` line scrolls, `g y`, `q:`

Owner decisions 9 to 11 of the Vim-parity audit (o-plans `plans/vim-parity-audit.md`). Every moved key keeps its verb; a user restores an old key with a `bindings.json` row.

- moved: `>` → `ctrl+w >` (`ochazuke.pane.grow`), `<` → `ctrl+w <` (`ochazuke.pane.shrink`), `=` → `ctrl+w =` (`ochazuke.pane.reset`), app normal, still guarded by `pane-split`, counts as before (`3 ctrl+w >`); the hint reads `ctrl+w >/< resize`. The bare keys are gone everywhere: they resized panes inside an editor and the outline, where Vim users expect shift operators. Restore one with `{"noun": "app", "mode": "normal", "keys": ">", "request": {"noun": "ochazuke.pane", "verb": "grow"}, "when": "pane-split"}`.
- added: `ctrl+w w` → `ochazuke.focus.next`, `ctrl+w W` → `ochazuke.focus.previous` (app normal; no count, Vim's count names a window).
- added: `> >` → `ochazuke.outline.indent`, `< <` → `ochazuke.outline.outdent` (outline normal), beside `tab`/`shift+tab`.
- moved: `ctrl+y` → `g y` (`ochazuke.yank-history.open`, app normal, guarded `no-input` so an editor's counted `5gg` is not held as a sequence); in an editor `g y` answers through its `g_pending` table (new row `y` → `ochazuke.yank-history.open`). Insert-mode `ctrl+y` (paste before, literal) is unchanged; Vim's copies the character above.
- added: `ctrl+e` / `ctrl+y` → `ochazuke.view.scroll {lines: 1 / -1}` from the count (app normal, `bindings/scroll.json`): Vim's line scroll on every scrollable. New verb `ochazuke.view.scroll {lines, count}` (a motion, guarded `view-scrolls`) and interface `LineScroller` (`CanScrollLines`, `ScrollLines`), implemented by `List` (tables and trees), `Timeline` and `markdown.Doc`: the innermost scroller on the focus path moves its window, the cursor staying unless it would leave the scrolloff margin; else the page's overflowing detail scrolls (as `J`/`K`). The timeline's own `ctrl+e`/`ctrl+y` rows (`timeline.scroll`) stay and answer first.
- added: `q :` → `ochazuke.palette.open {recall: 1}`: an exact `:` row in `macro_pending` (`bindings/palette.json`) beats its `any` register row, so `q:` opens the command line on the last command (Vim's command-line window, without the window) and `q a` still records. New optional parameter `recall` on `palette.open`. `q/` and `q?` stay unbound (no search history yet).
- fixed (docs): README said `sequence-timeout` defaults to 1000 ms; it is `0` (no deadline). The default is unchanged.
- Tests `TestPaneKeysAreTheCtrlWNamespace`, `TestViewScrollMovesAListWindowKeepingTheCursor`, `TestViewScrollFallsBackToThePageDetail`, `TestYankHistoryOpensOnGY`, `TestOutlineShiftsWithVimOperators`, `TestEditorCountedGGBesideGY`, `TestQColonOpensThePaletteOnItsHistory`, markdown `TestCtrlEScrollsTheDocumentWindow`. Stories `scroll/ctrl-e-ctrl-y-list`, `tables/ctrl-e-scroll`, `markdown/ctrl-e-scroll`, `detail/ctrl-e-scrolls-detail`, `tree/outline-shift-operators`, `panes/bare-keys-stand-aside`, `palette/q-colon-history`, `lists/g-y-yank-history`; the `panes/*` stories and the yank-history gap stories press the new keys; the help golden counts five more rows.
- Consumers: an app or user config that pressed `>`/`<`/`=` for panes or `ctrl+y` for the yank history uses the new keys or restores the old ones in `bindings.json`. An app that binds `q` itself (the o-story sandbox's quit) shadows `q:` as it shadows `q` recording.

### Settings: a session layer above settings.json (the `:set` plan, lane B)

- New: a session layer (`settings_session.go`). A value set for this session lives in memory above `settings.json` and is not written, as Vim's `:set`; the effective value is session, else file, else the declared default, and observers fire on the effective value as before. `Settings.SetSession`, `ResetSession`, `Revert`, `RevertAll`, `Write`, `WriteAll`, `ResetAll`, `Diff`, `Session`, `Origin`, `StartSession`, `HasSession`, `Autosave`; `SettingValue.Session` and `Saved` (the persisted value beneath a session value).
- Writes-through rule: a session write goes to `settings.json` when the runtime has no session (no Model's `Init` ran: a CLI process, a script) or `settings.autosave` is on. Writing the file (`Set`, `Reset`, the Settings page, `settings set`, the page's undo) drops that setting's session value. A file change the poll reads leaves a session value on top and marks the setting changed on disk. The Settings page and `settings set` still save at once; nothing in the framework writes the session layer yet (the `:set` line, lane C, will).
- Provenance: `Origin{Layer, Path, Line, By, Source, Via, At, Disk}` and `Origin.String()` for `:verbose`: a default's embedded file (`settings/ochazuke.json`) or the application's declaring `file:line`; settings.json's path and the key's line; a session value's history source (`palette`, `key:<keys>`, `cli`, `api`, `macro:<r>`), the name it ran by and the time (the Model's clock); "settings.json changed on disk at T".
- New verbs: `setting.revert {name}`, `setting.write {name}` (alias `setting.save`; the name defaults to the focused Settings row), `settings.write` (aliases `settings.save` and the bare `mksettings`), `settings.revert`, `settings.diff` (rows `SettingDiff{Name, Type, Saved, SavedIn, Session, Origin}`), `settings.reset-all` (asks first from every path: "Reset all N changed settings to their defaults for this session?" or "… in PATH?").
- New settings: `settings.autosave` (boolean, default false), `settings.mark-session` (boolean, default true): the Settings page draws a session value bold (tone `marker.session`, `ToneSession`) with the saved value beside it, `‹ stack ›  (saved: tabs)`, only while the two differ; the detail reads "Set this session (palette, 14:02); settings.json holds tabs. setting.write saves it, setting.revert drops it."
- **Changed CLI behaviour:** `BIN settings set`, `settings reset`, `settings write` and `settings reset-all` now ask before they write `settings.json` from a CLI process ("Write scrolloff = 5 to PATH? [y/N]"); `--yes`, or the new short `-y`, answers yes, and a script without either now fails with "declined". `settings reset` of a key the file does not hold, and a write with nothing to write, do not ask. The Go API (`Execute` from code, source `api`) and a running TUI are unchanged.
- o-story: a story's `values` (saved values) and `session` (session values, set on the driver's clock); stories `settings/session-marker`, `session-marker-off`, `session-marker-80`. The `settings/` goldens count one more section tab (`settings`); `settings/page-tabs` wraps to it.
- Consumers: nothing to change. A script that runs `BIN settings set` non-interactively adds `-y`.

### Settings: the setting verbs and `settings.apply` (the `:set` plan, lane C)

- New verbs on `setting`, each `{name, scope}` (name defaults to the focused Settings row; scope `session` by default, by the writes-through rule, or `file`): `setting.show {verbose}` (status line `name=value`, `name`/`noname` for a boolean; verbose adds `Origin.String()`), `setting.set {value}`, `setting.toggle`, `setting.increase`, `setting.decrease`, `setting.multiply {by}`, `setting.append`, `setting.prepend`, `setting.remove {value}` (`setting_verbs.go`). Which types each verb fits comes from the `:set` type table; the verb computes the new value and checks it with `coerceSetting` before writing. A write answers `SettingWritten{…, written: "session"|"file"}`, a show `SettingShown{…, line, origin}`. CLI: `BIN setting set scrolloff 5`, `setting increase scrolloff 2`, `setting append paths a,b`, `setting show paths --verbose`; from a CLI process each asks before writing settings.json (`-y`).
- Changed: `setting.cycle` gains `name` and `scope`; `setting.reset` and `setting.edit` gain `name` and `scope`. Kept: every existing verb (`setting.add`, `setting-item.*`, `settings.set`, `settings.reset`, …) and every key.
- New verb: `settings.apply {line, verbose, scope}`, the `:set` front-end: `BIN settings apply 'scrolloff=5 nowrap'`. Every word is planned first (all or nothing: one refused word changes nothing), then each runs as a request to its verb; one undo step on the Settings page; show words go to the status line together; a leading `verbose`/`verb` adds origins; the empty line and `all` answer their lists (the palette opens them as results); `all&` asks; from a CLI process one question names every change. Session values it sets record `settings.apply` (or the alias it was typed as) as their origin's name. The line is one string parameter for now; the palette's rest-of-line parameter and the `set`/`se` aliases are lane D.
- Changed: the Settings page's keys (`h`/`l`/`left`/`right`/`space` cycle, `r`/`backspace`/`delete` reset, `enter` edit) run the setting verbs with `scope: file` in their rows (`bindings/settings-editor.json`), so the page still saves each change at once. The page's undo now restores session values too, and a step may hold several settings.
- `Origin.String()` says "set in settings.json" when there is no config directory.
- Stories `settings/set-line-show`, `settings/set-line-error`, `settings/set-line-all`.
- Consumers: nothing to change.

### Settings: `:set` on the palette line, with completion (the `:set` plan, lane D)

- New: `Parameter.Rest`, a parameter that takes the rest of the line verbatim (quotes and spaces as typed); the palette hands it the text from its first word to the end, the CLI joins the remaining words with spaces. General: any verb may declare one (at most one, a string, with no required parameter after it; `Operation.RestParameter`). Its `Source` is asked with the text as `query` and, when the source declares `cursor` (`ChoicesCursorParameter`), the cursor in it; each choice's id is the text up to the cursor completed, and `tab` splices it in place with no space after (`PaletteEntry.Splice`, `From`). History recall spells a rest back as typed, after the bare alias it was typed by. `settings.apply`'s `line` is a rest parameter: `:settings apply scrolloff=5 nowrap`, `BIN settings apply scrolloff=5 nowrap`.
- **Changed palette behaviour:** a `settings apply "…"` line keeps its quotes now (the rest is verbatim); type the line without them.
- New aliases (framework): `set`, `se`, `setlocal`, `setl`, `setglobal`, `setg` → `settings.apply` (settings are global, so the local and global forms are `:set`); `verbose`, `verb` → `settings.apply {verbose: true}`, so `:verbose set opt?` adds where values were set; `options`, `opt` → `settings.edit` (the Settings page).
- New: `ActionAlias.Shadows` (and `shadows` on an `actions.json` alias row), a bare alias that may take a verb's name and yields to it by what follows (`ReadBareAlias`, one rule for the palette and the CLI): a noun with the verb of its name right after it runs that noun's verb (`set mode normal` is `mode set normal`; `set focus`, `set palette`, `set setting`, `set settings` likewise); an exact noun wins, then the rest parameter's reading (its source offers the word exactly, then as a prefix); a word that is both a noun's prefix and offered by the rest is an error naming both (`"foc" is ambiguous: noun focus (focus set), or line focus-display`). Such an alias is named by its bare word (`set`), since `NOUN.VERB` is the verb it shadows; it needs no `aliases.shadow: allow`. Bare aliases of the same action after the first add their presets (`verbose set`).
- New verb: `settings.complete {query, cursor}`, the `:set` line's completion source: setting names, sorted (`noNAME`/`invNAME` for booleans once a word is typed, `all` and `all&` as the first word), the current value after `=` or `:`, an enum's values and `true`/`false`, a list's items after `-=`; each label names the class and the current value.
- `CanonicalRequest` of a bare alias request (`Request{Verb: "w!"}`) resolves through the alias row directly (same result for every existing alias).
- `testdata/set-collisions.json`: every family app's noun words that are a setting's name or prefix, with how `set WORD` reads; `TestSetCollisionsAcrossTheFamily` checks each. Four route to a noun's `set` verb: `focus` (`focus-display`), `palette` (`palette.missing-opens-form`), `setting` and `settings` (`settings.autosave`, `settings.mark-session`).
- Stories `settings/set-line-bare`, `set-complete`, `set-ambiguous`, `set-verbose` (dark, 80 columns); `set-line-show` and `set-line-error` type their lines unquoted.
- Consumers: nothing to change. An app with a bare alias named like a verb under `aliases.shadow: allow` keeps it; `shadows: true` on the row is the new way.

## v0.48.0 (2026-10-29)

### Inherited sequences no longer shadow a mode's own keys

- fixed: in `visual-line` (which extends `normal`) a row noun's normal `d d` (mtm's task delete, pane kill) made the list's `visual-line` `d` wait for a second key, so it took `V j d d`. A sequence a level only inherits from the mode its mode extends (`Noun.Extends` or `ModeExtends`) no longer waits when a row of the mode's own tables, at any level, completes the keys typed: `V j d` acts at once, as in Vim, and the footer and `bindings list` leave the shadowed `d d` out. A mode's own sequences, and inherited ones nothing in the mode binds shorter, wait as before.
- New setting `sequence-inherit` (`SequenceInheritedSetting`, `drop` (default) | `keep`): `keep` lets an inherited sequence wait as before.
- Test `TestVisualLineInheritedSequenceDoesNotShadow`; stories `tables/visual-line-inherited-sequence-drop`, `tables/visual-line-inherited-sequence-keep`. The `settings/` stories count one more General row. UX-RULES R1.7, R5.16.
- Consumers: nothing to change. mtm's `V j d` on Tasks and Panes asks once; o-json's `node/visual-line` `d` is unchanged.

### Path chooser: start source and per-source Keep

- `PathChooserSpec.Source` names the source the path chooser opens on (`known`, an application's `AddPathSource` name, or `browse`/`recent`/`typed`), passed through as `ChooserSpec.Source`. mtm no longer steps `chooser.next-source` after opening to reach its known source.
- `ChooserSource.Keep`: the cell columns cut last while that source is current, in place of `ChooserSpec.Keep` (the same `fitWidths` rule). A known-folder source keeps its path readable while the branch and project columns narrow.
- A chooser whose `Source` names a source this open does not have (a builder said false) now opens on the remembered source (`chooser.remember-source`), else the first; before, it opened on the first.
- Test `TestPathChooserStartSourceAndKeep`.
- Consumers: nothing to change; mtm adopts both.

## v0.47.0 (2026-10-29)

### Framework settings are known from startup

- Every framework setting with a fixed row is declared when the runtime is made (`NewRuntime`), from the embedded `settings/ochazuke.json`, instead of on first use or at `Model.Init`: an application can set `forms.single-path` (or any of them) at startup, and a user's `settings.json` naming one no longer reports `unknown setting` when it is read before the Model starts. Moved into the file from Go: `modal-dim`, `dim-unfocused`, `focus-display`, `help.pick-view`, `help.table-view`, `help.pending-modes`, `palette.missing-opens-form`, `status.segments`, `status.segment-order` (same names, types, defaults and checks).
- An application's `DeclareSetting` of a framework setting's name, before or after, replaces the row with its own default (the embedded check kept when it gives none); a second application declaration is still refused. Application settings list before the framework's, as before.
- `terminal-leader` moved to `settings/terminal.json`: still declared only by `EnsureTerminalLeader`, so an application without terminals never lists it.
- A setting declared after `settings.json` was read (a theme, a segment, an extension's) withdraws the file's `unknown setting "…" (kept)` warning for its name.
- Settings still declared by `EnsureConfigSettings`, because their rows depend on the application: `theme` (its choices), `pages.hidden` (its default), the per-segment settings, `history-limit` and `history-fetches`.
- Consumers: nothing to change; an app that called `EnsureConfigSettings` early only to have these settings may drop the call. Tests that count `settings list` rows see the framework's too.
- Tests `TestFrameworkSettingSetAtStartup`, `TestFrameworkSettingsFileLoadsWithoutWarning`, `TestApplicationDeclaresFrameworkSetting`, `TestDimDefaultsMatchEmbeddedRows`. The `settings/` stories now show the framework's rows beside the story's own (General counts, the name column's width, the section tabs; `page-tabs` wraps from General back to `path`).

## v0.46.0 (2026-10-29)

### Dot-repeat after typing, nothing to repeat, a lone path opens its chooser

- fixed: after anything was typed into an inline input, every later `.` outside an input replayed the typing and failed (`Error: no focused input`). Dot-repeat now takes the last repeatable change that can run at the focus (`repeatApplies`: available there, and an input focused for the editor's implicitly guarded `input.*` changes); typing is skipped outside an input and the change before it runs (UX-RULES R9.5).
- `.` with nothing to repeat is no longer an error: new setting `repeat.empty` `info` (default: `nothing to repeat` in the plain tone) | `silent`. `history.repeat` returns no error in that case.
- A key or command line whose only missing required argument is a path opens the path chooser at once, its pick running the action: new setting `forms.single-path` `chooser` (default; optional parameters left out) | `alone` (only when the path is the verb's one parameter) | `form` (the args form, as before). An args form that opens on a missing path stop (`path.field` `picker`) now opens its chooser over it, so typing reaches the path instead of the form's normal keys (`u` was `form.undo`) (UX-RULES R12.6).
- o-story: a story verb's path parameter may name `{{files}}` in its `base`, and its writes show the folder as `{{files}}`.
- Stories `actions/dot-after-input-closes`, `dot-nothing-to-repeat`, `dot-nothing-silent`, `palette/form-single-path-opens-chooser`, `form-single-path-esc-cancels`, `form-single-path-setting-form`, `form-path-with-optional`; test `TestDotSkipsTypingOutsideAnInput`.
- Consumers: o-json's `e` (`viewer.open {path}`) now opens the path chooser directly; nothing to change. An app that wants the old form sets `forms.single-path` to `form`.

## v0.45.0 (2026-10-29)

### Alias presets are part of a request

- fixed: an alias row's preset `args` were checked as a whole request, so an alias whose action has a required parameter the presets leave out was refused at load (`alias w! args: path is required`), the v0.43.0 example `{"alias": "w!", "action": "restructure.write", "args": {"force": true}}` among them. Presets are now checked as a partial request at load (known parameter names, types, choices: an unknown or wrongly typed preset still skips the row), and the required parameters when the alias runs, merged with what was typed or bound: a key or the palette opens the args form for the missing one (R12.6, `palette.missing-opens-form`), the CLI and the API fail with `NAME is required`. One check for both bindings.json row args and alias presets (`validatePartialArguments`, `runtime.go`).
- Tests `TestAliasPartialPresetArgs`, `cli` `TestCLIAliasPartialPresets`; story `actions/alias-partial-preset`.
- Consumers: o-json can point `w!` (and kin) at `restructure.write`/`viewer.write` with `{force: true}` presets in place of a separate verb.

## v0.44.0 (2026-10-29)

### Chooser sources, a path chooser for path parameters, choices that see the typed text

- A chooser can list its rows from sources, one at a time: `ChooserSpec.Sources []ChooserSource{Name, Label, Load(query) Result, Live, Empty, Open, Up, Create, CreateLabel}` and `ChooserSpec.Source` (the one to open on). New verbs on `ochazuke.chooser`: `next-source`/`previous-source` (`tab`/`shift+tab`, guard `chooser-sources`: more than one source, so `tab` stays free elsewhere), `open` (`enter` with guard `chooser-enter-opens`, `ctrl+l`; guard `chooser-opens`), `up` (`alt+up`, and `backspace` on an empty query, guard `chooser-up-empty`); `alt+enter` is `chooser.pick` on the highlighted row whatever it is. The query carries over between sources; the title reads `Title · one [two] three`. A Live source loads again on every edit (rows shown as given); a `Pending` load runs off the loop as a replacing job of the chooser (`ochazuke.chooser.load`), `loading…` meanwhile, a failure is the empty line. `ChooserRow.Opens` marks a row its source opens (a folder), `NoPick` one that only opens (picking it opens it). Go: `Chooser.Source`, `Sources`, `Loading`, `SetQuery`; `Runtime.ChooserLastSource(kind)`.
- New settings (rows of `settings/ochazuke.json`, declared by `EnsureConfigSettings`): `chooser.remember-source` (default on: a chooser reopens on the source last used for its kind; off, on its first); `chooser.enter` `open` (default) | `pick` (what enter does on a row that both opens and picks); `path.start` `base` (default: the application's folder, `SetPathBase`, such as the open file's; else the working directory) | `home` | `cwd`; `path.field` `picker` (default: a path field is a stop whose `enter`/`space`/`i` opens the path chooser) | `text` (typed freely, `ctrl+space` or `enter` in normal mode opens it); `path.hidden` (default off); `path.no-auto-list` (list, default `/Volumes/`, `/mnt/`, `/media/`, `/run/media/`: folders under these are never listed on their own, only once opened into or typed; checked on the path string, no file system access).
- Path parameters: `Parameter.Type` `"path"` with `Path` (`file`, the default, or `dir`), `Base` (where browse starts, `~` and relative to the working directory) and `New` (a path not there yet may be chosen). Accepted by the palette, the args form, `actions.json` and the CLI as a string. `Runtime.OpenPathChooser(PathChooserSpec{Kind, Title, Pick: PathPick{Name, Parameter, Value}, Choose})` opens the path chooser (kind `path-chooser`) with sources `browse` (one folder, folders first with `/`; a folder chooser lists folders only and ends with `use <folder>`; with `New`, the create row names a new file in the folder), `recent` (`SetRecentPaths`, then the paths picked this run, `RecentPaths`), `typed` (Live: completes the path as typed, rows read as typed, the typed path itself when it exists, `new …` after the completions when `New` allows); then the application's (`AddPathSource(name, func(PathPick) (ChooserSource, bool))`). `PathStart(pick)`: the field's folder (a relative value under `Base`), else `Base`, else `path.start`. Form fields of type `path` open it (record editors and the args form). New files `chooser_sources.go`, `path_chooser.go`.
- Choices that see the typed text: a choices source (`Parameter.Source`, or a noun's list verb) that declares a parameter named `query` (`ChoicesQueryParameter`) is called with the text typed so far: the palette word (cached per word), a form's suggestions chooser (a Live source, asked again as you type), and the check of a value. Go: `Runtime.ChoicesFor(ctx, p, query)`, `ChoicesTakeQuery(p)`; `Choices` is `ChoicesFor` with no query. A source without the parameter is called as before.
- Inline completion options, per trigger: `CompletionTrigger.DropTrigger` (accepting replaces the trigger too), `Boundaries` (characters besides whitespace that end a token both ways, so a jq `.` completes after `|` or `(`; may not hold the trigger's characters), `SearchAt func(ctx, CompletionQuery{Query, Text, Cursor, Token})` in place of `Search` (the whole text and cursor, to complete a document path after `.`). `FindCompletionTokenIn(text, cursor, triggers)`; `FindCompletionToken` is unchanged.
- Tests `chooser_sources_test.go`, `path_chooser_test.go`, completion tests; stories `chooser/sources-cycle`, `live-source`, `remember-source-on`, `remember-source-off`, `forms/path-browse`, `path-dir`, `path-typed`, `path-text-field`. o-story stories take `files` (a temp tree, `{{files}}` in field bases, values and writes), chooser `sources`, and `demo.choose` reopens the page's chooser. UX-RULES R7.7, R7.8, R9.7, R12.6.
- Consumers: nothing changes until they adopt. o-json: declare `viewer.open {path}` as `{type: path, base: <open file's folder>}` (or `SetPathBase`), `restructure.write {path}` and `viewer.export {path}` as `{type: path, new: true}`; the jq input's function trigger can use `DropTrigger`, and path completion after `.` a trigger `{Trigger: ".", Boundaries: "|(),", SearchAt}`. mtm: directory fields become `{type: path, path: dir}`; register `AddPathSource("known", …)` and `AddPathSource("project", …)` with `ChooserRow.Data` the path, and `SetRecentPaths`.

## v0.43.0 (2026-10-29)

### Visual selection is a mode on lists, tables and trees

- `V` on any list, table or tree with marks (was: tables only) enters `visual-line`, the line-shaped selection mode the editor's `V` already used; the list follows the mode (`List.ModeChanged`): entering it on a row starts the selection, leaving it ends it, and a modal opened over it (a confirm, the palette, a chooser) holds it until its close resumes it. The badge reads `VISUAL-LINE`.
- In `visual-line` one key acts on the selection, then visual ends: `d` (`list.apply {verb: delete}`, the rows' own `delete`), `y` (`list.yank {what: row}`, now on any list over a selection; guard `list-yanks`), an application's single keys in its row noun's `visual-line` table (o-json `>`, `<`, `p`, `S`), any row's own verb through the existing `Subjects`/`Many` fan-out; `esc`/`V` leave, `j`/`k`/counts/`G`/`gg` extend (`ochazuke.list/visual-line` extends `normal`), `:` and `?` open the palette and help (`app/visual-line`).
- fixed: a verb available only over a selection was always refused from a key: `list.apply` ended the selection before its rows' requests were judged. The selection now holds while they run, and `list.apply` is itself available only when a row it would act on takes the verb and the verb is available there (guard `list-applies`), so `d` over rows without a `delete` passes the key on.
- New settings: `visual.after-action` `end` (default, as in Vim) | `keep` (the selection stays over the rows still shown); `visual.list` `mode` (default) | `marks` (opt out: the older selection in normal mode, the rows' normal keys acting on it).
- Selection shape is a mode property, not a name (SC-05): `ModeSpec.selection` in a bindings file's `"modes"`, `Runtime.DefineSelection`, `SelectionShape`, `SelectionMode`, constants `SelectionChar`/`SelectionLine`/`SelectionBlock`; `editor.json` declares `visual`/`visual-line`/`visual-block`, the grid declares `visual-row` (`row`) and `visual-column` (`column`). The editor reads shapes (`isVisual`'s three names are gone); `visualApply` no longer names `ochazuke.list` (SC-09).
- Mode fallback (`ModeExtends`): `DefineMode(name, pending, ModeExtends("normal"))` or `"modes": {"query": {"extends": "normal"}}` makes a mode read the parent mode's rows for every noun with no table of its own in it (no rows, no per-noun extends; an empty group counts as a table), so a "normal plus a few keys" mode binds only its own keys. `visual-line` extends `normal`; `ochazuke.outline/visual-line` is an empty table so the outline's `d d` never makes `d` wait. `Runtime.ModeParent`; `bindings export` writes it.
- Changed: `V` answers on plain lists and trees (it did not); `y` in `visual-line` on a cell-mode table yanks the rows (in normal it is still the cell). A row noun's own `visual-line` table replaces its normal keys there.
- Tests `list_visual_test.go`; stories `tables/visual-line-delete-two`, `visual-line-yank-two`, `visual-line-motions-esc`, `visual-after-keep`, `visual-after-end`, `tree/visual-line-nest`; `tables/visual-apply` now `V j j d y`. UX-RULES R5.16.
- Consumers: o-json moves its selection rows (`node/normal` `d` `restructure.delete`, `y` `restructure.yank`) into `node/visual-line` and adds `>`/`<`/`p`/`S` there; mtm replaces `isVisualMode` with `r.SelectionShape(mode) != ""` (`copymode.go`).

### Tree zoom with a breadcrumb

- Deep in a tree the outer levels pushed the rows off the right with no trail. New verbs on `ochazuke.outline` (every tree, editable or not; guard `tree-zoom`): `zoom-in` (`z +`) draws only the node's subtree, `zoom-out` (`z -`) steps out one level onto the zoomed node's parent, `zoom-reset` (`z 0`) shows every level. `h` past the zoom's top steps the zoom out and lands on the parent, as `h` steps onto the parent anywhere. Zoom is view state like collapse: never on the undo stack, never a change; an edit, undo or `Select` that moves the selection out of the zoom steps the zoom out to show it. Rows are hidden, so the default `z` rows carry no hint.
- The hidden ancestors are a breadcrumb: the nearest `tree.breadcrumb.ancestors` named and dimmed (`Styles.Muted`), farther ones folded into `…`, cut from the left like the focus path (`FitFocusPath`), after the nested-json `1-drill` prototype.
- New settings (rows of `settings/ochazuke.json`, declared by `EnsureConfigSettings`): `tree.zoom` `manual` (default) | `auto` (also zooms, onto the selected row's grandparent, when its indent takes over half the tree's width) | `off` (verbs unavailable, a zoom in place dropped at the next frame); `tree.zoom.root` `crumb` (default: the node ends the breadcrumb and its children are the top rows) | `row` (it stays as the top row); `tree.breadcrumb` `line` (default: a line above the rows) | `title` (the header beside the mode while the tree has the focus: after the noun, or in the `stack` before the focused row) | `off`; `tree.breadcrumb.ancestors` (integer, default 2, 0 names all).
- Go: `Tree.Zoom(id)` (`""` shows every level), `Tree.ZoomOut`, `Tree.Zoomed`, `Tree.Breadcrumb`, `Tree.Height` (the list's Height plus the breadcrumb line; size a tree to its rows with it), `Runtime.TreeZoomMode`, `TreeZoomRoot`, `TreeBreadcrumb`, `TreeBreadcrumbAncestors`, constants `TreeZoomSetting`, `TreeZoomRootSetting`, `TreeBreadcrumbSetting`, `TreeBreadcrumbAncestorsSetting` and their values. New file `tree_zoom.go`.
- Tests `tree_zoom_test.go`; stories `tree/zoom-in-out-reset`, `zoom-h-steps-out`, `zoom-breadcrumb-dims`, `zoom-ancestors`, `zoom-ancestors-all`, `zoom-root-row`, `zoom-breadcrumb-title`, `zoom-breadcrumb-off`, `zoom-auto`, `zoom-manual-no-auto`, `zoom-off`. o-story sizes a tree with `Tree.Height`. UX-RULES R11.7.
- Consumers: o-json's structure tree and mtm's outline get the verbs and keys with no change. A host that sizes a tree with `tree.List().Height()` should switch to `tree.Height()`, or the breadcrumb line windows one row.

### Verb checks: a before-quit hook

- New: a verb carries checks run before it from every path, after its guard and before its Confirm (`verb_checks.go`): `Runtime.AddVerbCheck(key, VerbCheck{Name, Check func(r, c, args) Verdict})`, `RemoveVerbCheck`, `VerbChecks`; verdicts `Allow()`, `AskFirst(prompt)` (the confirm modal; `y` runs the request Confirmed) and `Refuse(reason)` (the request fails with it). The first check that does not allow decides; a request that is Confirmed or carries `force: true` skips them. `Operation.Checks` holds them (not in JSON).
- New: `Runtime.BeforeQuit(name, func(r, c) Verdict)`, a check on `app.quit`. `app.quit` gains the parameter `force` (boolean): `app.quit {force: true}` quits without running the checks.
- o-story: a story's `checks` (`{verb, name, ask, refuse, dirty}`) and the expectation `quit`. Stories `quit/asks-before-quit`, `quit/clean-quits`, `quit/force-skips`, `quit/refuse`; tests `verb_checks_test.go`.
- Consumers: no change needed. o-json drops `guardQuit` (its wrapper of `app.quit`) for one `BeforeQuit` call; its `Q` row keeps `app.quit {force: true}`.

### Outline nodes that refuse a change

- New: `TreeNode.Fixed []string` (`fixed` in JSON): the changes a node refuses, `rename`, `move` (move, indent, outdent) and `delete` (the node or any ancestor of it); `TreeNode.Refuses(op)`. `Tree.Refuse func(node, change) error` is an application's check on every change (add included, its node the parent); undo and redo are not checked. A refusal holds from keys, the palette, the CLI, the API and `Tree.Edit`, and nothing is written.
- changed: `outline.edit`, `indent`, `outdent`, `move` and `delete` are guarded by `outline-renamable`, `outline-movable` and `outline-deletable` (each implies `tree-editable`), so on a fixed node their keys and hints drop out and the palette does not offer them.
- Stories `tree/fixed-root-refuses`, `tree/fixed-rename-only`; tests `tree_fixed_test.go`.
- Consumers: no change for trees without `Fixed`. o-json marks its restructure root `Fixed: [rename, move, delete]` (and array items `[rename]`) and drops its own root checks.

### Tone backgrounds; the error tone

- New: `ToneStyle.Bg` (`bg` in `themes.json`: a colour or a role, light/dark sides), drawn under the run; `Styles.Tone` sets it too. `ToneSpan.Bg` names the tone whose background goes under a span while `Tone` keeps the foreground (`Tone` is now `omitempty`; a span may carry `Bg` alone).
- New: the built-in tone `error` (`ToneError`): bold on `#F6D3CE` (light) / `#5E2722` (dark), bold alone in mono and `NO_COLOR`. Chip tones for a query's terms or tags: `chip` (`ToneChip`, a dim background `#E2EAE5` / `#26362F`) and `chip.focused` (`ToneChipFocused`, the scheme's surface on its accent, bold); test `TestChipTones`.
- New setting `tones.selected-background` (`SelectedBackgroundSetting`, `drop` default | `keep`; `settings/ochazuke.json`, declared by `EnsureConfigSettings`; `Runtime.SelectedBackground`): the selected row drops a tone's background with its colour, or keeps the cell on its background, bold, out of the reverse bar.
- o-story: `attrs` reads `bg`. Stories `tables/tones-error-background`, `tables/tones-error-background-keep`; tests `tone_bg_test.go`.
- Consumers: no change; themes.json files without `bg` read as before. o-json tones a duplicate key's cell `error` (or `{"tone": KEYTONE, "bg": "error"}`).

### Alias rows with preset args; bare aliases

- New: `ActionAlias.Args` / `AliasRow.Args` (`"args"` on an actions.json alias row): preset arguments the alias runs its action with, from keys, the palette, the CLI and the API; a typed or bound argument of the same name wins. Checked against the action's parameters at load (a bad one skips the row with a notice).
- New: bare aliases. An alias without a dot (`w!`, `q`) takes its action's noun (`ActionAlias.Bare`, `Name()`; full name `noun.w!`); its one word runs it in the palette (before any verb of the name), on the CLI (`bin w!`) and in code (`Request{Verb: "w!"}`, `Do("w!")`, `ResolveAction`, `ActionAlias`). A bindings.json row names it by its full name. A bare alias may not be a noun's name.
- New setting `aliases.shadow` (`AliasShadowSetting`, `refuse` default | `allow`; `settings/ochazuke.json`; `Runtime.AliasShadow`): a bare alias named like a registered verb is skipped and reported, or loaded and, typed alone, wins over the verb.
- fixed: a palette line naming an alias under a framework noun's short word carried a `Via` the alias table did not know (the handler saw the canonical name); it now carries the alias's name.
- changed: on the CLI, an alias with presets is its own command (not an oden alias of its verb), with its preset parameters not required and a `Presets:` help line.
- Stories `actions/alias-preset-args`, `actions/alias-shadow-refused`, `actions/alias-shadow-allowed`; tests `alias_args_test.go`, `cli` `TestCLIBareAliasWithArgs`.
- Consumers: none until they add rows. o-json adds `{"alias": "w!", "action": "restructure.write", "args": {"force": true}}` and kin (as its own `AddActionAlias` calls, or documented rows for users' actions.json).

### Input.Selection

- New: `Input.Selection() (start, end int, ok bool)`, the visual selection's rune range `[start, end)` in `visual` and `visual-line` (the runes under the anchor and the cursor both in it; a line selection whole lines and its last newline); `ok` false outside visual and in `visual-block`. Test `TestInputSelection`.
- Consumers: o-json's `jq.wrap` (`wrapSelection`, `query.go`) and `queryline.go` read it instead of `min/max(Anchor, Cursor)`.

### Foreground programs

- New: `Foreground(cmd *exec.Cmd) Result` (`foreground.go`) hands the terminal to another full-screen program: a job of the verb whose `ForegroundMsg` the Model answers with `tea.ExecProcess` (the TUI suspends and resumes on exit; `Model.Foreground` replaces the answer), and `Runtime.Deliver` runs in place when there is no Model (the CLI shell; `ForegroundMsg.Run`). The completion's value is `ForegroundExit{Program, Code}`; status 0 posts `PROGRAM exited` (`ctx.Info`), another status fails the job with `PROGRAM exited with status N` (`ctx.Error`).
- New: actions.json command rows take `"foreground": true` (`Action.Foreground`; refused with `timeout` or `json`). `actions.example.json` shows one, and an alias row.
- o-story: build answers foreground programs headless (no input, output dropped); the driver answers a `ForegroundMsg` while it waits for a job. Story `foreground/exit-status`; tests `foreground_test.go`.
- Consumers: o-json's "open the export in o-csv" is a verb returning `Foreground(exec.Command("o-csv", FILE))`, or a user's actions.json row `{"noun": "export", "verb": "open-csv", "command": ["o-csv", "{{data.path}}"], "foreground": true}`.

## v0.42.0 (2026-10-28)

### Standard editing keys in every input; renames open selected

- Every input (page inputs, prompts, the palette line, form fields, outline lines) answers the standard editing keys in insert, as rows of `bindings/editor.json`'s `ochazuke.editor/insert` table: `alt+backspace`, `ctrl+backspace` → `input.kill {motion: word-previous}`; `super+backspace` → `input.kill {motion: line-start}` (beside `ctrl+u`); `alt+d`, `ctrl+delete` → `input.kill {motion: word-next}`; `alt+left`, `ctrl+left` → `input.move {motion: word-previous}`; `alt+right`, `ctrl+right` → `input.move {motion: word-next}`; `super+left`, `meta+left` → `input.move {motion: line-start}`; `super+right`, `meta+right` → `input.move {motion: line-end}`. `ctrl+w`, `ctrl+u`, `ctrl+k` unchanged. The new kill rows are `hidden` (footer); `?` help lists them. UX-RULES R9.1a.
- `input.kill` gains the motions `word-previous` and `word-next` (what `alt+b` and `alt+f` move over), declares its motion enum, and kills an open select range whatever the motion, as backspace and delete do (R9.6). No new verb.
- `meta+left`/`meta+right`: a kitty terminal spells cmd+arrow `CSI 1;9D`, which the decoder reads with xterm's modifier bits as meta; tuios re-encodes it as `CSI 1;33D`, which reads as super. Both are bound.
- New setting `rename.select` (`RenameSelectSetting`, boolean, default **true**; in `settings/ochazuke.json`, declared by `EnsureConfigSettings`): `outline.edit` without `select` (`i` on an outline; o-json's `r`) opens the line with its text selected, typing replaces it, a motion keeps it and moves (`end`/`ctrl+e` to the end, `left` to the start). `RecordSpec.Rename` (new) does the same for a record editor's start field and opens it in insert. Off: cursor at the end, as before. `Runtime.RenameSelect()`. An explicit `select` still wins. UX-RULES R11.6.
- Tests `input_keys_test.go`; stories `inputs/word-delete-keys`, `inputs/word-motion-keys`, `tree/rename-select`, `tree/rename-select-off`. Tree stories that appended after `i` now press `end` first.
- Consumers: every app gets the keys with no change. An outline line opened by `i` now starts selected (set `rename.select` false for the old behaviour). o-json's `r` picks the rename up from the bump. mtm's rename form (`OpenRecordEditor` with `Insert: true`) opts in with `Rename: true`.

## v0.41.0 (2026-10-28)

### Hide any page from the tabs (opt-out for pages)

- New setting `pages.hidden` (`PagesHiddenSetting`, a list of page ids, default the pages declared hidden by default): a hidden page leaves the tab row and `page.next`/`page.previous` (ctrl+j/k) but stays registered and reachable: `page.open` opens it and says it is hidden, the `'` picker lists it marked `hidden`, the palette and the CLI complete it, an app's `OpenPage` still opens it; its tab shows while it is current. One setting for every page: an app's own, mounted catalog pages, the Settings page (UX-RULES R6.13, the R6.12 opt-out rule for pages).
- New verbs `ochazuke.page.hide {id}` and `ochazuke.page.show {id}` (id positional, default the current page; availability: hide while the named page is in the tabs, show while it is hidden). `page.hide` refuses the last page in the tabs. `ochazuke.pages.list` rows gain `hidden`.
- Opt-in pages: `Page.DefaultHidden`, the `DefaultHidden()` instance option (AddPage, RegisterPage) and `cli.CatalogPage.DefaultHidden` (`defaultHidden` in a catalog; DeclarePage and MountPage pass it, the catalog publishes it for Go-drawn pages too) declare a page hidden by default; those ids are the setting's default (once settings.json holds the setting it is the whole list; `settings reset pages.hidden` returns to the defaults).
- Start page: `Model.Init` calls `Runtime.OpenVisibleStart`, which opens the first page in the tabs when the start page is hidden; afterwards opening a hidden page stays on it. Go: `Runtime.HidePage`, `ShowPage`, `PageHidden`, `HiddenPages`, `TabPages`.
- Applies live: read every frame and page motion, so `settings set`, `config.reload`, the verbs and another process's write show at once.
- o-story: a story page takes `defaultHidden`; build leaves a hidden start page as Model.Init does. Stories `pages/hide-tab-gone`, `pages/hidden-opens-from-palette`, `pages/default-hidden`; tests `page_hide_test.go`, `cli` `TestCatalogPageDefaultHidden`.
- Consumers: mtm and every app get the setting and verbs with no change (`page hide runs` hides mtm's Runs page); an app that wants a new page opt-in passes `DefaultHidden()`.

## v0.40.0 (2026-10-28)

### One sequence-timeout for every pending prefix; leader rows name their setting

- Setting `sequence-timeout` (`SequenceTimeoutSetting`, duration, **default `0s`: wait until a key comes**) governs every pending prefix: a multi-key row's prefix (`g` of `g g`, `z` of `z R`) and a row entering a pending mode with a prefix (the terminal leader). Before, sequences waited `DefaultBindingTimeout` (1s) and the leader had its own setting. Set it (`settings set sequence-timeout 1s`) for the old expiry. `Runtime.BindingTimeout` now defaults to 0 (read the setting); a positive value still fixes the timeout in code.
- Setting `sequence-unknown` (`SequenceUnknownSetting`, `pass` | `swallow`, default `pass`): a key no row continues a pending sequence with. `pass` runs the sequence's shorter match and handles the key as if typed alone (today's behaviour for `g x`; for the leader, the leader then the key, now two sends instead of one); `swallow` drops both with a notice `ctrl+a x is not bound` (the "unknown live leader key is swallowed" item, as an option).
- The framework's settings the resolver reads are rows of an embedded `settings/ochazuke.json` (name, type, default, description, enum, deprecated), declared by `EnsureConfigSettings` / `EnsureTerminalLeader` unless the application declared the name first. `terminal-leader`'s default moved there too.
- A binding row's keys, hint, help and string arguments may name a setting, `{terminal-leader}`: the row is resolved when installed (Go `Bind`, the builder, defaults files, the user's `bindings.json`) and again when the setting is declared or changes. The terminal rows in `bindings/terminal.json` are keyed `{terminal-leader}`. Removed: finding leader rows by matching `mode.enter {mode: leader_pending}` and rewriting them (`leaderRow`, `rekeyRows`, `releadRow`, `applyTerminalLeader`). **An application's own leader row must name the setting** (`Bind("{terminal-leader}", Enter("leader_pending", Payload{Prefix: "{terminal-leader}", Request: &send}))`, `send` with `keys: "{terminal-leader}"`); a row written `ctrl+a` stays on `ctrl+a`. `SetTerminalLeader(key)` now sets the setting.
- Removed rows: `leader_pending`'s `any` and `paste` catch-alls; `sequence-unknown` covers them.
- Removed special cases: the `if mode == "leader_pending"` branch in `armPrefix` (SC-01), `terminal-leader-timeout`'s own declaration and `leaderTimeoutDefault` (SC-02), leader-row matching (SC-03).
- Deprecated for one release: `terminal-leader-timeout` is an old name of `sequence-timeout` (`Setting.Deprecated`, new): `settings get|set|reset`, a declaration and a `settings.json` key under it reach `sequence-timeout`, warning once; a write moves the file's value to the new name. `TerminalLeaderTimeoutSetting` and `DefaultBindingTimeout` move to `deprecated.go` (gone under `ochazuke_strict`).
- Tests `sequence_timeout_test.go` (replaces `terminal_leader_timeout_test.go`); stories `lists/sequence-waits-forever`, `lists/sequence-timeout-expires`, `terminal/sequence-unknown-swallow`, `terminal/leader-user-row-names-setting`, `terminal/leader-timeout-deprecated-alias`; renamed `terminal/sequence-timeout-default-waits`, `sequence-timeout-sends-leader`, `sequence-unknown-pass`.
- Consumers: mtm (declares `terminal-leader-timeout`, binds its leader row on `ctrl+a`): move to `sequence-timeout` and `{terminal-leader}`. Any app relying on the 1s expiry: set `sequence-timeout`.

## v0.39.0 (2026-10-28)

### Timeline: inside a card (card-steps follow-ups)

- Fixed: a motion pressed before the next frame was drawn measured from the old window (`Timeline.settle` places it first). After `G`, `k` past an open card taller than the window never moved; on a card closed and opened again by `l`, `j` inside a tall step did not scroll. The gap stories pass and moved to `turns/k-after-g-past-open-card`, `turns/opened-tall-step-j-scrolls`.
- The body instance is parented under the card it walks while the focus is inside (back under the list when it leaves, and while `SetCards`/`Patch` change the rows), so rows on the card's noun answer inside a card and the footer and `?` offer them (R13.6). The focus path reads `<id>/body < CARD < <id>/list < …`. Goldens `timeline/step-marker-{dark,mono}-80` (footer).
- New verb `ochazuke.timeline.row {delta, count}` (available inside a card), bound to `g j`/`g k` on `ochazuke.timeline-body`: move by screen row; the marker covers the row, the step under it is current (R13.7).
- `J`/`K` inside a card keep the step on screen: a scroll that leaves it off screen moves the cursor to the nearest step shown (R13.8); before, the next `j`/`k` scrolled back or stuck.
- Stories `turns/card-rows-inside`, `turns/row-gj-gk`, `turns/scroll-keeps-step` (new).
- Consumers: an app that binds keys on its card noun now sees them answer inside the card (check that a card row does not shadow something the timeline answers further out); mtm's `turn` noun gains its `esc`, `R`, `Y` inside the body.

## v0.38.0 (2026-10-28)

### Select: a new line opens with its text selected, typing replaces it

- `Input.SelectText(start, end)` and `Input.SelectAll()` open a select range in insert mode, like Vim's select mode; `Input.SelectedText()` reads it; the snapshot reports it as `typeOver {start, end}`. The first text typed (`input.insert`) or pasted (`input.paste`) replaces the range and typing continues; `input.delete` (backspace, delete) removes it; a motion (`left`/`right` land on its edges), a mode change (`esc`), an undo or any other edit closes it and keeps the text. Drawn in the visual-selection style (reverse). It is a state of the input, not a new mode (UX-RULES R9.6).
- Outlines: `ochazuke.outline.add` takes `text` (the new line's text, written with the add) and `select` (open it selected, a placeholder that typing replaces); `ochazuke.outline.edit` takes `select`; `Tree.Edit(id, selected)` opens a line from Go (UX-RULES R11.6). Consumers: o-json (its wrapper key's placeholder, lane R6b) binds a row with these args; mtm's outline is unchanged (no default binding sets `select`).
- Tests `select_text_test.go`; stories `tree/select-type-replaces.json`, `tree/select-esc-keeps.json`, `tree/select-move-keeps.json`.

## v0.37.0 (2026-10-28)

### Terminal emulators are engines; tuios.dev/vt is the default

- The terminal emulator behind `cli.PtyStream` is now an engine chosen per terminal, behind one interface, `cli.Emulator` (`Write`, `Read` for the bytes the program reads, `Resize`, `Render`, `Cursor() EmulatorCursor`, `SendKey`, `Paste`, `Close`). Engines are rows, `cli.TerminalEngine{Name, Description, New, Unavailable}`, added with `cli.RegisterTerminalEngine` and listed by `cli.TerminalEngines()`; `cli.NewEmulator(engine, cols, rows)` makes one; `cli.EmulatorFrame(e)` is its screen and cursor as a `TerminalFrame`. Nothing outside an engine's adapter knows which engine runs.
- Engines: `tuios-vt` (new default, `tuios.dev/vt` v0.3.2, pure Go), `x-vt` (`charmbracelet/x/vt` v0.1.0, today's engine, kept to compare), `ghostty` (libghostty-vt through `tuios.dev/vt`; only with `-tags ghostty`, which needs cgo and the Zig toolchain; a default build lists it as unavailable and refuses it with that reason).
- Setting `terminal.engine` (`cli.TerminalEngineSetting`, default `cli.DefaultTerminalEngine`), declared by `cli.EnsureTerminalEngine(r)`, which `cli.AddCommands` calls; its values are the engines the build runs. It applies to terminals opened after the change; an open terminal keeps its engine (switching one live would mean replaying its screen into the new engine). `cli.TerminalEngineOf(r)` reads it.
- `cli.StartPty(cmd, cols, rows)` is unchanged and opens the default engine; `cli.StartPtyWith(cmd, cols, rows, cli.PtyOptions{Engine})` picks one. The Commands page opens each run with the setting's engine.
- With tuios-vt, keys are encoded by ochazuke as tuios does: the kitty keyboard protocol or modifyOtherKeys when the program asks for them, otherwise xterm's legacy encoding (`cli/ptyencode.go`), which now sends modified cursor, Home/End, Insert/Delete, PgUp/PgDn and F-keys (`ctrl+up` is `CSI 1;5A`; x/vt sent nothing for these). Pastes are sanitized (control characters other than tab, CR and LF dropped) before bracketing.
- Behaviour differences, pinned in `TestEngineDifferences`: tuios-vt supports left/right margins (DECSLRM), screen alignment (DECALN) and insert mode (IRM), which x/vt v0.1.0 gets wrong; colours (256, true colour), underline styles, OSC 8, wide and combining characters, scroll regions, the alternate screen, cursor shape and visibility render the same in both.
- Settings: `Setting.Validate` now runs before the enum check, so a setting can say why a value is not offered.
- Tests: the vim-like fixture (`cli/testdata/vimlike.vt`: alternate screen, scroll region scrolled both ways, wide characters, colours, reverse-video status line, cursor moves, shape and visibility, DECCKM, bracketed paste) runs against every engine directly and through a real pty; the pty key, paste and cursor tests run per engine.
- Consumers: mtm (its live screen moves to `cli.NewEmulator`), any app using `cli.AddCommands` or `cli.StartPty` (gets tuios-vt with no change; set `terminal.engine` to `x-vt` for the old one). New dependency: `tuios.dev/vt` v0.3.2 (MIT, Gaurav Gosain; brings `tuios.dev/progstatus` v0.1.0, MIT; `go.mitchellh.com/libghostty` is in the module graph but only compiled with `-tags ghostty`).

## v0.36.0 (2026-10-28)

### Status segments: live items in the header rule

- New: status segments (`segment.go`, `segment_view.go`). A segment is a data row, `Segment{ID, Label, Format, Provider|Command|Verb, Args, Every, Timeout, Tone, Order, Enabled, Path, Description}`, drawn right-aligned in the rule under the header (`Layout.HeaderRule`, `Header.Segments`); it takes no row (UX-RULES R6.11). Sources: a Go provider (`Runtime.SegmentProvider`), a command (only from Go or the user's `segments.json`, never a catalog) or a registered verb. `Runtime.AddSegment` adds rows or changes one by id; `segments.json` (JSONC array, read by `EnsureSegments` from `EnsureConfigSettings`, again on `config.reload`) patches or adds rows, a bad row a notice.
- Refreshes run off the loop as tea.Cmds (not jobs: no jobs.list or history noise), each under its timeout, one per segment at a time, one timer for the earliest next refresh (`SegmentTickMsg`, `Model.SegmentTick`, `Model.SegmentsInFlight`, `SegmentMsg`). Answers are text, JSON strings or `{text, tone}`.
- Settings (UX-RULES R6.12, the opt-out rule): `status.segments` (the whole area; off stops every refresh), `status.segments.<id>` (one segment, default its row's `enabled`), `status.segment-order` (written by `segment.move`).
- Noun `segment` (`ochazuke.segment`): `list`, `show`, `names`, `enable`, `disable`, `toggle`, `move {id} {to}` (first, last, up, down, ±N, a position), `refresh [id]`.
- New package `sysstat`: CPU busy percent, memory used/total, disk free at a path, macOS and Linux, no cgo (macOS CPU through `github.com/ebitengine/purego`, a new dependency of this package only). `sysstat.Register(r, Options{Enabled, DiskPath, Every})` adds providers and rows `cpu`, `ram`, `disk`, off unless `Enabled`. CPU logic adapted from tuios.
- cli: `CatalogDoc.Segments` (`CatalogSegment`) lists a tool's verb-sourced segments; `cli.CatalogSegments(doc, prefix, noun)` gives a host the rows to mount against its proxy verbs.
- o-story: `page.segments` (`SegmentSpec`), event `tick: "segment"`, expect `segments`/`noSegments`; story `segments/header-rule.json`.

### The palette asks for missing arguments with a form

- Enter on a palette line whose verb lacks a required word opens the args form (`Runtime.OpenArgsForm(request, run)`, modal `ArgsFormID` = `args-form`) instead of stopping at `missing NAME`: one field per parameter (`ArgsFields(op)`: required first, optional marked `(optional)`, the description after the name), prefilled from the typed words, the first missing one focused. Nothing is read from the focus (R12.5, new R12.6).
- A key whose binding row leaves a required argument out opens the same form, and the request runs as the key's (history source `key:…`). Behaviour that changed: `Bind` and the bindings files now accept such a row (before: refused with `NAME is required`); with the form off the key fails naming the argument, as the API does.
- Setting `palette.missing-opens-form` (boolean, default on; `MissingOpensFormSetting`). Off restores the `missing NAME (type): description` line for the palette and the plain error for keys. The CLI and the API never open a form.
- Record editors: `RecordSpec.Submit func(Record) Result` makes a form that runs once (Save is never called; `form.run` closes a modal and hands Submit the values; a failure reopens it with them and `! not run: …`; leaving runs nothing), `RecordSpec.Start` names the first field focused; `RecordEditor.Runnable`, `Ran`. A form opened from a prompt starts in normal mode unless its first field is a text field and `Insert` is set.
- New actions `ochazuke.form.run` (available on a Submit form whose every field is valid: availability `form-runnable`) and `ochazuke.form.next-missing {insert}` (available on a Submit form with a field still not valid: `form-missing`; focuses that field, opening its chooser when it has choices). `enter` in form fields and choices is `form.run`, then `form.next-missing`, then its old action, so an editor that writes each change behaves as before. `/` on a choice field is `form.suggest`.
- Noun-typed parameters (`Parameter.Noun`) are choice fields in a record editor, picked from the noun's list or live things (`Runtime.Choices`); they were free text. `form.suggest` also opens on a choice field: the type-to-filter chooser over its choices, no create row, `(none)` at the bottom of an optional one.
- Consumers: nothing breaks. An app that relied on a binding row missing a required argument being refused at load now gets the form at press time.
- Tests `palette_form_test.go`; stories `palette/form-*.json`.

### A plain message can no longer be drawn as an error

- Bug: `ctx.Status` and `r.UI().Status` were the error tone under a name that reads "any status", so apps posted notices through them and users saw `Error: …` for an image paste, a turns refresh, "no starred panes". The name goes, not the messages one by one.
- Deprecated (working for this release, removed in the next): `Ctx.Status`, `UI.Status` (use `Error` for a failure, `Info` for anything else), `UIStatus` (use `UIError`, the same wire value `status`), `InfoSink` (see below), `RecordingSink.LastStatus` (use the new `LastError`).
- `UISink` gains `Info(text)`: every sink shows both tones, and `UIMessage.Apply` no longer hands a plain message to `Status` for a sink without `Info`. A wrapping sink forwards each message with `UIMessage{...}.Apply(next)`. Breaking only for a sink without `Info`; none in the family (mtm's `noticeSink`, the Model, `cli.Shell`, `RecordingSink` all have it).
- Deprecated names live in one file, `deprecated.go`, built only without the `ochazuke_strict` tag. New package `strictcheck`: `strictcheck.Check(t)` runs `go vet -tags ochazuke_strict ./...` from the module root, so any use of a deprecated name fails the app's test at its line (type-checked: a `Status` method of the app's own types is fine). `strictcheck.Tag`.
- Docs: R6.4 (choose the tone by what happened; no fallback; the check), README "Telling the user" and "Deprecated names and the strict check", AGENTS.md.
- Tests `TestOchazukeBuildsStrict`, `TestCheckCatchesADeprecatedCall`, `TestDeprecatedStatusNamesStillWork`; `TestStatusInfoReachesEverySink` now checks a wrapping sink keeps the tones apart.
- Consumers: replace each `.Status(` with `Error` or `Info` by what it says, drop `InfoSink` references, add `func TestNoDeprecatedOchazuke(t *testing.T) { strictcheck.Check(t) }`. Counts of `Ctx.Status`/`UI.Status` calls on 2026-10-28: mtm 84 (swept on branch `notice-kinds`: 73 Error, 11 Info), o-md 6 (two are notices: "no tuios agent panes", "sent N notes to …"), o-gh 3, o-slack 3, o-git 2, o-inventory 2 (its `testdata/errmap` fixture), o-csv, o-json, o-proj, o-mcp, nabe 0.

### Availability-driven dispatch

- An action reports whether it is available now: `Runtime.Available(request, focus, mode)` reads its availability guard (the verb guard `NounBuilder.When` sets; `Runtime.SetAvailability(key, guard)` sets it on an operation registered with `Register`) with the request's arguments in `KeyEvent.Args`, so a call naming its target (`timeline.open {card}`) is judged by that target. The resolver, `bindings list`, the footer, `?` help, the palette and `Execute` ask the same question; `VerbShown` is `Available` with no arguments.
- One key may be bound to several actions in one context. At each level of the focus path, innermost first, the first row on the key, in line order, whose action is available runs; when none at a level is, the key goes on to the next level out. `Runtime.DispatchOutcome(key)` tells the three outcomes: `KeyHandled`, `KeyUnhandled` (no level had an available row), `KeyConsumed` (the answering row runs `NoopAction`, `ochazuke.key.ignore`, the always-available noop a clamping container binds). `Dispatch` still returns a bool.
- A batch of rows (one `Bind` call, one default file, one `bindings.json`, one `bindings load`) may repeat a key: its first row on a table, mode, key and `when` replaces the rows there, its later ones are alternatives after it. Two `Bind` calls on one key still replace.
- Rows with `when` keep working (guards are honoured as before). `bindings check` warns about each guard row of the user's file (a debt) and each merged row that can never answer (an earlier row on its key is available whenever it is: two always-available actions on one key, or the same guard); `bindings check --debts` lists every table's guard rows and the count. `Runtime.UnreachableBindings()`, `Runtime.BindingDebts()`. `CheckDefaultBindings` reports a dead row as an error, replacing the same-key-and-guard clash.
- Bindings files: one row per line, `{"key": "l", "action": "ochazuke.timeline.open", "args": {...}, "hint": ...}` inside `{"context": "...", "bindings": [ ... ]}`, fields in the order key, action, args, then the rest; an unbind is `{"key": "x", "unbind": true}`. `bindings export`, `bindings defaults` and `config export` write it, one group per table. The reader still takes the v0.33 key map and the old array of rows. The embedded default files are converted, rows and order unchanged.
- Timeline: the card's state is its actions' availability and its rows carry no guard. `timeline.open` is available on a closed card, `close` on an open one, `enter` while there is a card, `leave` on a card or inside one (`step`, `yank-step`, `fold-step` as before). `l` is open then enter, `h` close then leave, inside `h` fold-step then leave. The guards `timeline-card-closed`, `timeline-card-open` stay registered for rows that name them; new `timeline-has-card`, `timeline-on-card`.
- Behaviour that changed: `z o` on an open card and `z c` on a closed one are no longer handled by the timeline (the key goes on out; before, a no-op), and the palette no longer offers `timeline.open` on an open card or `close` on a closed one; a CLI `timeline open --card X` on an open card is refused as not applicable (it was a no-op). Every other key, story and test is unchanged.
- Consumers: nothing breaks. An app that binds `l` to `ochazuke.timeline.enter` guarded `timeline-card-open` on its card noun (mtm's `turn`) keeps working (the guard reads the focused card as before). To drop the guard, give `turn.open` an availability of its own and bind `l` to `turn.open` then `ochazuke.timeline.enter`.


### Actions over a visual selection act on every selected row

- A row's own verb run while `V` is on (key, palette or API) now has the whole selection as its subject. Before, list.apply ran the verb once per row with no way to ask once: a verb that opens a chooser (a status, a snooze time, a project to move to) or a confirm of its own opened it for the first row and failed on the second ("already open"), so only one row changed; `Y` copied only the cursor row; the fan-out ran only from a key (runRow), not the palette.
- New `Request.Subjects` (JSON `subjects`): the instances a request acts on when it acts on several, its Target among them. New `Operation.Many` (catalog `many`, `NounBuilder.Many()`): the handler runs once with every subject in the new `Ctx.Subjects`; a verb without it runs once per subject. New `Ctx.SubjectIDs()` (hand the subjects on to the request a chooser's choice runs) and `Ctx.Each(handler)` (run a one-subject body per subject with a narrowed Ctx, one notice for all).
- One notice per run: info `VERB: done on N rows`, or an error `VERB: D done, F failed (...)`; a run fails only when no row took it. A per-row verb that opens a modal stops after that row and says how many it left. A confirm over several subjects reads `{{label}}` as "FIRST and N more".
- The fan-out moved from `runRow` into `execute` (`visualApply`), so it applies from keys, the palette and the API alike; key lookup is unchanged.
- `list.yank` over a visual selection copies every selected row (or the current column's cell of each), one per line (shape `line`), and ends the selection.
- Trees: a run over several rows is one undo step (`u` restores every row, `ctrl+r` redoes them all).
- o-story: a story verb (`page.verbs`) takes `row` (log ` row=ID`), `fail` (row ids it fails on) and `many` (log ` subjects=A,B,C`). Stories `tables/visual-many-subjects`, `tables/visual-partial-failure`, `tree/visual-undo-group`; tests `TestVisualSelection*`, `TestRequestSubjects`, `TestVisualYankCopiesEverySelectedRow`. UX-RULES R5.15.
- Consumers: per-row verbs need nothing. A verb that asks something (a chooser) or should make one change and one notice for all rows: add `.Many()`, open the question once for `ctx.Subjects`, and run the answer as a request with `Subjects: ctx.SubjectIDs()` (or `ctx.Each`).

## v0.35.0 (2026-10-28)

### Timeline: event cards

- `Card.Event`: a card drawn as an event, indented two cells with a dashed border (`┄ ┆`) that stays muted until selected. mtm uses it for background-agent notification cards on the Turns tab.

## v0.34.0 (2026-10-28)

### Default key bindings are embedded data

- The framework's default rows moved out of Go `Bind` chains into embedded files in the grouped format, the only place a default key is written: `bindings/*.json` (app, chooser, completion, confirm, detail, editor, focus, form, help, history, list, list-modes, macros, modal, palette, panes, picker, registers, settings-editor, table, terminal, timeline, tree, yank-history), `markdown/bindings.json`, `grid/bindings.json`, `cli/bindings.json`: 478 rows. `editor_bindings.json` and `registers_bindings.json` are gone, with their spec format (`maps`/`expand`, `pending`, `rows`); `Runtime.LoadBindingSpec` is removed (no consumer used it): use `LoadDefaultBindings`.
- New `Runtime.LoadDefaultBindings(source, data)`, `MustLoadDefaultBindings`, `LoadDefaultBindingsFS(fsys, patterns...)`: an application's embedded defaults go into the default layer, below the user's `bindings.json`, exactly where Go rows would.
