An error in an embedded file fails the load (unknown operation, argument, source, guard, mode, instance), unlike a user file's skipped rows.
- New `Runtime.CheckDefaultBindings(source, data)`, the rule check for tests: every name exists, `mode.enter` goes into a pending mode, no two rows of a table clash.
- New verb `ochazuke.bindings.defaults` (`BIN bindings defaults`) and `Runtime.DefaultBindings` (`DefaultBinding{Binding, Source}`): the default layer, the user's rows left out, each group tagged with its source (the embedded file, `go`, `generated`). New result type `Verbatim`, which the CLI prints as text.
- The grouped format gains two group fields: `"fallback": true` (keep the keys the table bound before the file loaded: the app noun's rows `NewModel` used to add only when the application had not bound the key) and `"source"` (written by `bindings defaults`, ignored on read). `BindingGroup.Fallback`, `BindingGroup.Source`.
- Rows from a file keep the types their operation declares (a whole number for an integer parameter is an `int`); `mode.enter`'s `request` is the decoded object, which it reads as before. The terminal leader setting moves the leader rows and keeps their source.
- Unchanged behaviour: every stored table of NewRuntime, NewModel (with picker, markdown, grid, the leader moved to ctrl+b), examples/full-project and all 313 o-story apps is row for row the same before and after; only the leader row's nested request prints its fields in sorted order.
- Tests `TestFrameworkBindingFilesPassTheRuleCheck`, `TestFrameworkBindingFilesDoNotClash`, `TestEveryFrameworkBindingFileIsLoaded`, `TestLoadDefaultBindingsFailsLoudly`, `TestLoadDefaultBindingsMatchesTheGoBuilder`, `TestBindingsDefaultsPrintsTheDefaultLayer`, `TestTerminalLeaderKeepsTheRowsSource`, `TestCommandBindingsPassTheRuleCheck`, `TestBindingsDefaultsPrintsAFile`, `TestGridBindingsPassTheRuleCheck`, `TestMarkdownBindingsPassTheRuleCheck`.
- Consumers: nothing breaks. An app moves its own defaults by embedding its files and calling `LoadDefaultBindingsFS` where its `Bind` calls were.

## v0.33.0 (2026-10-28)

### Bindings files are grouped by context; config files are JSONC

- Config files are JSONC: `bindings.json`, `actions.json`, `settings.json`, `themes.json`, `bindings load` and the embedded default specs accept `//` and `/* */` comments and trailing commas (`github.com/tailscale/hujson`, new dependency). Files keep their `.json` names; errors name the line and column of the file as written. Plain JSON without a `/` skips the parse (100 KB: 0.12 ms against 2.5 ms). New `StandardizeJSONC`, `DecodeJSONC`.
- `settings set` edits an existing `settings.json` in place through the hujson AST: the user's comments, layout and member order stay.
- New grouped bindings format: `{"modes": {...}, "bindings": [{"context": "noun/mode", "extends": "...", "bindings": {"key": "noun.verb" | {action, args, hint, when, ...} | null | [...]}}, {"instance": "ID", "mode": "MODE", "bindings": {...}}]}`. `config export`'s `bindings.reference.json`, the new verb `bindings export PATH [--force]` and the `cmd/ochazuke` keybinding tool write it (one header comment, groups by noun then mode, then instances, keys one per line in exact table order: a key that returns after other keys starts another group for the same context, an alias-spelled row is written as spelled). New `ParseBindings`, `FormatBindings`, `Runtime.BindingsDoc`, `GroupBindings`, `BindingsDoc`, `BindingGroup`, `ModeSpec`, `Config.BindingsFormat`, `BindingsGrouped`/`BindingsRows`/`BindingsHeader`.
- `bindings.json` and `bindings load` read the grouped format and the old array of rows (the top level tells them apart), so existing files keep working and nothing is rewritten. `bindings check` names the format read on the status line. A bad group or key is skipped and reported (`bindings.json: pane/normal q: ...`). A user file's `modes` or `extends` that differ from the application's are reported and ignored.
- `settings.reference.json` and `actions.example.json` are written one row per line (`FormatRows`, `FormatRowsJSON`). `bindings list --json` still answers an array of rows, now with `keys`/`pattern` before `request` (the `Binding` field order).
- The keybinding tool imports both its old `{keys, action}` array and the grouped file.
- Tests `TestFormatBindingsGolden`, `TestFormatBindingsRoundTrip`, `TestParseBindingsReadsTheRowsFormat`, `TestBindingsRowsToGroupedSameEffectiveRows`, `TestBindingsFileJSONC`, `TestBindingsFileMalformedPosition`, `TestBindingsCheckReportsFormat`, `TestBindingsFileModesMustAgree`, `TestBindingsFileBadKeySkipped`, `TestBindingsExportVerbLoadsBack`, `TestSettingsWriteKeepsComments`, `TestFormatRowsKeepsStructOrder`, `TestConfigExportWritesRowFiles`, `TestBindingExportGroupedFormat`; `TestBindingsExportRoundTripsEveryTable` (examples/full-project) and `TestBindingsExportRoundTripsEveryStoryApp` (every o-story app: exported, read back and loaded into a fresh build, the same ordered rows per table); benchmark `BenchmarkStandardizeJSONC`.
- Consumers (mtm, nabe, o-* tools) pick it up with the release; nothing to change. A user's `bindings.json` in rows format keeps loading.

## v0.32.0 (2026-10-28)

### The half-page rule: one motion press scrolls at most half a view (R3.14)

- A motion (`j`/`k`, a step inside a card, `}`/`{` in a Markdown document) whose target would scroll the view more than half its height scrolls half toward it, and the cursor moves only once the target shows. Page keys (`ctrl+d`/`ctrl+u`, `pgdown`/`pgup`, `J`/`K`), `G`/`gg` and searches keep their sizes. One helper: `MotionScroll(want, page)` and `SpanInView(from, to, page)` (motion_scroll.go).
- Card timelines, measured in a 15-line window over cards of 5, 30, 12, 40 and 3 lines: card-to-card `j` scrolled 9, 34, 16 and 36 lines per press, now 7 at most; step-to-step `j` scrolled up to 15 and 16 lines (16 onto the next card), now 7 at most. A tall card or step is read half a window per press (`List` routes `list.move` to a component's own motion; the timeline's `cardMotion`).
- Card timelines reach their end before leaving: `j` on the last card, or on its last step, scrolls until the card's end shows, then spills (R3.4) or, `NoSpill`, stays with the focus where it was. Before, `j` on the last step of a tall last card spilled with the card's end still off screen. `k` on the first card scrolls to its top first.
- `markdown.Doc`: `}`/`{` through a block taller than the window page half a window per press, the line cursor held inside the window until the next block shows. `]]`/`[[` and `] KIND` stay jumps.
- Stories `timeline/card-half-page`, `timeline/card-end-clamp`, `timeline/step-end-clamp`, `timeline/card-top-clamp`, `timeline/spill-after-end`, `markdown/motion-long-block` (new), `timeline/step-tall` (now exactly half a window per press); tests `TestTimelineHalfPageRule`, `TestTimelineNoSpillScrollsToTheEnd`.

### `h` inside a card folds the open section

- Inside a card, `h` (and `left`) on a step in an open foldable section (a titled step with a body: a tool call, thinking, an answer, any of its paragraphs) folds it and puts the cursor on its title: new verb `timeline.fold-step`, guarded row `timeline-step-open` before the unguarded `timeline.leave`. On a folded or plain step `h` goes back to the card as before; `l` on a folded step opens it as before. The footer inside an open section reads `h timeline.fold-step`.
- Story `timeline/step-fold` (new), goldens `timeline/step-marker-{dark,mono}-80` (footer); test `TestTimelineHFoldsAnOpenStep`; UX-RULES R13.1's table.
- Consumers: mtm (Turns) and any app with a card timeline get both with the release; an app that wants `j` never to leave its timeline marks it `NoSpill()`.

## v0.31.0 (2026-10-28)

### A noun's row source prefers its own rows

- `cli.CatalogDoc.RowSource` took the first verb (sorted) declaring rows, so `slack-channel.history` (rows of `slack-message`) beat `list` as the channel noun's row source. The rule is now, each step in sorted order: `list` when it declares the noun's own rows; another verb declaring the noun's own rows; a `list` declaring none (the noun's own rows); a verb declaring another noun's rows. A catalog page's list with no `source`, `ListColumns` and the hosts' mounts (mtm, nabe) follow it.
- Checked against every installed tool's catalog (`BIN catalog`: mtm, nabe, o-ai, o-animation, o-csv, o-dotfiles, o-gh, o-git, o-inventory, o-json, o-mcp, o-md, o-proj, o-qbt, o-tui, and o-slack from source): every row source is the same except the ones the bug picked: `o-gh` `pr` (`ignored` → `list`), `o-mcp` `mcp-server` and `mcp-tool` (`calls`, rows of `mcp-call` → `list`), `o-slack` `slack-message` (`dms` → `list`) and, with its workaround removed, `slack-channel` (`history` → `list`).
- No explicit row-source declaration on the noun: no catalog needs one the rule does not give.
- Tests `TestCatalogRowSourcePrefersTheNounsOwnRows`; story `catalog/row-source-prefers-list` (new).
- Consumers: o-slack can declare `.Rows(messageNoun)` on `slack-channel.history` again once it takes this release.

### The status line: an error tone and a plain tone

- `Ctx.Status` was drawn as `Error: …` in the error style while the docs called it a plain status line, and the CLI printed it as `status:`. The status line is now one slot with two tones: `ctx.Error(text)` (an error: `Error: TEXT` in the error style in place of the hints) and `ctx.Info(text)` (a plain message, before the hints cut to half the width); either replaces the other and the next key clears both. `ctx.Status` keeps its meaning as the error tone (the older spelling of `Error`), so every existing caller that reports a failure stays an error. `Notify` stays the notice that persists. Same on `r.UI()` (`Error`, `Info`, `Status`).
- Sinks: `UISink` is unchanged (`Status` is the error tone); a sink that also implements the new `InfoSink` (`Info(text)`) shows the plain tone, one without it gets it through `Status` (as before). `Model.Info` is the plain tone beside `Model.Status`. `UIInfo` (`"info"`) is a new `UIMessage` kind; `UIStatus` keeps `"status"` on the wire. `UIMessage.Line`. `RecordingSink.Info`, `LastInfo` (`LastStatus` is the error tone).
- CLI: `cli.Shell` and `ochazuke NOUN VERB` print the error tone as `error: TEXT` (was `status: TEXT`) and the plain tone as `status: TEXT`.
- Framework callers: `list.yank`'s "copied the row (N characters)" is `Info` now (it read `Error: copied …`); `markdown` writes `Error`.
- Rules: R6.4 (rewritten). Stories `chrome/status-info-plain-80`, `chrome/status-error-clears-80`, goldens `chrome/status-info-80`, `chrome/status-error-80` (new); tests `TestStatusLineTones`, `TestStatusInfoReachesEverySink`, `TestShellPrintsNoticesAndStatusOnStderr` (extended).
- Consumers (nothing breaks; plain messages read `Error:` until moved): move plain messages to `ctx.Info`: o-csv (`wrote … rows`, `rows N–M of T`, `copied …` x2), o-json (`copied …` x2), o-git (`changed`'s `say`), o-md (`sent N notes`), mtm (`queued …` x2 in prompt_box, `attached …` in prompt_attach). mtm's `noticeSink` forwards `Info` (implement `InfoSink`), or the plain tone still reads `Error:` there. Every `err.Error()` caller can stay on `Status` or be renamed `Error`.

### Focus never rests on what the last frame did not draw

- After the Model draws the current page, a focus whose stop that frame did not place (a tab switched away from it, a collapsed panel) moves to its nearest ancestor below the page that was drawn (a list nested under a row: the row), else to the page's first stop in drawing order (its remembered child), else to the page; the page is drawn again before the footer, so the hints and the next key are what is on screen (`Runtime.keepFocusShown`, `Model.render`). A list row counts as drawn when its list is, a grid cell when its grid is, a card's body when its timeline is. It is a plain focus change: the mode rule applies (a hidden prompt in insert leaves to normal) and the stop left remembers its child, so `}` goes back once it is drawn again. It waits while a modal is open, before the page's first frame (the CLI), after a frame that placed no stop, and never judges a focus on no stop (the page, a panel).
- Rules: `docs/UX-RULES.md` R3.13 (new). Stories `focus/hidden-stop-moves-focus`, `focus/hidden-input-leaves-insert`, `focus/hidden-timeline-card` (new); tests `TestFocusHidden*`. o-story: `demo.hide {id}` stops drawing a list, tree, input or the timeline, or draws it again.
- Consumers: an app that moved the focus off a panel it stopped drawing (mtm's `leaveHiddenTurns`) can drop that code. An app whose own focusable view never calls `Runtime.Placed` while other stops on the page do now loses the focus off it after a frame (jumps already skipped such a stop); call `Placed(id)` from the view.

## v0.30.0 (2026-10-28)

### Timeline: step inside a card

- Inside a card is a focus place, not a mode (`timeline_steps.go`). `l` on a closed card opens it (as before); `l` on an open card steps inside onto its first step, the focus on the timeline's body instance (`<id>/body`, noun `ochazuke.timeline-body`, under the card list); `h` inside goes back to the card (still open), `h` on an open card closes it, `h` on a closed card goes to the timeline's parent stop (`ParentStop(id)` option, `Timeline.SetParentStop`; without one it stays). `enter`/`space` still toggle; closing the card from inside puts the focus on the card.
- Inside: `j`/`k` move by step (counts work, a count stops at the edge); a step taller than the window moves half a window per key first; past the last step `j` spills to the next card (open or closed), past the last card to the next stop; past the first step `k` goes back to the card; `l` opens the step's folded section; `y` yanks the step's text through the registers; `esc` leaves like `h`. The current step is marked `▌` (accent, bold) in the left padding of its lines, never reversed; the window scrolls to keep it shown.
- API: `Card.Steps []CardStep{ID, Title, Summary, Role, Folded, Header, Paragraphs, Body}` (a `Header` step is the card's header; a titled step with a body folds like a section and counts in `z N` and `timeline.fold`; `Paragraphs` splits a body into a step per paragraph). Without Steps a card steps through its Body's paragraphs (blank-line separated, a fenced code block one paragraph) and then each section as one step, and draws exactly as before. `TimelineBodyNoun`, `Timeline.InBody`, `Step`, `StepText`, `BodyID`.
- Verbs on `ochazuke.timeline`: `enter {card}`, `leave`, `step {delta, count}` (a motion), `yank-step {register}`; `open`, `close`, `toggle` stay. Guards `timeline-card-closed`, `timeline-card-open`, `timeline-in-body`. The `l`/`right` and `h`/`left` rows on `ochazuke.timeline` are now guarded by place (closed: open and leave; open: enter and close), so `?` help and the footer show the row that answers. Body rows on `ochazuke.timeline-body`: `j`/`down`/`k`/`up` step, `l`/`right` enter, `h`/`left`/`esc` leave, `y` yank-step.
- `List` gains an unexported part (an instance that holds the list's focus as a row does); `List.syncFocus` keeps it while the selected row stays. o-story: `CardSpec.Steps` (`StepSpec`) and `Expect.Register` (the unnamed register's text).
- Rules: `docs/UX-RULES.md` R13 (new). Stories `timeline/step-in`, `step-out`, `step-spill`, `step-back`, `step-tall`, `step-yank`, `step-explicit`, `step-enter-toggles`, `step-marker-{dark,mono}-80` (goldens). Goldens `timeline/open-close-120` and `follow-tail-120` updated (the footer reads `l timeline.enter` on an open card).
- Consumers: an app that binds its own `l` on its card noun (mtm's `turn.open`) shadows `timeline.enter`; bind `l` to `ochazuke.timeline.enter` guarded `timeline-card-open` on that noun to step in. Existing `Card` users compile unchanged.

## v0.29.0 (2026-10-28)

### Help: whole sequences, a row for every prefix, a pending mode's keys

- `?` help's key column is the whole sequence (`ctrl+a v`, `g c x`); the v0.27.0 prefix | key split is gone (R6.9 keeps its order, R6.10 is new).
- Every prefix has a row of its own, read the same whatever makes it one: a row entering a pending mode (`ctrl+a` → `leader_pending`: `mode.enter`, "enters leader_pending: waits for the next key (N keys)", and what it does alone after the timeout), or the first key of sequences with nothing bound on it alone (a made row, no operation, "waits for the next key (N keys)"). A prefix bound to an operation of its own keeps it. Under the prefix's row come its sequences, each once, a longer prefix's row under the one it extends.
- A pending mode's rows are listed under the key that enters it as whole sequences: over a live terminal `?` shows `ctrl+a`, then `ctrl+a d`, `ctrl+a ctrl+a` and every app row bound in `leader_pending`, following the `terminal-leader` setting. Hidden rows of the mode (the leader's `any` and `paste`) stay out; a mode row that enters a mode expands once more, then shows `mode.enter`. Rows with their own `Help` (registers, macros, counts) and operators stay one row.
- `HelpRow` gains `Group` (a prefix row), `Under` (the prefix row it is listed under), `Enters` (the pending mode the row's key enters) and `Steps []HelpStep{Key, Operation, Mode, Description}` (a sequence key by key). Additive (`omitempty`). `BindingHelp` and `PlaceHelp` return the made prefix rows and the mode rows; `Key` is the whole sequence as before.
- The help (here and pick) is a tree (R11.1): a prefix row holds its sequences and starts open; a sequence row holds its steps, one key per line (`ctrl+a` "enters leader_pending: waits for the next key", then `v` and what it runs), and starts closed. `l`/`h` (and the arrows) open and close, `enter` toggles (a hidden row on the `binding` noun, guard `help-tree`, so the footer is unchanged), `j`/`k` move across everything shown, `z l`/`z h` scroll the columns. The filter reads a row with its steps and its sequences. The table view stays a plain table (h/l scroll its modes). The help's tree is `__bindings-help` and its list keeps the id `__bindings-help/list`. `TreeNode` gains `Section` (a label line above a run of rows, as `ListItem.Section`).
- o-story: a page takes `pending` (pending modes defined before its `bind` rows).
- Rules: R6.9 (reworded), R6.10 (new). Stories `help/leader-sequences`, `help/leader-rebound`, `help/mode-cycle`, `help/sequence-steps`, `help/filter-finds-steps` (new), `help/prefix-columns-{80,120}` (rewritten), `tables/modal-clamps` (now on the help's table view, whose h/l still scroll). Goldens regenerated for the one-column key and the prefix rows: `testdata/help-80.golden` (`d` reads "waits for the next key (2 keys)" above `d d` and `d esc`; the list's `g esc` moves under movement beside `g g`), `help/pick-place.golden`, `help/prefix-columns-{80,120}.golden`, `tables/modal-clamps.golden` (the table view), `help/pick-view.golden` (the tree's fold markers).
- Consumers pick it up on the next tag. mtm: `?` over a live pane lists the leader and every leader key with its description; its `terminal.keys` modal stays.

## v0.28.0 (2026-10-28)

### Actions: a flat table with aliases

- The action table is flat: a key is a name looked up whole, and the action carries its own noun and verb, so a verb may hold a dot again (`dirs.base.add` is the verb `base.add` of `dirs`). `Register` no longer panics on a dotted verb; two declarations that spell the same name (`tool` + `part.run` beside `tool.part` + `run`) are still a duplicate. `CanonicalKey` looks a name up whole before reading its dots, `Runtime.Operation`, `VerbGuard` and `NounBuilder.When` read a registered key's parts from its action. This is what fixes nabe's startup panic (`verb "base.add" of mtm-dirs holds a dot`) on a registry holding mtm's old catalog, with no nabe change.
- Action aliases (`action_names.go`): `ActionAlias{Alias, Action, Noun, Verb, Since, RemoveAfter, Note, Source}`; `Runtime.AddActionAlias`, `AliasAction`; `NounBuilder.Alias(verbs...)` (same noun, permanent), `AliasOn(noun, verb)` (one operation under both nouns' names: `project.associate` for `pane.associate`), `Renamed(old, since, removeAfter)` (a deprecated old name); lookups `ResolveAction`, `ActionAlias`, `ActionAliases`, `AliasesOf`, `ActionAliasProblems`, exported `CanonicalRequest`. An alias may not take the name of an action or of another alias; an action registered after an alias of its name takes the name and the drop is recorded, never a panic.
- Running through an alias runs the canonical action: `Request.Via` (new, `omitempty`) keeps the alias a request was spelled by (history shows it); the handler reads `Ctx.Invoked` (new field) or `Runtime.Invoked()`: `Invocation{Name, Noun, Verb, Alias}`, `Noun` being the alias's noun (the subject side). A deprecated alias's first use in a runtime posts one notice: `OLD is an old name of NEW (renamed in SINCE; removed after AFTER)`.
- Listed once, under the canonical name: `Operation.Aliases` (filled by `Catalog`/`operations.list`), `cli.CatalogVerb.Aliases` (`ActionAlias` objects), `HelpRow.Aliases` with `· also: NAME` after the description in `?` help, `Binding.Aliases` on `bindings list` rows (a stored row never holds them), the palette's `PaletteEntry.Aliases` and `· also NAME` labels (a typed alias narrows to its canonical verb and runs it; aliases are never completions of their own), oden aliases on the CLI verb command (`show, display`), and a command `alias of X` in another noun's group for a cross-noun alias. `ochazuke.actions.aliases` lists every alias.
- `actions.json` alias rows: `{"alias": "pane.k", "action": "pane.kill", "note": "..."}` (`AliasRow`, `UserAliases`). Installed after the file's actions; a reload drops the previous ones. A row that would shadow an action or another alias, names an unknown action or noun, repeats an earlier row or has an unknown field is skipped and reported (notice, CLI `warning:`, `actions reload`). New `ochazuke.actions.check` (`CheckUserActions`) reports what a load would skip without installing; `bindings check` now includes those lines and says what a `bindings.json` row spelled by an alias resolves to (with the new name for an old one).
- Writes are never filled silently: a request with a required parameter missing fails naming it (API, CLI) and the palette asks (`missing NAME`); pinned by `TestWritesAreNeverSilentlyFilled` and `TestCLIAliasesRunTheCanonicalVerb`.
- Old names (Keep actions: an old name is kept as a deprecated alias for one release after its rename, then removed by decision): the framework renamed no verb from v0.1.0 to v0.27.0 (the catalogs of every tag were compared), so it registers none. The family renames found are mtm's, registered in mtm with `Renamed` (mtm branch `a1-actions`): `dirs base.add` → `add-base`, `dirs base.remove` → `remove-base`, `project delete.confirm` and `task delete.confirm` → `confirm-delete`, `project directory.edit` → `edit-directory`, `project directory.remove` → `remove-directory` (renamed in mtm 5ed9521, 2026-10-27, when ochazuke v0.18.0 refused dotted verbs; kept through the next mtm release).
- Rules: `docs/UX-RULES.md` R12 (new). Stories `actions/alias-in-palette`, `actions/help-lists-alias-once`, `actions/old-name-binding`, `actions/user-alias`, `actions/alias-clash-reported`. o-story: a story takes `config` (user config files by name, loaded after the pages) and a verb takes `aliases`; a run through an alias logs ` via=ALIAS`.
- Compatibility: additive. `Runtime` gains one unexported field. Code that indexes `Runtime.Operations` by an alias's name gets nothing (use `Runtime.Operation`); a consumer reading `CatalogVerb` ignores `aliases`. Hosts mounting catalogs (nabe, mtm) can register `aliases` as aliases of the mounted verb in a later lane; until then the canonical names work.

## v0.27.0 (2026-10-28)

### Help: key prefixes in their own column

- `?` help splits each key sequence into a prefix and its final key and draws them as two columns before the operation: `z l` is `z` | `l`, `ctrl+a v` is `ctrl+a` | `v`, a three-key `g c x` is `g c` | `x`, a single key has an empty prefix. Inside each section (the sections and their order are unchanged) the single keys come first in the order they answer, then each prefix's rows together, sorted by key (characters case folded, lower case first, named keys such as the generated `esc` cancel last); the prefixes keep the order they first answer, a longer one right after the prefix it extends (`g`, then `g c`). The prefix is drawn on its group's first row and left blank below it, and again on the first row of a scrolled window. The prefix column has no title (the header's `key` names both) and is left out when no row has a sequence, so an 80-column help keeps its arguments column. The pick view gets the same; the table view (a row per key, a column per mode) is unchanged.
- `Column.Group` (`"group"` in a catalog's columns): a table cell that repeats the cell above it in the same section is drawn blank (with every Group column left of it), the first row a frame draws always shows it; the rows keep their cells and `TableText` does not blank. A Group pin drops with the pin after it when the pins do not fit (R5.13).
- `HelpRow` gains `Prefix`, `Final` (the split key), `Request` (the `noun.verb` the row runs, also when `Operation` shows the row's Hint) and `Hint`. All are additive (`omitempty`); `Key` and `Operation` are unchanged, and `BindingHelp` keeps resolution order (only the modal reorders). `SplitKeys(keys) (prefix, final)` is exported.
- Rules: `docs/UX-RULES.md` R6.9 (new), R6.7, R5.13. Stories `help/prefix-columns-{80,120}` (new goldens). Goldens regenerated: `testdata/help-80.golden` (prefix and key columns; `d d` / `d esc` read `d` | `d`, `esc`; `g esc` reads `g` | `esc`), `help/pick-place.golden` (the same columns in the pick view), `tables/modal-clamps.golden` (the 44-column help shows the prefix and key pins together). Tests `TestSplitKeys`, `TestHelpOrderGroupsPrefixes`, `TestGroupColumnShowsARepeatedCellOnce`, `TestGroupPinDropsWithThePinAfterIt`.
- Consumers (mtm, nabe, the o-* tools) pick it up on the next tag with no change. An app matching help rows by request (mtm's `keyForRequest`) can read `HelpRow.Request` and `Args` in place of re-walking `ListBindings`. An app drawing its own key table (mtm's leader modal) can use `Column.Group` for the same once-per-group prefix.

## v0.26.0 (2026-10-28)

### Tones: per-cell colour through the theme

- A cell, or a run of runes inside one, can carry a named tone: `ListItem.Tones []ToneSpan` (`ToneSpan{Cell, Tone, From, To}`, rune offsets into the cell's drawn text, `To` 0 the cell's end; `CellTone(cell, tone)`, `SpanTone(cell, from, to, tone)`). In a list without columns cell 0 is the label. The text never changes, so widths, cuts, the filter, yanks and `--format` read the cells as before.
- Built-in tones, drawn by every built-in theme (`DefaultTones()`; constants `ToneWorking` …): `state.working` (blue), `state.needs-input` (amber, bold), `state.done` (green), `state.errored` (the scheme's `error`, bold), `state.idle` (`muted`), `state.exited` (`muted`, dim), `marker.star` (yellow, bold), `marker.queued` (`accent`). Mono and `NO_COLOR` draw the attributes alone. An unknown tone draws plain.
- `themes.json`: a scheme takes `"tones"`, an object of `ToneStyle` by name: `{"fg": colour or role, "bold": true, "dim": true}`, or a colour or role alone (`"#5FAFD7"`, `"error"`); a role (`text`, `muted`, `accent`, `border`, `surface`, `error`) follows the scheme. A scheme's tones are laid over the built-in ones, each replacing the tone of its name whole. An entry named after a built-in theme now holds tones alone (`{"dark": {"tones": {...}}}`) and overrides or adds to that theme's (`adaptive`'s reach `dark` and `light` too); anything else under a built-in's name is still skipped with a notice. Such an entry is not a theme choice (`SchemeSet.Names`, `themes list` and the `theme` setting leave it out). A bad tone skips its scheme with a notice. `themes.example.json` shows every built-in tone.
- Selection: the selected row stays one reverse-video bar; inside it a tone draws its bold alone, never its colour or dim, and a cell-mode table's current cell keeps its underline. A page list without the focus fades its tones with it (`dim-unfocused`).
- Trees: `TreeNode.Tones`; cell 0's tones stay on the label, after the indent and the fold marker.
- Catalog and CLI: `cli.Row.Tones` (`"tones"` on a row line, optional), read by `DecodeRows`, the row reader and state answers, and passed to the mounted list's rows, so an extension colours its table with no Go code. A host that does not know the field draws the cells plain.
- Go: `Scheme.Tones`, `ToneStyle`, `Styles.Tone(name)` (a tone's `lipgloss.Style` for an application's own lines). `Scheme` holds a map now, so it is no longer comparable with `==`; `Appearance` still is.
- Rules: `docs/UX-RULES.md` R5.14. Stories `tables/tones-{table,selected-row,mono}`, `tree/tones-tree-row`, `chrome/tones-{themes-override,user-scheme}`, `catalog/tones-rows`. o-story: list items take `tones`, and an expect takes `attrs` (text → `bold`, `faint`, `reverse`, `underline`, `fg`, each `-name` for absent). No golden changed.
- Consumers (mtm, the o-* tools with tables) pick it up on the next tag: nothing changes until a row carries tones; mtm can move its pane-state colours and the `W` marker to `state.*`/`marker.*` tones.

### Fixed: a tree with columns keeps its h/l

- v0.25.0's scroll-then-focus rows also answered for a tree's own list, which sits inside the tree and answers before it: on a tree with columns that fits (mtm's Panes page), `l`/`right` on an open node ran `focus.next` and left the tree instead of stepping onto its first child, and `right` on a leaf left it too; on a tree that hides a column `h`/`l` scrolled instead of collapsing and expanding.
- Rule: the h/l scroll, spill and clamp rows (`table-column-*`, `table-scrolls-*`, `table-spills-*`, the clamps) never answer for a tree's list; the tree's `outline.expand`/`outline.collapse` do. `z l`/`z h` (and `$`, `^`, `0`) still scroll a tree's columns. The h/l scroll clamp row's guard is now `table-scrolls-clamp`; `table-scrolls` stays the `z l`/`z h` row guard and `scroll-columns`' verb guard.
- o-story: a page's trees take `columns` (nodes bring `cells`). Stories `tree/columns-l-expands`, `tree/columns-hidden-l-expands`; test `TestTreeHLWinOverTableScroll`. Rules: `docs/UX-RULES.md` R5.11, R11.1.
- Consumers: mtm's Panes tree gets `l` expand/step-in back on the next tag, with no change on its side.

## v0.25.0 (2026-10-28)

### Tables: h/l scroll, then move the focus; edge markers; pinned columns

- Scroll, then move the focus: the horizontal twin of the `j`/`k` spill. On a row-select table `l` scrolls the view one column right (`list.scroll-columns {delta: 1}`, counts work) until the last column shows, then moves the focus to the next stop (`focus.next`); `h` scrolls left until offset 0, then moves it to the previous stop (`focus.previous`). On a cell-mode table `h` on the first column and `l` on the last move the focus; in between they move the column as before. The arrows do the same. A count never spills (it stops at the edge, as `3j` stops at the last row). In a modal, on a `NoSpill` list and at the page's first or last stop the keys clamp. A row noun's own `h`/`l` (a setting's cycle, an app's open) still answers first. (This release also said a tree's expand and collapse still answer first; they did not on a tree with columns, whose list answered before the tree: see the fix under Unreleased.) Every table, cell-mode or not, takes part: a table that fits is at both edges, so `l` on it moves the focus where nothing inner binds `l` (it did nothing before).
- Done with guarded rows, no new verb: per key, rows of `list.column` (guard `table-column-right`/`-left`), `list.scroll-columns` (`table-scrolls-right`/`-left`), `focus.next`/`focus.previous` (`table-spills-right`/`-left`), then the clamped `list.column` (`table-cells`) and `list.scroll-columns` (`table-scrolls`); the first whose guard holds answers. `table-scrolls` is kept for `z l`/`z h`.
- Edge markers: a table with a header that hides a column reserves 8 cells (two markers and their gaps; more with ten or more scrolling columns) and draws `‹N` after its pinned columns (N scrolling columns hidden before the view) and `N›` at its right edge (hidden after it), each only when N > 0, so the user sees whether `h`/`l` will scroll or move on. A table that fits reserves nothing. A table without a header draws none.
- Pinned columns: any columns can be pinned; they draw first, in declared order, and never scroll. The default is the columns declared `Column.Pinned` (`pinned` in a catalog's columns), else the first column, which is the old frozen first column. Verbs on the list noun (verb guard `list-table`): `pin-column {column}`, `unpin-column {column}`, `toggle-pin-column {column}` (`z p` on a cell-mode table, the current column) and `unpin-columns`; `column` is a title or a 1-based number in declaration order, and without it a row-select table opens the column chooser. `reset-columns` (`z =`) also puts the default pins back, and its verb now answers on any table (the palette resets a row-select table's pins; `z =` stays a cell-mode key). Pins are remembered per table id in `tables.json` (`TableState.Pins`; a row-select table now writes its pins too, nothing else). When the pins leave no room for a scrolling column the view drops pins from the right (the saved pins stay). On a cell-mode table `h`/`l`, `0`/`$` and `N|` move in drawing order (pins first).
- `z l` from the start skips an offset 1 that draws what offset 0 draws, and offset 1 equal to 0 is drawn as 0, so no key press scrolls without a visible change (`2 z l` from the start lands one column further than before).
- Go: `Column.Pinned`, `List.PinnedColumns`, `SetPinnedColumns`, `PinColumn`, `UnpinColumn`, `UnpinColumns`, `ResetPins`, `TableState.Pins`. Unscrolled layout: scrolling columns drop by `Priority` down to one before any pin, then pins from the right down to the first; a first column with a larger `Priority` than the others no longer drops before them.
- Resolution fixes found on the way: a key-sequence prefix waits only for sequences whose row guard holds too (`z` on a row-select table that fits still waits for nothing, with the cell-only `z =`/`z p` rows guarded), and a row whose own guard is false no longer shadows an outer row's hint on the same key (the timeline's `l timeline.open` hint is back beside the list rows).
- Rules: `docs/UX-RULES.md` R5.11 (scroll, then move the focus), R5.12 (edge markers), R5.13 (pins); R5.9 and R5.10 updated. Stories `tables/scroll-then-focus-{right,left}`, `tables/cell-edge-focus`, `tables/nospill-clamps`, `tables/modal-clamps`, `tables/row-noun-l-wins`, `tables/edge-markers{,-start,-fit}`, `tables/pin-{cell,row-palette,declared,narrow}`, `tables/unpin-columns`. Existing table stories at 44 columns (`column-motions`, `row-select`, `cell-motion`, `column-count`) are 52 wide so the markers' 8 cells leave the same columns; `tables/row-select` now expects `l`/`h` to scroll; goldens with a hiding table (`tables/narrow`, `tables/column-motions`, `help/table-view-*`, `help/pick-place`, the o-story index at 40 columns) show the markers or the new list rows.
- Consumers (mtm, nabe, the o-* tools) pick it up when they bump ochazuke: every table whose columns do not fit shows markers and one column fewer at the same width; `l`/`h` on a table row whose noun does not bind them now scroll and then move the focus; catalog tables and the Settings page included (the groups rail's `h` back row is an instance row and still wins).

## v0.24.0 (2026-10-28)

The palette names the parameter a word fills: a header with its name, type, required/optional, enum size, source or noun, its description and what is still to fill, in place of a bare "choices".

### Palette: a parameter's choices name the parameter

- The palette's bare `choices` heading is now the parameter the next word fills, on one line: its name (accent), then muted what it is: its type, `required` or `optional`, `one of N` for an enum, `from NOUN.VERB` for a choices source or `a NOUN` for a noun-typed parameter, `or any text` for a `Free` one, then ` — ` its `Description` and `· next: …` the parameters still unfilled after it. `: bindings list ` reads `all · boolean · optional — Return every stored binding row, …` over `true` / `false`.
- The header follows the word being typed: after `open-file main.go ` it moves from `path` to the next parameter. A parameter with nothing to pick still gets its header (over `No completions`, or after the nouns a bare verb lists), so a free-text or required word is named too.
- Narrow widths drop the `next:` hint first, then cut and drop the description, then drop the facts from the end (the type last); the name is always kept. Mono and `NO_COLOR` read the same text (accent is bold, muted is faint).
- Not shown: a default value (`Parameter` declares none) and a `--flag` spelling (the palette has no flag syntax: every parameter is a bare word filled in order, `Positional` first).
- Go: `Palette.ViewSized` is unchanged (plain text); the model passes its `Styles` to the palette body. CLI help (oden) and the record editor's chooser list choices through their own code and are unchanged.
- Stories `palette/param-{boolean,enum,noun,source,required,narrow}`; o-story pages take `verbs` (demo verbs with declared parameters, `positional`, and `rows` a verb returns so another can name it as its `source`). `examples/csv`'s completion test reads the new header.
- Consumers (mtm, o-md, nabe, the o-* tools) pick it up when they bump ochazuke; any golden that shows a palette `choices` heading changes to the parameter's header.

## v0.23.0 (2026-10-27)

Timeline cards show selection with the border, never reverse video: heavy accent border when focused, a mid border when the timeline is unfocused, dimmer borders on the rest (`Styles.CardBorders`); in mono the line styles carry the states.

### Card timelines: the selection is the border

- A timeline's selected card is no longer drawn in reverse video: its header text keeps its own style (bold for `Active`, muted for `Muted`, now also while selected), and the border carries the selection. Three states: the selected card of the focused timeline, a heavy border with heavy corners (`┏━┓`) in the accent, bold; the selected card of a timeline without the focus (mtm's prompt box focused), a heavy border with rounded corners (`╭━╮`) halfway from the accent to the `border` role; every other card, the light rounded border in the `border` role faded toward the surface by `dim-unfocused` (dimmer than before). Mono and `NO_COLOR`: bold, plain and faint, the line styles telling the three apart. The active card's `●` is unchanged.
- Go: `Styles.CardBorders() (focused, selected, other lipgloss.Style)`, from the scheme's `accent`, `border` and `surface` roles, so built-in and `themes.json` themes (and `Appearance.Accent`) work.
- Rules: AGENTS.md and `docs/UX-RULES.md` R5.1 record cards as the stated exception to reverse-video selection. Stories `timeline/border-{focused,unfocused}-{dark,mono}-80`; the other timeline goldens updated (no `R|` rows, heavy corners on the focused selection).
- Consumers: mtm's Turns tab picks it up when it bumps ochazuke; its turn-card goldens change (no reversed header rows, `┏┓┗┛` corners on the selected card).

## v0.22.0 (2026-10-27)

`BIN catalog inspect` writes a self-contained HTML inspector of an app's nouns, verbs, effective key rows, user actions and framework nouns, merging more tools with `--bin`/`--catalog` and suggestions with `--annotations`/`--audit` (package `cli/inspect`, stdlib only).

`BIN catalog inspect` writes an HTML inspector of an app's catalog, effective key rows, user actions and the framework nouns, plus other tools' catalogs (`--bin`, `--catalog`) and annotations (`--annotations`, `--audit`). Library: package `cli/inspect`.

### The catalog inspector: `catalog inspect`

- Moved from `~/hub/tools/catalog-inspector` (hub `ef53054`) into ochazuke as package `cli/inspect` (standard library only) and the `inspect` word of Mount's `catalog` command, so every app built with `cli.Mount` has it. `catalog` with no words prints the catalog JSON as before; another word than `inspect` is now an error.
- `BIN catalog inspect [--out FILE] [--open]`: the app's catalog, its effective key rows (what `config export` writes), its `actions.json` verbs and the framework nouns, kept once (newest ochazuke wins). Without `--out` it writes `<state dir>/catalog-inspect.html` (the temp dir without one) and prints the path.
- Generic multi-tool input replaces the hub tool's hard-wired mtm discovery: `--bin PATH` (runs `PATH catalog`) and `--catalog FILE.json`, repeatable; `--namespace PREFIX` for a mirror prefix whose tool is not read. The family run is one `mtm catalog inspect` line (README, "Catalog inspector").
- Generic annotations replace the hard-wired `verb-audit.md` reader: `--annotations FILE.json` (`inspect.Annotations`: per noun, suggested verbs with params, maybe, note, group; a dated source) fills the Gaps view, hidden without annotations. `--audit FILE.md` reads the family verb-audit format through `inspect.AuditAnnotations(markdown)`.
- Changes from the hub tool: the page names the host instead of mtm and starts on its first noun; tool roles are `host`, `tool`, `saved`, `framework`; an action the host's catalog already lists (installed) is marked user instead of repeated as `@user`; a mirror prefix may be followed by `.` as well as `-`. For the family run the merged nouns, verbs, key rows and audit matches are the same as the hub tool's.
- Go: `inspect.ParseCatalog`, `Collector{Add, AddBin, AddFile, SeeBin}`, `Namespaces`, `Merge`, `Write(data, w)`, `ReadAnnotations`, `Annotations.Normalize`/`Match`, `AuditAnnotations`, `RunningOchazuke`; `cli.InspectFile`.
- `cli/inspect/smoke.mjs` is the page's headless Chrome check; `OCHAZUKE_INSPECT_OUT=FILE go test ./examples/demo -run TestCatalogInspect` writes the demo's page to run it on.
- Consumers (mtm, nabe, o-md, the o-* tools, the examples) get `catalog inspect` when they bump ochazuke; nothing else changes.

## v0.21.0 (2026-10-27)

`o-story explore --watch` plays a run in a TUI (pause, step, speed, replay a finding); headless stays the default and its output is unchanged. Library: `explore.Budget.Observe`, `explore.Step`, `explore.Frames`.

### Watching the explorer: `o-story explore --watch`

- New optional hook `explore.Budget.Observe func(explore.Step)` (`json:"-"`, left out of `Report.Budget`): called on Run's goroutine, in order, for the initial state and each seed's (`PhaseStart`), every action the search plays (`PhaseSearch`), and the end of the trap search (`PhaseTraps`). A `Step` carries `Seq`, `Phase`, `Path`, `Action`, `State` (the state key), `New`, `Visited`, `Steps`, `Budget`, `Depth`, the `Findings` first recorded at that step (as found), and `Frame func() string`, the state's screen at the session's size, valid only during the call. The observer sees the walk and never steers it: the same subject and budget give the same steps and the same report with or without one (`TestObserveSeesEveryStepInOrder`); a slow observer only slows the run. A `CompareFull` second search is not observed. Unset, nothing changes.
- New `explore.Frames(s, path) ([]string, error)`: the screen before the path and after each key, for a viewer replaying a finding.
- `o-story explore … --watch [--delay D]` plays the run in a full-screen TUI built on ochazuke: the application and status, states visited, steps of the budget and left, depth, the key path, the findings as they turn up, and the explored state's frame boxed beside them. Keys: `a` `watch.pause`, `l`/`right`/`space` `watch.step` (one step), `+`/`=` `watch.faster`, `-` `watch.slower`, `q` `watch.stop` (the run finishes unwatched and the same report prints), `enter` `finding.replay` (the path frame by frame on a replay page: `l`/`h` step, `r`/`f` start/end, `a` auto, `q`/`esc` back). `--delay` defaults to 50ms (0 is full speed) and needs `--watch`. The TUI draws on `/dev/tty`, so stdout can take the report. Without `--watch` the output is byte for byte what it was.
- Consumers: `explore.Survey` and every example's `TestExplore` set no observer and run as before.

## v0.20.0 (2026-10-27)

The `focus-display` setting (off, noun, stack) shows the focused noun or the focus path beside the mode badge; `focus.display` cycles it.

### The focus beside the mode: `focus-display`

- New framework setting `focus-display` (`FocusDisplaySetting`; `off` default, `noun`, `stack`), declared by `EnsureConfigSettings` unless the application declared one; on the Settings page and in `settings.json`, applied on the next frame. Off leaves the chrome unchanged.
- `noun`: the title row reads `NORMAL · pane`, the focused instance's noun as `DisplayNoun`. `stack`: `NORMAL · panes › pane-list › pane`, the focus path outermost first (`Runtime.FocusPath`; app left out; open modals after the focus they opened over; a level's label when it is at most `FocusLabelMax` (16) cells, a page's title, else its noun). Drawn in the muted style; the text alone carries it under mono and `NO_COLOR`.
- A path wider than the room is cut from the left a level at a time behind `… › `, keeping the innermost level (`FitFocusPath`); the title keeps a quarter of the row.
- New verb `focus.display {mode}` (`ochazuke.focus.display`): sets the setting; with no mode it cycles off → noun → stack. No default key: palette (`display focus`), CLI (`BIN focus display`), or a `bindings.json` row.
- `Header.Focus` (levels, outermost first) for code that draws its own `Layout.Shell` header.
- Consumers (mtm, nabe, o-md, the o-* tools, the examples) see a new `focus-display` row on their Settings page and nothing else until a user turns it on.
- Stories `chrome/focus-noun-row-80`, `focus-noun-modal-80`, `focus-stack-row-80`, `focus-stack-modal-80`, `focus-stack-chooser-80`, `focus-stack-narrow`, `focus-stack-mono`, `focus-stack-dim`, `focus-display-cycles`.

## v0.19.0 (2026-10-27)

Detail kinds: a mounted catalog page's detail can be drawn by a host-registered kind (`cli.RegisterDetailKind`, `CatalogDetail.Kind`/`PathField`); `markdown/cliview` draws it as an editable Markdown document. Package `cli` still carries no Glamour.

### Detail kinds: a mounted page's detail can be a Markdown editor

- `cli.CatalogDetail` has `kind` (`""`/`cli.DetailRecord`, the record view as before) and `pathField` (the record field naming a file). A host registers what draws a kind: `cli.RegisterDetailKind(kind, factory)` for the process, or `PageConfig.DetailKinds` for one mount (wins); `cli.DetailKinds()` lists them. A kind the host has not registered shows the record with a line saying so, so a catalog naming a new kind never breaks an older host.
- `cli.DetailView` (`Show(row, ok)`, `Block(w, h)`, `Focused()`), `cli.DetailFactory`, `cli.DetailHost` (`Model`, `Page`, `Parent`, `Spec`, `Field(row, name)`); `PageView.DetailView()`, `PageView.DetailParent()`. The view is built once per mount under a row stand-in instance `<page>/detail/row` whose noun, `Data` and `Label` are the selected row's, so the row noun's verbs and keys answer from inside the view (the view's own rows first) and act on that row. The stand-in's component tells the view the selected row after every event when it, or its record, changed.
- New package `markdown/cliview`: the `markdown` kind (`cliview.Kind`), an editable `markdown.Doc` over `markdown.File(row[pathField])`, committed on change, a new row opening its file (the open element of the last written first), a changed record reloading it unless an element is open, a row without the field or an unreadable file saying why. `cliview.Register()`, `cliview.Factory(opts...)` (`Source`, `Render`, `Author`), `cliview.Of(pageView)`, `cliview.DocOf(view)`. Package `cli` does not import `markdown`: Glamour reaches only hosts that register the kind.
- Consumers: mtm registers `markdown` (o-md's Docs page); nabe and the o-* tools are unaffected unless they register a kind.
- o-story: a catalog page's `files` (by path), `author` and `today` feed its markdown detail; writes log as `PATH write LINE -OLD +NEW`. Stories `catalog/markdown-detail-edit`, `catalog/markdown-detail-follows`.

## v0.18.0 (2026-10-27)

The `markdown.Doc` is a vim-style inline Markdown editor: line cursor, motions by component and kind, editing in place with list and table structure, item notes (`> **author** date:` under a list item) with resolve, fold and CLI helpers. Also: explore dedupe and gap stories; surveys behind `-tags explore`.

### `markdown.Doc` is an inline editor

- The Doc's cursor is a drawn line, not a task item: `j`/`k` move by drawn line, `g g`/`G`, `ctrl+d`/`ctrl+u`, `ctrl+f`/`ctrl+b` and counts are the line list's (`doc.ListID()` is now `id/lines`; rows `markdown-line`, `markdown-task`, `markdown-note`). The cursor starts on the first line. A drawn document is a focus stop even without checklist items (an empty read-only one is not). Consumers that relied on the Doc standing aside without tasks (mtm notes) see it as a stop.
- New motions (verbs, all in `?` help and the palette): `}`/`{` components, `] ]`/`[ [` same kind, `]`/`[` + `h t x l c q n p` by kind, `g O` heading outline.
- Editing in place: `i a I A`, `o`/`O` continuing lists (renumbered), tables and notes, `d d`, `y y`, `p`/`P`, `u`/`ctrl+r` for every edit; insert-mode `enter`, `tab`/`shift+tab`, `backspace` on an empty marker and `/` (block chooser) as guarded rows on `markdown-edit`. Commit on change: typing debounced, every other edit at once, bytes outside the edited element unchanged, the source reloaded and the target found again before every write.
- Notes: `> **author** date:` blockquotes under a list item, drawn as boxes; `n`, `x` (resolve), `z a` (fold), sent and resolved markers, and helpers for CLIs (`Notes`, `AddNote`, `ReplyNote`, `SetFlags`, `FindItem`, `CleanNote`). Setting `markdown.note-author`.
- `ErrRefused` keeps its meaning; refusals now carry their own message (`not written: …` for edits, `not toggled: …` for toggles). New: `Elements`, `Element`, `Doc.Block`, `Doc.SetSource`, `Doc.Leave`, `WriteFile`.
- o-story: the markdown spec takes `author` and `today`; writes other than toggles log as `write LINE -OLD +NEW`; the debounce tick covers the Doc.

## v0.17.0 (2026-10-27)

The framework namespace is `ochazuke` (was `app` in v0.16.0, which collided with the `app` noun's own verbs); `app.<framework noun>` stays a silent alias for one release. `cli.ExtensionNamespace` strips `o-` (o-ai → `ai`). The catalog lists each noun once. New card timeline component (`AddTimeline`).

### Framework namespace `ochazuke`

The framework namespace is `ochazuke`, not `app`: v0.16.0's `app.<noun>` framework names collided with the app noun's own verbs (`app.state`, an application verb, against the framework noun `app.state`).

- Framework nouns are canonical `ochazuke.<noun>` (`ochazuke.settings`, `ochazuke.list`, `ochazuke.page`, `ochazuke.state`, `ochazuke.command`, `ochazuke.grid`, `ochazuke.markdown`, …); `FrameworkNamespace` is `"ochazuke"`, and the new `AppNoun` (`"app"`) is never namespaced. Short names are unchanged aliases. The CLI nests them under `ochazuke` (`bin ochazuke settings list`, `bin ochazuke.settings list`, `bin settings list`). Exported noun constants changed value again (`cli.CommandNoun` `ochazuke.command`, `cli.GroupNoun`, `CompletionNoun`, `TreeRowNoun`, `Form*Noun`, `markdown.DocNoun`, `markdown.TaskNoun`). Code comparing with `app.<framework noun>` must compare with `ochazuke.<noun>`.
- An application verb on `app` named like a framework noun (`app.state`, `app.settings`) is accepted and stands beside it: `app.state` is the application's verb, `state.get` / `ochazuke.state.get` the framework's.
- Compatibility: the v0.16.0 spelling `app.<framework noun>` (keys, `Do`, `Request`, `bindings.json`, `actions.json`, restored `history.jsonl`, the palette, `bin app settings list`, `bin app.settings list`) is a deprecated alias of `ochazuke.<noun>` for one release, unless a noun of that exact name exists or `app` has a verb of that name; history reads it back canonical (`ochazuke.`).
- `cli.ExtensionNamespace(bin)`: the namespace every host mounts an extension under (`cli.Namespace`, `cli.Local`): the base name of the binary, lower-cased, a leading `o-` dropped (`o-ai` is `ai`, `o-qbt` is `qbt`, `o-git` is `git`, `o-csv` is `csv`; `mtm-mermaid` stays `mtm-mermaid`); an error when what is left is not one noun word or is `ochazuke`, `app` or the short name of a framework noun.
- Fix: `cli.Catalog` listed a noun more than once when its verbs sorted around the nouns nested under it (`app` three times in o-qbt's catalog); it now groups by noun, nouns by name and each noun's verbs by name.

### Card timelines

- New component `Timeline` (`timeline.go`): `Runtime.AddTimeline(id, cards, opts...)` draws a scrolling column of boxed cards of varying height (`Card`: header wrapped in full, a meta line, a body and foldable `CardSection`s shown only while open, each section in its own `CardRole` style). Cards are instances of the app's nouns (rows of a list child), so selection, j/k with counts and spills, `g g`, ctrl+d/ctrl+u and the `/` filter are the list's. Options `FollowTail()` (stick to the end: appends select the newest card, a growing last card keeps its end in view; moving or scrolling up stops it, `G` or reaching the end resumes), `HeaderLines(n)` (cap a closed header with `… N more lines`), `NoSpill()`.
- Emphasis: `Active` (bold header, `●` in the top border, readable in mono), `Muted` (older cards), `Group` (a labelled full-width rule between groups). Selection: the selected card's header reversed and its border heavy in the accent.
- Keys (rows on `ochazuke.timeline`): `enter`/`space` toggle, `l`/`h` open/close, `z o`/`z c`/`z a`, `z R`/`z M` all, `z 1`–`z 9` fold a section, `J`/`K`/`ctrl+e`/`ctrl+y` scroll line-wise, `G` the end.
- Data in: `SetCards` keeps selection, open cards and folds by id; `Patch(cards...)` updates or appends cheaply for streaming. Bodies render lazily (open and on screen), cached by id, width, `Revision` and folds; a frame costs the same at 500 and 5000 cards (`BenchmarkTimeline`).
- o-story: a page's `timeline` field (`TimelineSpec`, `CardSpec`, `SectionSpec`), stories `timeline/wrap`, `open-close`, `follow-tail`, `active-mono` at 80 and 120 columns, `timeline/open-close`, `follow-tail`, `spill-to-list`.

## v0.16.0 (2026-10-27)

Noun namespaces: framework nouns are `app.<noun>` with their short names as aliases, `DefineOwn` lets an app claim a short name, dotted nouns split on the last dot (`SplitKey`), nested and dotted CLI spellings, `cli.Namespace` for extension hosts. The state explorer: `ochazuke/explore` (`explore.Run`, `Survey`, `Reproduce`), `o-story explore`, `make explore`.

Noun namespaces: a noun may be dotted, every framework noun is `app.<noun>` with its short name as an alias, and an application declares a noun of its own under a framework name with `DefineOwn` instead of deleting the framework's verbs.

### Noun namespaces

- Dotted nouns (`app.settings`, `ai.skills`); a verb is one word (`Register` refuses a dotted verb). Every key splits on its last dot: `ochazuke.SplitKey`, `CutKey`; `Do`, `When`, `VerbGuard`, the cli page/widget sources and state, the story builder and `cmd/ochazuke` help use it. A verb `y` on noun `x` beside a noun `x.y` (either order) is refused with an error naming both.
- Framework nouns are canonical `app.<noun>` (`app.list`, `app.settings`, `app.page`, `app.command`, `app.grid`, `app.markdown`, …; `app` stays `app`): what `Runtime.Builtin` registers is namespaced and its short name recorded as an alias (`Runtime.Alias`, `Aliases`, `AliasOf`). Resolution (`CanonicalNoun`, `CanonicalKey`, `Operation(key)`, `Noun(name)`): a noun of that exact name wins, else the alias. `Execute`/`ExecuteAt`, binding rows (`Do`, `Bind`, `LoadBindingSpec`, `bindings.json`), `actions.json` (an action keeps the file's spelling for `{{noun}}` and its default `call`; `call` takes the noun's words then the verb), `Kind`, `ListItem.Noun`, `OpenModal` kinds, `Parameter.Noun`/`Source`, `Rows`, `RegisterNoun`, `NounPlaces`, `NounHelp`/`PlaceHelp`, `Launch` and restored `history.jsonl` lines all accept short names.
- Canonical output: history entries, `bindings.list`, `operations.list`, the snapshot and the catalog print canonical keys; the footer hints, `?` help (operations, sections, the pick/table header and place labels, the noun chooser), the jobs ticker, CLI job lines, confirm `{{noun}}`, the "is not applicable now" error and o-story's recorded `job` events and verb tables draw the short name (`DisplayNoun`, `DisplayKey`) while it reaches the noun.
- `Define(name)` of an alias extends the framework's noun (`Define("terminal").Verb("copy", …)` adds `app.terminal.copy`; `Register` and `Instance`/`Kind` read the alias the same way). `Runtime.DefineOwn(name)` declares the application's own noun under exactly that name: the short name reaches it from then on and the framework's stays reachable as `app.<name>`, verbs intact.
- CLI: `cli.Mount` nests dotted nouns as oden groups (`bin app settings list`), adds a top-level group per unshadowed alias (`bin settings list`), and reads a dotted first word as its groups (`bin app.settings list`); `cli.Shell` takes the longest run of leading words naming a noun (`Runtime.NounWords`), then the verb (prefixes within that noun). The catalog publishes the active aliases (`CatalogDoc.Aliases`, `CatalogDoc.CanonicalNoun`).
- Palette: a dotted noun is one word; each noun is completed by its short name and its canonical spelling is accepted as typed.
- Hosts: `cli.Namespace(ext)` (a `PageConfig.Noun`: catalog noun `skills` is the host's `ext.skills`) and `cli.Local(ext, noun)` replace hand-built `ext-noun` prefixes.
- Compatibility: `Runtime.Operations` and `Runtime.Nouns` are keyed by canonical names, so code indexing them with a framework short name (`r.Operations["terminal.hint"]`, `r.Nouns["list"]`) must use `CanonicalKey`/`Operation`/`Noun`; comparisons of `Instance.Noun` with a framework short name (`inst.Noun == "list"`) must compare with `app.list`. Exported noun constants changed value (`cli.CommandNoun`, `cli.GroupNoun`, `CompletionNoun`, `TreeRowNoun`, `Form*Noun`, `markdown.DocNoun`, `markdown.TaskNoun`).

### Explorer triage

- `explore.Dedupe(reports, weight)` groups findings across reports by check and `NormalizeSummary` (ids, numbers, the `app.` namespace stripped), best occurrence first (shortest path, lightest subject, name); `o-story explore --dedupe DIR [--reproduce] [--out DIR]` prints the groups, replays each on the current build and writes `groups.json` plus one gap story per group.
- `explore.FrameworkRefusal`, a `Subject.Expected` for the framework's own refusals (nothing to undo/redo/repeat, no palette history, a palette line or completion naming nothing, a chooser with no match, an empty or misnamed register, single-line inputs, an empty list item, page motions with no pages), matched by operation and message (the runtime has no sentinel errors for them); the four examples and o-story's story subjects use it, the CSV example adds its sheet refusals and o-story the stories' fakes.
- `explore.Shows` and `explore.Gap` (a known gap as a skipped Go test); 16 gap stories in `cmd/o-story/stories/gaps/` for the confirmed bugs of the 2026-10-27 survey, and `TestExploreGaps` in `examples/core`, `demo` and `full-project` for the ones only those apps show.
- Explorer surveys left `go test ./...`: every survey-running test (the examples' `explore_test.go`, the explore package's `TestRunFindsEachCheck` and `TestRunIsDeterministic`, o-story's `TestExploreWritesGapStories`) carries the `explore` build tag; `TestRunFindsTheShallowBugs` keeps a fast untagged check. `make explore-quick` runs them at the small budget, `make explore` adds the deep run.
- `o-story explore` picks a non-skipped story to stand for an application, so a gap story copied from a story keeps that story's subject name.

## v0.15.0 (2026-10-27)

`config.reload` reads every config file again (with `OnConfigReload` for an app's own); noun places record where each noun shows up (`nouns places`); the `?` help gains pick and table views (`S`, `n`, `m`, `p`) that resolve keys at a noun's recorded place, defaulting to where `?` was opened; `ctrl+\` opens the help from any insert mode; the bindings export is ordered mode, noun, verb, keys.

### State explorer

- New package `explore`: `Run(Subject{Name, New, Close, Seeds, Checks, Size, Sizes, Fork, Serial, Expected}, Budget{Depth, Steps, Key, CompareFull, ...}) Report` presses every key that answers (plus esc, enter, tab, shift+tab, backspace, ctrl+c, a wait and resizes) breadth first by replay, keys states by the interaction state (`Abstract`; `Full` adds the 80x24 screen), and checks in every state: panics, the focus invariants, render fits at each size, honest footer hints, no traps (a way home), jobs settle, operation errors (grouped by operation), commands settle, plus a static check for modes no row leaves and the subject's own `Checks`. Findings carry the shortest key path, shrunk by delta debugging; `Report.Text`/`JSON`, `Finding.GoTest`, `Reproduce`. Commands run inline until they finish or park; timers are never slept on: a wait delivers the framework's through their tick accessors on a fake clock. Deterministic: same subject and budget, same report.
- `explore.Survey(t, subject)` for tests (depth 3 / 2000 steps; `OCHAZUKE_EXPLORE=deep` for depth 6 / 20000; fails only on the explorer's own errors and nondeterminism); `TestExplore` in `examples/demo`, `core`, `csv` and `full-project`; `make explore`; `o-story explore [NAME|AREA...] [--depth] [--steps] [--out DIR] [--compare-full]` over every distinct story-built application, writing each finding as a skipped gap story.
- Read-only hooks for it: `Runtime.FocusInTopLevel`, `GuardNames`, `GuardHolds`, `PendingModes`, `IsPendingMode`, `IsPassthrough`, `ModeKnown`, `ModalReturn`, `HintBindings(r, focus)` (the rows `BindingHints` shows), and timers a host with a fake clock delivers at once: `Runtime.BindingTimeoutTick` (the message is due), `Tree.DebounceTick`, `RecordEditor.DebounceTick`.
- `examples/core` builds its tree in `newRuntime()`.

### Noun places

- Where a noun shows up, from observation: every instance added is recorded as a `NounPlace{Noun, Ancestors, Page, PageTitle, Modal, Count, First, Last}` (ancestor nouns outermost first, `app` left out; inside an open modal they start at the modal instance and `Page` is the page beneath; never instance ids), deduplicated and counted; list rows once per `SetItems` per noun, not per row. Settled after each Model update (so a page registered after its children still places them), capped at `NounPlaceLimit` (2000, least recently seen dropped). `Runtime.NounPlaces(noun)` (most seen first; `NounPlace.Chain()` is the walk outermost to innermost), verbs `nouns.places {noun}`, `nouns.list`, `nouns.places-path` (builtin fetches). Persisted per app in `noun-places.json` in the state directory (`EnsureNounPlaces` from `Model.Init`; written off the loop at most every 2s and on `Config.Flush`, temp + rename; a file that does not parse is one notice and is replaced; none without a state directory or in a test binary).

### Configuration

- The bindings export reads in a fixed order: a row's JSON fields are `mode`, `noun`/`instance`, `request` (`verb` before `noun`, then `args`), `keys`/`pattern`, then the rest (`from`, `when`, `capture`, `hint`, ...), in `bindings.list` (both forms), `bindings.stops`, the catalog's `bindings` and `config export`'s `bindings.reference.json`; and `bindings.list --all` and the reference sort their rows by mode, then noun (a noun's rows before an instance's of that name), then verb (a row without a request last), then keys (pattern rows after), stable (`SortBindings`). Output only: the accepted `bindings.json` shape is unchanged, and the reference loads back as `bindings.json` leaving every row as it was. The focus-scoped `bindings.list` keeps its order.
- `config.reload` (`Runtime.ReloadConfig() ([]string, error)`): one verb reads every config file again, in dependency order: `themes.json` (so the `theme` setting's choices are current), `settings.json` (observers fire; the Model applies the theme), `actions.json`, `bindings.json` (applied once; a row can name an action's verb), each only when the app uses it, then the app's own files. A bad file or entry is skipped and is a notice; each file's previous notices are withdrawn first. The result is every problem (`problem[]`), each naming its file; an error without a config directory. A fetch with no default key; in the palette, `operations.list`, the catalog (builtin) and the CLI (`BIN config reload`).
- `Runtime.OnConfigReload(name, fn func() []string)`: an application joins `config.reload` with its own files (run after the framework's, in registration order); problems get the `name: ` prefix unless they carry it, are notices and are withdrawn at the next reload.

### Help

- `?` help has three views, `S` (`bindings-help.view`) cycling them: **here** (unchanged: what the focus answers now), **pick** (the same table for a chosen noun and mode, as if the focus were there: `n` `bindings-help.noun` and `m` `bindings-help.mode` open choosers of every noun and of the modes; the header reads `noun: X · mode: Y`) and **table** (a noun's keys by mode: a row per key or key class, a column per mode whose own table has rows for the noun or app, each cell the operation that answers, `.verb` for the noun's own, `(op)` when app answers, `op?` when guarded; `z l`/`z h`/`$` scroll the modes with the key column kept; the selected key's rows per mode, arguments, guard and answering level under the table). The title names the view (`Keys · pick`); the rows are hinted (`S view`, `n noun`, `m mode`) and guarded to the views they act in. `NounHelp(r, noun, mode)`, `Runtime.HelpTable(noun, pending)`, `Runtime.HelpModes(noun, pending)`.
- The hypothetical walk shares the live one's code: `walkOf` (the capture pass) and `scanRows` (first row per key, mode table before any-mode table, exclusive pending modes, prefixes) serve both `levelRows` and `nounRows`; a guarded row is kept with its guard (`HelpRow.Guard`), and the rows answering when it is false follow it. Stories `help/pick-view`, `help/table-view-120`, `help/table-view-80`, `help/settings-*`.
- Pick and table resolve at a place: `p` (`bindings-help.place`, hinted `p place`, only in pick and table and when the noun has `NounPlaces`) opens a chooser of `no place (noun + app)` then the noun's recorded places, most seen first, labelled `PageTitle › ancestors… · modal X` with the count trailing; the default is the place of the focus `?` opened over while the noun is that focus's noun (computed as the recorder does, `placementOf`), else the noun's most seen place (else none), and changing the noun resets it. The walk is the place's chain innermost first, as the live stack orders it: the noun, each ancestor noun (list, panel, page), the `modal` noun inside a modal, app, the editor fallback, so a list row's keys from its list and page are listed and pick at a row's place answers as here does for that row. Header `noun: X · mode: Y · place: Context › page › list` (or `place: none`). `PlaceHelp(r, place, mode)`; `Runtime.HelpTable` and `Runtime.HelpModes` take a `NounPlace` (`NounPlace{Noun: noun}` is no place). The help modal's own instances are no longer recorded as places. Story `help/pick-place`.
- `ctrl+\` (`HelpInsertKey`) opens `?` help from insert mode, where `?` types itself: a hidden `NewModel` default row on the `editor` noun in insert mode, so it answers in every input (page inputs, modal prompts, the palette, chooser search, form fields, the outline line, the settings prompt). No Readline default or framework row binds it, and in raw mode it sends no SIGQUIT. Story `help/insert-key`.
- Settings (section help, declared by `EnsureConfigSettings`): `help.pick-view` and `help.table-view` (booleans, default true: the view is in `S`'s cycle; with both off `S` is not offered) and `help.pending-modes` (default false: pending modes and key-sequence prefixes as pick's modes, pending modes as table columns).

## v0.14.0 (2026-10-27)

Modals take about 80% of the width with no 78-column cap and choosers fill it; terminal overlays let an app label a live pane (hints) with the new `upper` and `ctrl+alpha` key classes and the `letter` source; a parameter's `Noun` lists that noun's things as its choices.

### Modals

- A modal's width is about 80% of the terminal columns with no 78-cell cap (`ModalSize`, `ComposeModal`, `ComposeModalBlock` unchanged in signature); content-sized modals only widen when their content is longer. A chooser fills the modal's width: its search line and rows are padded to it, so the panel does not resize while typing, and column fitting and the preview split use it. Story `chooser/wide-fills`.

### Terminal

- `Terminal.SetOverlay(*TerminalOverlay)` / `Overlay()`: an application draws over a terminal's live frame without touching it. `TerminalOverlay{Dim, Marks}` fades the pane (the `modal-dim` backdrop style; faint in mono and NO_COLOR) and draws each `OverlayMark{Row, Col, Text, Style}` over the cells from its pane cell, cut at the edge, wide-rune and style aware (the empty style draws reverse and bold). A new frame keeps the overlay; nil clears it; `Frame()` and the stream never see it.
- Hints over a live pane need no new mode machinery: an application base mode made passthrough (`DefineMode(name, false)`, `DefinePassthrough`) entered by a verb a `leader_pending` row runs (`SetMode`), whose rows on the terminal noun take every key, so nothing reaches the pane; leaving is `SetMode("terminal")`. Recipe in AGENTS.md's terminal row; stories `terminal/hints-*`.

### Keys

- Key classes `upper` (`Upper`: one uppercase letter, answers before `alpha`) and `ctrl+alpha` (`CtrlAlpha`: ctrl+a to ctrl+z, exclusions spelled in full, `ctrl+alpha-ctrl+h-ctrl+m`), and the argument source `letter` (`SourceLetter`: the key's letter lowercased without ctrl+/shift+, so `f`, `F` and `ctrl+f` all read `f`). Legacy terminals send ctrl+h/i/j/m as backspace/tab/enter and ctrl+[ as esc: labels read through `ctrl+alpha` should avoid h, i, j and m.

### Palette

- `Parameter.Noun` (`noun` in the catalog): the value names one of the noun's things, so the palette and CLI help list the rows of `NOUN.list` (a read with no required parameter; `Runtime.ChoicesSource`, published as the catalog's `source`) or else the noun's live instances, list rows included (every row, in list order). `ParseChoices` reads a map row's id from `id`, `key` or `name` and its label from `label` or `title`, and `[]Operation` as `noun.verb`. `page.open`, `mode.set`, `mode.enter`, `confirm.set`, `list.focus`, `input.focus` and `modal.push` name their nouns. Story `palette/noun-choices`.

## v0.13.0 (2026-10-26)

Table cell mode is opt-in: CellSelect(); every other table selects rows as before v0.12.0.

Table cell mode is opt-in: only a table added with `CellSelect()` is cell-based.

### Tables

- `CellSelect()` (an `InstanceOption` for `AddList`) makes a table cell-based: the current column, `h`/`l`, `0`/`^`/`home`, `$`/`end`, `N|`, `g c`, `y` (copy the cell) and `z >`/`z <`/`z =`, with the column and widths remembered in `tables.json`, as `v0.12.0` gave every table. `AddTree` ignores it.
- Every other table selects rows again, as on `v0.11.0`: `h`/`l` are free for row nouns, `$`, `^` and `0` (without a count) run `list.scroll-columns` (verb guard `table-scrolls`), `z l`/`z h` scroll a column, no current cell is drawn, and nothing is read from or written to `tables.json` for it (widths a `v0.12.0` cell table saved no longer stick to it). Story `tables/row-select`.
- `RowSelect()` is a deprecated no-op; the framework's trees, `?` help modal and Settings page no longer pass it.
- `Y` (copy the row's cells joined by tabs) answers on every table (verb guard `list-table`), `y` only on a cell-mode table.
- `V` answers on every table with marks: a visual range over the marks, cell-mode or not. `esc` ends it through `list.end-visual` (verb and row guard `list-visual`), so the row no longer hides an outer `esc` hint, such as a modal's `esc modal.pop`, over a table.
- The width keys `z >`/`z <`/`z =` need a current column, so they answer on cell-mode tables only; `List.SetColumnWidth` still sets a width on any table.
- `list.column`, `list.choose-column` and `list.resize-column` refuse a row-select table ("not a cell table"); `list.yank {what: cell}` too.
- cli: catalog tables select rows, so the groups rail's `h` back row (guard `list-first-column`) answers at once on them. Story `catalog/groups-table-h`.

### o-story

- A story list takes `cellSelect` (`ListSpec.CellSelect`); the cell stories set it, and the goldens of tables that select rows read `R|` again.

## v0.12.0 (2026-10-26)

Tables are cell-based: a current column, per-column widths, and V for rows.

Tables are cell-based, like o-csv's grid: a current column, column widths, and a visual row selection.

### Tables

- A list with columns has a current column (`List.Column`, `SetColumn`; `column` in its state). On the `list` noun in normal mode, on a cell-mode table only: `h`/`l`/`left`/`right` move by column (counts), `0`/`^`/`home` and `$`/`end` go to the first and last, `N|` to column N, `g c` chooses one from a chooser of the titles (`list.column`, `list.choose-column`, motions); `j`/`k` keep the column. Every frame scrolls the column offset as little as shows the current column; `z l`/`z h` carry it along. `$`, `^` and `0` no longer run `list.scroll-columns`; `list.column` scrolls a row-select table the same way. Stories `tables/cell-motion`, `tables/column-count`.
- The current cell is drawn reverse and underlined inside the row's highlight, the header underlines the current column's title, and an unfocused table fades both. Story `tables/unfocused-cell-dimmed`.
- `y` and `Y` copy the current cell, or the row's cells joined by tabs, into the registers (`list.yank {what, register}`).
- `z >`/`z <` widen or narrow the current column by `ColumnWidthStep` (2) cells, never below 3; `z =` makes every column automatic (`list.resize-column`, `list.reset-columns`; `List.SetColumnWidth`, `ColumnWidths`, `ResetColumnWidths`). The widths and the current column are remembered per table id in the state directory's `tables.json` (`Runtime.TableState`, `SetTableState`). Story `tables/column-resize`.
- `V` starts a visual row selection drawn into the marks: `j`/`k` extend it (they clamp), a row's application verb runs for every selected row through `list.apply`, `esc` or `V` ends it and the marks go back (`list.visual`, `List.Visual`, `StartVisual`, `EndVisual`). Story `tables/visual-apply`.
- `list.apply` takes `args` for every row's request, and a verb with a confirm asks once for all the rows.
- `RowSelect()` keeps a table on row selection: trees' lists (h/l expand and collapse), the `?` help modal and the Settings page use it.
- y, Y, V and the width keys are hidden from the footer; `?` help lists them.
- cli: the groups rail's `h`/`left` back rows take the new guard `list-first-column`, so in a table `h` moves left and goes back to the rail from the first column. Story `catalog/groups-table-h`.

### grid

- Per-column widths: `z >`/`z <`/`z =` on the grid noun (`grid.resize-column`, `grid.reset-columns`; `Grid.SetColumnWidth`, `ColumnWidth`, `ColumnWidths`, `ResetColumnWidths`). A given width replaces `MaxCellWidth` for its column and is remembered under the grid's id.

### o-story

- Goldens read `C|` (`c|` dimmed) for a highlighted row that holds a table's current cell; the expect field `cell` names the current cell's text.

## v0.11.0 (2026-10-26)

The terminal leader can wait for the next key indefinitely.

- `terminal-leader-timeout` (`TerminalLeaderTimeoutSetting`, duration): how long `leader_pending` waits for the next key before it sends the leader alone. `0` never expires, so the leader waits like tmux's prefix; a positive value is the leader's own timeout. `EnsureTerminalLeader` declares it beside `terminal-leader` unless the app declared it first (with its own default); the framework default is `DefaultBindingTimeout`, and until settings.json holds a value the leader follows `BindingTimeout`. Other key sequences keep `BindingTimeout`. Story `terminal/leader-timeout-zero-waits`.

## v0.10.0 (2026-10-26)

An unfocused list keeps its selected row, faded; the dims are settings. A list can clear its filter when enter picks a row.

### Unfocused lists and dims

- A page list without the focus keeps its selected row in reverse video and is drawn faded as a whole, so the row an unfocused list is on (a tree beside a live terminal) stays visible and the focused row is the one highlight at full strength. A list in a modal stays plain until focused; a tree editing a line is not faded. Replaces the old rule "an unfocused list draws every row plain" (UX rule R5.1). Stories `lists/unfocused-dimmed`, `lists/beside-terminal-keeps-row`.
- Two framework settings, declared by `EnsureConfigSettings`: `dim-unfocused` (percent an unfocused list fades toward the background, default 20) and `modal-dim` (the page behind a modal, default 35, the backdrop's look until now). Both 0 to 90 (`MaxDim`); 0 is off.
- `Styles.Fade(percent)` blends the scheme's text colour toward its surface (`lipgloss.Blend1D`, perceptual); in mono or NO_COLOR any fade is the faint attribute. `Styles.WithDims(modal, unfocused)` sets the new `Styles.Backdrop` and `Styles.Unfocused`; `Model.Styles()` applies the settings. `NewStyles` uses the defaults.
- o-story: `highlighted` counts full-strength highlights only; the new `dimmed` counts highlights under faint; goldens mark a dimmed-only row `r|`.

### Lists

- `ClearFilterOnEnter()` (an `InstanceOption` for `AddList` and `AddTree`): enter in the list's filter line chooses the selected match (`list.filter-choose`, guard `list-chooses`): the filter clears, the selection stays on that row and on screen, the mode is normal. With no match enter keeps the filter, as on every list; other lists are unchanged. Story `lists/filter-enter-clears`.

### Fixes

- Custom components' explicit `Block.Cursor` now reaches `Model.View().Cursor` without a focused input, using the normal block or visual underline shape. Blocks without a cursor remain hidden; input and terminal cursor shapes are unchanged.

## v0.9.0 (2026-10-26)

### List motions

- Every list answers Vim's paging motions in normal mode: `g g` and `G` go to the first and last row, and with a count to that row (`5G`, `5gg`, `list.goto {position, row}`); `ctrl+d`/`ctrl+u` move half a page (a count is rows) and `ctrl+f`/`ctrl+b` a page (a count is pages) (`list.scroll {delta, half, steps}`). A page is the list's own window, else the page body's height; the window moves with the selection. They clamp at the list's edges, never spill, and are motions (off the footer, under `movement` in `?`). A row noun that binds the same key keeps it. Story `lists/page-motions`.
- A table with more columns than fit scrolls them (`list.scroll-columns {to, delta, steps}`, `List.ColumnOffset`): `$` brings the last column into view, `^` (and `0` without a count) goes back, `z l`/`z h` move one column.
